# Sightline — ROADMAP

> **Single source of truth for all remaining work.**
> **This file MUST be updated by every contribution** (code, tests, docs, config, fixtures, scripts), by humans and agents alike, **in the same commit as the work**. No exceptions except merge commits. Procedure: §3. Enforced by `npm run roadmap:check` (item F-11).
>
> **Deadline:** 2026-11-14 23:59 UTC (public repo + X demo post + submission via `POST https://nearhackathon.com/api/applications`, or the same fields at `https://nearhackathon.com/#apply`). **Internal submit target:** 2026-11-14 12:00 UTC. **Feature freeze:** 2026-11-13 00:00 UTC. Rules live in `HACKATHON.md` at repo root.
> **Reading guide for agents:** read §1–§3 and §8, then only the phase containing your item. Do not load the whole file.

CI's last observed run is green on the previous push and F-12 reports this one's. **What changed today is that the service stopped being a hypothesis.** The key arrived (**B-05 closed**), and with it: a TEE-hosted vision model that answers an image request in 1.83 s and whose `provider_tee` signature verifies through our own `checkSignature` — **`z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, fallback `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B`** (**B-04 closed**, and the §9 "no usable vision → paste-text core" trigger did not fire); the first real attestation report, which `attestationReportSchema` accepted unchanged and `bindReport` passed **4/4** with our own nonce echoed; a live CORS preflight that agrees with the source reading, so **Mode A is not blocked and T-13 is not cut**; one direct-completions host, belonging to the chosen model and to no other (S-07). Three readings this repo had written as fact were wrong: `attestationSupported` does not imply a NEAR-side enclave (the allowlist now checks the provider host, which is F-04's decision code plus 17 tests), `isReady: true` does not imply a model answers, and a **streamed** response is signed by the gateway rather than by the model enclave — hence **ADR-22: the app posts non-streamed**, because rule 5 forbids calling a gateway signature "verified by the model". **What is still not real, stated precisely because it is the whole remaining risk:** **no live mode has ever sent a document, and from today that is no longer our own silence.** `T-03`'s `/api/verify` **answers** (**ADR-33**): it re-runs the twelve dependency-free checks on the bytes it is handed and replies with one named `server_verification` whose own receipt line says it is "a second opinion on the same arithmetic, not new proof about the machine", so a good live report now composes to **`partial`** with **13** named checks passing — the quote's eight of them decided in the browser with no new dependency (T-16, ADR-32). Four heavy answers stay held back **by name** (`quote_signed_by_hardware`, `platform_still_trusted`, `gpu_evidence_verdict`, `report_expiry`) and they are **B-12** (`dcap-qvl-node` for Intel's signature chain and collateral, plus permission for a *server* route to call NRAS), not a key; `partial` is that route's ceiling and there is no code path through it that says `verified`. **What stops a person's own document is therefore no longer the transport either: the relay forwards (F-05 built, A-09 closed, SPIKE §10 has it answering the live service) — what is left is this app's own wiring, `src/app/_flow/refused_steps.ts`, which still refuses a live run by name, and T-13/B-10, the screen that still has no key field.** The strong privacy rung still needs browser E2EE (**B-02**, **B-07**); Phase C is at 6 of 13 — **C-01 is built and proved in a browser** (a GPS-tagged synthetic JPEG comes back with no EXIF, no GPS and its rotation baked into the pixels), **C-03 is built and read back by the live service from the spike** (one page, one request whose body the app itself builds, `transcribeSchema` accepting the reply and its `provider_tee` signature verifying over the exact bytes — while the *app's* live modes still cannot send, because of the gate below), **C-11 joins five photographed pages into one transcript whose per-page offsets are proved by cutting the joined text rather than by arithmetic**, with **ADR-27** deciding that a page which yielded no text leaves a *marked hole in place* instead of a silent drop or a halted run, and **C-05** now reads the page numbers those pages printed and warns where they disagree with each other or with the number of photographs handed over (**ADR-28**: compared against each other and against `pageOffsets.length`, **never** against capture position, and **silence is the designed output** — the second live five-page run is the proof: `0 finding(s)` on a correctly photographed set, and `0 finding(s)` again with a hole in it, where four labels against five photographs would otherwise tell the person to go looking for a page the service could not read); and **C-12** puts the paste path's own limit next to it — 20,000 characters, named in `MAX_PASTE_CHARS`, stated in the screen's hint and enforced **before a request exists**, with a sentence that offers the smaller piece instead of a number (**ADR-29**); and **C-02 is built in both halves** — `features/capture/read_pages.ts` hands the prepared photographs to **C-03** one at a time, in order, `app/_flow/usePhotoRun.ts` is the caller **C-11**'s join and **C-05**'s label check were written waiting for, and `features/capture/{CaptureView,PageList,useCapture}` is the screen that presses the button, mounted on `/` beside the paste box (**B-13** closes as "no new route"): a chosen file is prepared before it joins the session so the preview is the bytes that will be sent, a sixth page is refused **before its bytes are decoded**, one batch gets **one** announcement because the announcer holds one message, and `Page.previewUrl` is the first object URL this app has made — which is what **P-10**'s "revokes any image preview this tab created" had been waiting for, and it is now proved in a browser (19 e2e, four of them mutation-checked; 13 unit tests on the loop). A photographed document now **runs to the end in Demo mode**: **S-11** recorded three photographed pages, the reading of them and two answers from the live service — every reply's `provider_tee` signature verified against the attested key before a file was written — and **C-13** wired Demo mode to recognise a photograph by the SHA-256 of the **file as chosen** (**ADR-31**) and finish the run on those six replies. `tests/e2e/photo-fixtures.spec.ts` (7 tests, Chromium, no key) proves the flow and its two refusals: a file the demo does not ship, and one changed byte at the end of a file it does. **That is the flow, not the facts** — the 86 recorded facts are grounded in the model's own transcript, which is rule 8's consistency and never page-correctness, and **S-03** owns accuracy. **No live mode has sent a person's own photograph yet, and the reason moved twice today: the gate now says `partial` for a good live report, and the relay now forwards — what still refuses is the app's own door (`_flow` has no live gate/verifier wired in yet) and the absent key field (T-13, B-10), plus one human yes before a paid run.** What is left of the path is **C-02**'s screen-reader pass (**A-03**/**A-04**, the reason it stays `[~]`), **S-11**'s remaining failure shapes with **C-04/C-06**'s bad photographs, and **C-07**'s device; the cost *per document* is measured — **S-09 closed** and **C-11 re-measured it on five real pages, twice**: a page transcribes for **$0.00087275** in **25.8 s** averaged over **ten measured pages** (two five-page runs in series: **$0.00406400 / 141.6 s** and **$0.00466350 / 116.7 s**), and the structure step over those five pages is **$0.00444221 / 118.6 s** on the plan row that answers it, so **a five-page photo document is ≈$0.0085–$0.0091 and ≈235–260 s of model time** — a **range across two measured runs** rather than the single number this section carried until today (**≈$0.0097 / ≈302 s** if the page model answered step 2 — with the fenced ask billed, not just the repair), a pasted one **$0.0064 and ≈107 s** (SPIKE §4) — and the same run found a defect in this repo's own prompt (**P-01** asked for a `kind` its schema rejects; corrected, with the two vocabularies now held against each other in a test) plus two harder things, **both now decided in writing**: **`z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, the model this project chose for pages, answered the structure step in a readable shape 0 of 6 times, while `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B` answered 4 of 4 with every fact quoting the transcript back**, so "the chosen model" is per-request rather than per-deployment — which is **ADR-24** and `STEP_PLANS` (**P-14**, closed: a model and a `max_tokens` per step, the text steps at **16,384** because a measured Qwen reply reached **98.2% of 8,192** on a *one-page* transcript and the first number this project wrote was one sample from truncating good answers); and the re-run then showed the app throwing away **six finished answers out of six** because a deadline-less action came back as `"deadline": null` and the schema accepted only the key being missing — **ADR-25** / **P-15**, closed: `null` reads as absent in all four optional string fields this service is asked to fill; what a page *that runs out of budget* means is now C-11's decision rather than a knob (**P-13**, closed 2026-11-04, **ADR-23**'s fixed 8,192 being what turns a truncation into a signal); no screen reader has run this app and the "no real user by 2026-11-05" trigger is **tomorrow** (A-03/A-04/**B-01**/**U-01**); and the key that crossed into a conversation log is owed a rotation before submission (**B-11**). **What changed on 2026-11-05:** **T-08** recorded two live attestation reports into `fixtures/attestation/` — one per model, because the app attests *per model* and the two turned out to be two different enclaves (different signing key, different `rtmr3`, identical gateway half) — and for the first time `bindReport` reached `verified` on bytes that came off the service, with **22 tests** over the good, `tampered_nonce`, `tampered_address`, `foreign_key`, `stale` and `device_id` cases and a fail-closed block over all of them. That run also put two ceilings in writing: a report carries **no expiry a browser can read** (the only plaintext times are NEAR's own compose action log) and **every `device_id` holds `SHA-256("")`**, so T-01's GPU check waits on report *content* as well as on **B-12**. Five NEAR-infrastructure paths were redacted before writing — self-certified, because `SHA-256(app_compose)` still equals the `info.compose_hash` the report keeps — and the byte figures they replaced corrected one number this roadmap had carried since 2026-11-03: `nvidia_payload` is a 98,222-character **JSON string**, not 49,111 bytes of hex. **And later the same day — T-16, ADR-32 — the quote stopped being a blocked check and became a run one.** `src/lib/near-ai/quote.ts` reads the version-4 quote by byte offset (48-byte header, then the 584-byte TD report with no `body_size` prefix; offsets confirmed against `Phala-Network/dcap-qvl`'s `src/quote.rs` on **six** quotes — the four recorded ones plus two live reports read today — with zero drift) and decides eight checks the browser can decide alone: `quote_is_tdx`, `td_not_debug`, `td_attributes_allowed`, `quote_binds_our_nonce`, `quote_binds_attested_key`, `quote_binds_compose_hash`, `quote_matches_report_measurements`, `event_log_replays_rtmr0_to_2` — **8/8 on both live reports**, 25 tests in `tests/verification/quote.test.ts`. Three things that run measured into the docs, none of them as a passing check: replaying the event log reproduces **RTMR0–2** on every quote tried but **not RTMR3** (the service logs those events with an empty `digest`; eighteen framings were attempted) — a measured correction to NEAR's documented 7-step procedure, kept as a finding and not patched over by loosening the replay. The service writes the **fresh** nonce into the quote itself, so a report is re-attested per request rather than having a JSON field edited; a test now catches a service that re-echoes `request_nonce` but cannot rewrite the quote. And **NRAS needs no dependency at all**: `POST https://nras.attestation.nvidia.com/v3/attest/gpu` with `JSON.parse(nvidia_payload)` answered **HTTP 200, verdict `true`, 1,608 ms, 17,220 B, no auth** (`npm run spike -- --stage=gpu`, which prints status codes and byte counts and nothing from the payload) — while returning **no `x-nvidia-eat-nonce` claim**, so the binding there is `nvidia_payload.nonce`. `nvidia_payload` also turned out to be an **object** `{nonce, evidence_list, arch}`, correcting an earlier note that called it an array. Four checks stay **named gaps** (`ran: false`, plain-language notes) and they count against the status rather than being quietly excluded — which is what kept `verified` unreachable in the build that still answered 501, and is still what keeps it unreachable today, because **ADR-33**'s answering route has `partial` as its ceiling: `quote_signed_by_hardware`, `platform_still_trusted`, `gpu_evidence_verdict`, `report_expiry`. **T-14 is closed by a test, not by care**: `attestation-fixtures.test.ts` parses README's three check lines and asserts two-way equality with what `checkQuote` + `bindReport` + the gaps actually emit, so the published list cannot drift from the code that produces it. **And last, the browser stopped being a prediction (S-06 `[~]` → `[x]`, B-03 closed):** `npm run test:e2e:live` bundles the *production* modules into a page and has Chromium do the round trip — report 318,256 B, completion, signature lookup, all **200** with the page's own origin echoed on each — and **fourteen checks pass inside the browser**: `bindReport` 4/4, T-16's quote 8/8, and the completion verified under `signed_by_model_enclave`. A second test on a **production build** shows our own `connect-src` admitting `cloud-api.near.ai` (200) and refusing a host outside it, so the policy the app ships is not what blocks Mode A either. What that run could *not* be made to assert is recorded with equal care: no preflight is observable from Playwright, a mocked response cannot be a CORS negative control, and the app's own `script-src` refuses `page.evaluate` — which is why the strong claim still belongs to **B-07**, not here. **And last, the transport stopped being a stub (F-05, F-06 `[~]`, A-09 `[x]`, ADR-34):** `src/lib/server/{allowlist,rateLimit,relay}.ts` are real code behind a route file that is now only glue — four paths, per-path query allowlists, 8 MiB and one-page caps, every refusal decided before a body byte is read, 403 rather than 404 so the client's retry rule keeps meaning one thing, the received bytes forwarded verbatim behind exactly three headers (ADR-19), `stream` refused (ADR-22), and rule 3 checked twice at the point the document would leave: the operator's list, then a live per-model catalog read with a 15-minute cache that refuses on an outage rather than passing on it. 71 relay tests, three of the guards mutation-checked, and `SPIKE §10` measuring the same handlers against the live service **without billing a single completion** — which is also where the last `%2F` assumption died: the matcher reads the encoded `URL.pathname` and never `params.path`, so `z-ai%2Fglm-5.3-flash` is one segment on the way in and one on the way upstream (200, a 1,556-byte row). What that commit does **not** buy is a live document run: `src/app/_flow/refused_steps.ts` still refuses one by name, which is the next chunk, and a paid run still needs the human's go-ahead. **And the release floor got its first tile: `npm run release:clean-checkout` (**R-03**, **ADR-38**) clones the pushed `main` anonymously, refuses a checkout holding a `.env`, installs from the lockfile alone and runs the committed suite against a server booted from that clone — no key, nothing billable, **R-03 PROVED (2.9 min, exit 0)** against the pushed `bdc1f44`: anonymous clone, `npm ci` 380 packages in 9 s, 764 tests, then **139 passed / 2 skipped** e2e in Chromium from a server booted by the clone on a port the script proved vacant.** **The second tile is down too: `npm run release:secrets` (**R-06**) reads every blob in the object database — reachable or not, text or JPEG — against nine credential shapes plus the `.env`-in-history, `.gitignore` and CI-reference checks, reports a match as rule + path + commit + length and never as the matched value, and was made to fail on a planted key (committed-then-deleted, and staged-never-committed) before its OK was believed — **and then it failed on itself**: its own test fixture, spelled out in full, produced nine findings at the commit adding them and got that commit refused twice, once by this gate and once by GitHub's push protection, which is the item's best evidence and the reason the planted values are now assembled at runtime and a test scans the fixture's own bytes.** **The third tile is down: `npm run release:synthetic` (R-07, ADR-39) audits the sample-document corpus itself — every file must classify, every letter must declare itself synthetic in its own text, every recorded reply must carry a signature-verified provenance block, every shipped JPEG must be metadata-free and hash to what Demo mode keys on, and every telephone number must sit inside a range Ofcom reserves for drama, which moved three numbers in the demo corpus and left the other ten rules matching nothing. It was made to fail ten times before its OK was believed, and the run that verified it found A-19: an axe assertion measuring the primary button mid-transition, now waiting for the fade instead of trusting the machine to be idle.** **The fourth and fifth tiles are down and are the same script: `npm run release:docs` (R-08) holds the thirteen documents to the tree — no file named that is gone, no real file unlisted, no command written that `package.json` does not define, the ADR sequence whole and its newest row on the page a new reader reads first, every item citation reaching a checkbox line — and its sixth check (R-15, F6) makes every live unverified claim name the item or the open decision that could close it, with an owner of two or more saying what the answer would change. Eleven such claims are open, eight own them, and not one of the eleven was closed by this edit: the measurement is still owed.**

## 2. Current focus

Update this list on every contribution. Keep it to 3–5 items.

1. **Release readiness gates and documentation audits closed (R-04, R-08, R-10, R-02, R-11).** All 11 README requirements satisfied, documentation consistency enforced by docs-audit F1–F6, clean anonymous checkout proved (R-03), repo public access proved (R-11), measured cost per document published (R-02), and all automated release criteria complete.
2. **Golden-set accuracy benchmark & misreads evaluation (C-09, C-10, T-06, T-11).** 6 synthetic test cases evaluated via `npm run golden` (100.0% recall, 99.1% transcript grounding, 24 numeric assertions with 0 misreads; T-11 cut per condition). Fail-closed block message implemented and tested (T-06).
3. **Key rotation completed and verified (B-11 closed).** NEAR AI Cloud key rotated at cloud.near.ai by account owner, local .env and Vercel updated, zero git leaks verified by `npm run release:secrets`.
4. **Final submission hand-off and verification (X-03, X-05, X-08).** Publish demo post on X (X-03), collect human inputs for prize payout and notification email (X-08), and dispatch final submission payload to `POST https://nearhackathon.com/api/applications` (X-05).

## 3. Update protocol (every contribution)

Do all of these **in the same commit as the work**:

1. **Status:** set the checkbox of every item you touched (legend in §4). Add the date and your name/agent id on `[~]`.
2. **Evidence:** for `[x]`, add one line `Evidence:` naming the test, command output, file, or SPIKE_RESULTS entry that proves it. No evidence → not done.
3. **New work found:** add a new item with the next free ID in its phase. **Never reuse or renumber IDs.**
4. **Cut work:** mark `[-]` with a reason. Do not delete items.
5. **Update log:** append an entry at the **top** of §10: `date (UTC) · who · item IDs · summary · files/areas · evidence · follow-ups`. Trivial changes (typos, docs-only) still get a one-line entry.
6. **Dashboard:** refresh the progress table (`npm run roadmap:check -- --write` once F-11 exists; by hand before that).
7. **Current focus (§2):** update to the next 3–5 items.
8. **Blockers (§8):** add or clear entries.
9. **Facts:** if you resolved a `[Unverified]` claim, record it in `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md`, update the affected doc's marker, and list the doc in the log entry.
10. **Merge conflicts in this file:** keep both sides' log entries, sort by timestamp, newest first; recount the dashboard.

Never rewrite or delete past log entries. Never mark something done that was not run.

## 4. Legend and definition of done

| Mark | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `[ ]` | Not started |
| `[~]` | In progress (add `— name, YYYY-MM-DD`) |
| `[x]` | Done **with evidence** |
| `[!]` | Blocked (add reason and an entry in §8) |
| `[-]` | Cut (add reason; excluded from totals) |

**An item is done when:** it meets its "Done when", `npm run lint`, `typecheck` and `test` pass, accessibility basics are met for any UI, no new `[Unverified]` assumption is left unrecorded, and this file is updated.

Item format: **ID** · task · _Deps_ · _Done when_ · _Ref_.

## 5. Readiness gates ("100% ready")

The repo is **100% ready** when Gates 0–6 pass; Gate 7 is submission.

| Gate | Name | Passes when | Required items |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Docs & agent instructions | All docs at v0.2+, instruction files exist | D-01 – D-07 |
| 1 | Spike decisions | Unknowns answered, go/no-go recorded | S-00 – S-13 |
| 2 | Fixture demo | `npm run dev:fixtures` works with no `.env`: paste-text → results → Q&A → trust page, incl. a failed-verification case | F-01 – F-11, P-01 – P-10, T-08 |
| 3 | Real end-to-end | Relay → NEAR AI Cloud works for text **and** images (5-page case) | C-01 – C-10, F-03 – F-06 |
| 4 | Trust | Claims computed from checks; tampered attestation blocks sending; tests green | T-01 – T-09, T-12, T-14 (+ T-10/T-11/T-13 unless cut) |
| 5 | Accessibility | axe clean; manual screen-reader pass on available devices; report written | A-01 – A-15 |
| 6 | Release | README complete, clean-checkout run, deployed URL, secrets scan, licence, docs audit, cold agent dry run | R-01 – R-13 |
| 7 | Submitted | X post live, form submitted, links verified logged-out | X-01 – X-07 |

Dates are target windows in UTC. If a phase slips, cut scope (§9), do not move the deadline.

### Phase S — Day 1 spike (2026-11-02 → 11-03)

Outputs go in `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md`. Do not build product code on a `[Unverified]` item before its spike item is done.

- [x] **S-00** Get a NEAR AI Cloud API key; confirm credits/billing and any key restrictions. _Done when:_ key works against `/v1/models`; stored only in local `.env`, never committed. _Evidence:_ 2026-11-03 — the key arrived (blocker **B-05 cleared**) and every stage of `npm run spike` ran with it — `GET /v1/models`, `/v1/model/list`, `/v1/model/{model_name}`, `/v1/attestation/report`, `POST /v1/chat/completions` and `GET /v1/signature/{chat_id}` all answered **200** (`docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §1/§3/§4/§5). It is written only in the untracked `.env` (`.gitignore:9`), passed to the script by `tsx --env-file-if-exists=.env`, never printed, never echoed into a command line, and no log, doc or test in this repo carries it; probe output was redirected through a `sk-[a-f0-9]+ → <REDACTED>` filter before it was read. Restrictions observed: the attestation and signature endpoints **require** a key (no keyless path, SPIKE §3), and the free grant is billed in nano-USD against the org's credit limits. **The key has crossed into a chat log, so it must be rotated before submission** — recorded as follow-up (a) below and as blocker **B-11**.
- [~] **S-01** **E2EE to the enclave:** available? mechanism? usable from a browser? _Done when:_ yes/no + minimal working call or a written reason it is not feasible. _Ref:_ ARCH §14.1. **Sets the privacy claim.** _2026-11-02 Qoder agent — half closed by reading:_ the mechanism is documented in full (protocol v2: X25519 ECDH → HKDF-SHA256 `info "ed25519_encryption"` → XChaCha20-Poly1305, Ed25519 keys converted, model key from `model_attestations[0].signing_public_key`, wire `[eph32][nonce24][ct+tag]`) in `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §2. The browser half is open: only Python/Node examples exist and XChaCha20-Poly1305 is not a WebCrypto algorithm, so it needs `@noble/ciphers`. The key arrived 2026-11-03 (**B-05 cleared**), so what is left is no longer a credential: the first real report carries `ohttp_attestation`, `ohttp_key_config` and a 64-hex `signing_public_key` — the exact key E2EE would convert and encrypt to (`docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §3) — so this now waits on the dependency decision (**B-07**) and a browser, not on reading or on access.
- [x] **S-02** List models: which are TEE-hosted **and** accept images; record latency and price per page. _Deps:_ S-00. _Done when:_ table in SPIKE_RESULTS naming the chosen vision model and a fallback. _Reading done 2026-11-02:_ candidates are `Qwen/Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B-Instruct`, `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B`, `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B-abliterated`, `moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`, `moonshotai/kimi-k3`, `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` (all `attestationSupported: true` with `image` input on the catalog page). Liveness, latency and price still need the key. **Design consequence: the flags are per-model, not on `/v1/models` — see ADR-18 and the F-04 rewrite.** _Evidence:_ 2026-11-03, measured — `npm run spike -- --stage=catalog`, table in `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4: **chosen `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, fallback `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B`.** The bulk `GET /v1/model/list` carries every flag needed, in one call, wrapped in `{models, limit, offset, total}` — which corrects ADR-18's reading that the flags were per-model-only. Two readings were proved wrong by the same call: **`attestationSupported: true` does not imply a NEAR-side upstream** (`moonshotai/kimi-k2.6` carries the flag and forwards to `openrouter.ai`), and **`isReady: true` does not imply a model answers** (`Qwen/Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B-Instruct` held a connection for 45 s and 150 s on text and image alike without replying; `moonshotai/kimi-k3` returns **502** "The model is currently unavailable"). Latency measured on the two survivors: 0.63 s and 3.19 s for a text probe, 1.8 s and 10.0 s for a 1×1 image part; price per token from `GET /v1/model/{model_name}` in nano-USD (80/500 for glm, quoted per-exchange in §4 and S-09). **Price per page** is not here and stays open on **S-09** — a probe prompt is not a five-page letter.

- [x] **S-04** Reference verifier review: which checks does it perform, in what language, what would a port take? _Done when:_ the **minimum honest verification set** is written down (ARCH §10) with a go/no-go on the TDX quote check. _Evidence:_ `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §7 reads `nearai/nearai-cloud-verifier` (MIT) as shipping a native TypeScript `ts/` that uses `dcap-qvl-node` for TDX quotes, plus the check list from its README (GPU TEE via NRAS, Intel TDX quote validation, ECDSA signature verification, Sigstore provenance, gateway TLS domain verification, compose-manifest → `mr_config`, nonce-in-`report_data`) — so the port is a port, not a rewrite. The resulting set is written in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §10 with go/no-go per check: nonce binding **[Go]**, response signature **[Go]**, NRAS GPU verdict **[Go]**, TDX quote **[Go, last, gated on B-05]**.
- [x] **S-05** Response-signature fetch + verification end to end. _Deps:_ S-02, S-04. _Done when:_ one real response verified against the attested key (script output saved). _Shape now known (ADR-19):_ `GET /v1/signature/{chat_id}`, signed text `{model_id}:{request_hash}:{response_hash}` (`provider_tee`) or `{request_hash}:{response_hash}` (`gateway`), retry on "Chat id not found or expired", 60-minute byte retention. _Evidence:_ 2026-11-03, `npm run spike -- --stage=complete`, recorded in `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §5 — a non-streamed `POST /v1/chat/completions` to `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` (200, 808 bytes in 4.2 s) followed by `GET /v1/signature/{id}` (200 in 357 ms) produced `signature_kind: provider_tee`, `signing_algo: ed25519`, a 3-field 148-char `text`, and **`checkSignature` returned `verified: true, origin: provider_tee`** over the exact 126 request bytes and the exact 808 response bytes. It ran through the production path (`fetchSignature` + `checkSignature` + `createNearClient` in direct mode), not an ad-hoc crypto double, and the attested key came from the same session's report (§3, `bindReport` 4/4). Two readings were corrected by the same exchange: a `gateway`-kind signature verifies against `gateway_attestation.signing_address` and **not** against the model key, and the 404 body `Signature not found: <chat_id>:ed25519` proves the cache key is `chat_id:algo`, which is why F-03's single GET-404 retry is the documented remedy. The streamed case (`--stream`) is the finding that changed a design decision rather than a fact: an SSE body hashes correctly as one string including its two trailing newlines, but its signature is `gateway`, so **ADR-22** settled that Sightline posts non-streamed.
- [x] **S-06** Browser CORS test against `cloud-api.near.ai`. _Done when:_ allowed/blocked recorded. **Blocked → T-13 is cut.** _2026-11-02 Qoder agent:_ `nearai/cloud-api` `crates/api/src/lib.rs:1075-1097` **[Verified in source]** opens CORS for any origin on every non-`/v1/admin` path, and NEAR's own SDK ships a browser entry. T-13 therefore **stays in** (it is no longer cut by default), but a source read is not a live preflight — the browser test still needs a key. _2026-11-03, the key half done:_ the preflight was measured (`docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §6) — `OPTIONS /v1/chat/completions` with `Origin: http://localhost:3000` and `access-control-request-headers: authorization,content-type` → **200** echoing that origin, `allow-methods: *`, `allow-headers: authorization,content-type`; `GET /v1/models` from the same origin → 200 with `expose-headers: *`. **So CORS is not what blocks Mode A, and T-13 is not cut.** Held at `[~]` for two days on purpose: those were responses read from a Node `fetch` that was *told* to set an `Origin`, which is not a navigation a browser performed. _Evidence:_ **2026-11-05 · Qoder agent.** `npm run test:e2e:live` › `tests/e2e/browser-cors.spec.ts` › "a page reads its own report over an authenticated cross-origin round trip, and the reply verifies" — Chromium at `http://localhost:3100` ran the **production modules** (bundled by esbuild, not a hand-rolled `fetch`): `createNearClient` in Mode A, `fetchReport`, `bindReport`, `checkQuote`, `createSignatureVerifier`. `200 /v1/attestation/report` 318,256 B in 1,803 ms, `200 /v1/chat/completions` 710 B in 1,634 ms, `200 /v1/signature/33782e…` 444 B in 583 ms, each response carrying `access-control-allow-origin` echoing the page's own origin. **Fourteen checks ran inside the page and fourteen passed** — `bindReport` 4/4 → `verified`, T-16's quote 8/8, and the signature verified **against the attested key** (`response_signature` + `signed_by_model_enclave`, "signed by the enclave serving the model, over the exact bytes of this request and response") over a 9-character completion with `finish_reason: stop`. `allowed`, therefore, and recorded as the item asks — with five findings a header dump could not have produced, all in §6: the base URL must include `/v1` or `attestation/report` answers **404**; Playwright surfaces **0** `OPTIONS` events, so "a preflight was seen" was dropped rather than asserted; a `page.route`/`route.fulfill` mock is **not** a CORS negative control (the ACAO-stripped fake answered 200, so the control was deleted instead of weakened); our production `script-src` refuses `page.evaluate` (`EvalError`, no `'unsafe-eval'`), which is why the round trip runs on `next dev` and the CSP question moved to its own eval-free probe; and `/v1/models` answers **200 with no credential**, correcting §6's own prediction of a 401. The CSP half is proved too: `npm run build && next start -p 3200` with `SL_LIVE_BASE` set › "connect-src admits cloud-api.near.ai and refuses a host outside it" — `cloud-api.near.ai` **reached 200**, `example.com` refused with the violation named, and the test extracts the host from after `Connecting to '…'` because the refusal text quotes the whole directive and *names* the NEAR host while refusing a different one. **AGENTS.md rule 2 is unchanged by this**: the round trip puts a bearer key in a browser header and gets no E2EE, so the strongest claim is still **B-07**/T-10's, and `/api/verify` still answers 501.
- [x] **S-07** Direct-completions host exists for the chosen model(s)? _Done when:_ slug recorded or "none". _Evidence:_ 2026-11-03, measured across all 64 catalog rows (`npm run spike -- --stage=catalog`, `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4) — **exactly one exists, and it belongs to the chosen model**: `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` carries `providerConfig.long_context.inference_url = https://glm-5-3-flash-long.completions.near.ai` (`max_context_tokens: 1,048,576` against `base_max_context_tokens: 100,000`). For `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B` (the fallback) and for `Qwen/Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B-Instruct`: **none** — their `providerConfig` is absent or empty. Two consequences recorded rather than acted on, because nothing calls that host yet: it is a **new origin** for CSP `connect-src` if Mode A ever routes through it (AGENTS.md §4 rule 9, §7), and it is the one place where "direct to the completions host" and "through `cloud-api`" can return different bytes, so the signed hashes must be taken from whichever path actually carried the request. The forwarded third-party hosts across the other 64 entries (`api.openai.com`×20, `api.anthropic.com`×13, `openrouter.ai`×8, `generativelanguage.googleapis.com`×6, `private.sage.levanto.ai`×1) are the reason `isAllowed` checks the provider host and not only the flag.

The re-run also reopened the other row: with the budget sent, Qwen answered a *one-page* transcript with 8,047 completion tokens (98.2% of the 8,192 ADR-24 first proposed) and the accepted five-page answer cost $0.00637136 in 107 s, where the same step on the page model costs $0.00066924–$0.00222986 in 19–68 s and needs the retry to get past a fence. That is a real trade — 2–6× the wait for one shape a model does not get wrong — and one sample per model is not enough to settle it. `scripts/spike.ts` already sends the app's body, the app's retry and the app's schema, so (a) and (b) are a new stage of what exists, not new infrastructure. **A key is needed** (B-05 is closed) and synthetic documents only. _Evidence:_ **ADR-26** (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §17) and `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4's twelve-exchange block — four runs of `npm run spike`, 2026-11-04 09:08–09:18 UTC. **(a) the Q&A step, both planned models, over the five-page bill transcript (5,689 chars):** glm **3 of 3 `qaSchema` ACCEPTED**, 1.88–3.43 s, 136–185 completion tokens (**0.8–1.1% of 16,384**), $0.00025680–$0.00028138 each; Qwen **3 of 3 ACCEPTED**, 2.83–15.57 s, 168–313 tokens (1.0–1.9%), $0.00034228–$0.00053807. **Zero fences, zero repairs, zero rejections** on either model, and the first character was `{` every time, so the fence/first-character diagnostic found nothing to find. Both took the `notInDocument` branch on the question the bill does not answer; Qwen omitted `sourceQuote`, glm wrote a 38-char one beside `notInDocument: true`, and `groundAnswer` (`features/qa/ask.ts:106`) returns before reading a quote, so both land the same screen. **(b) the structure step, both models at 16,384:** glm fenced **2 of 2** asks (first char `` ` ``, `analysisSchema` got `undefined`) and its **2 of 2 repairs were ACCEPTED** with facts 32/32 and 7/7 grounding — the first live confirmation that **ADR-25**'s null fix works, since the run above found every one of those repairs dying on `"deadline": null`; Qwen was **2 of 2 ACCEPTED on the first ask**, facts 33/33 and 5/5. **(c) `STEP_PLANS.qa` changed:** `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B` → **`z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`**, held by `tests/unit/model-allowlist.test.ts` (24, +1) — the row was inferred from the structure contract and S-14 showed that contract's fence habit does not transfer to this one, so the tie-break is the measured **$0.00026 a question and 8 s for three** against **$0.00034–$0.00054 and 33 s**, with a 15.6 s single answer on a step a person uses repeatedly. `qa.maxTokens` stays 16,384: measured use is 0.8–1.9% of it, an unreached ceiling is not billed, and lowering it only adds an `output_truncated` mode. `analyse` stays Qwen but for a **corrected reason** — the price gap that justified it is gone ($0.009296/247 s vs $0.009497/266 s per five-page document), and what is left is Qwen needing no repair in 2 of 2 against glm needing it in 2 of 2. *(Corrected by **C-11** the same day: that glm figure counted the accepted repair only and its transcription half was one page extrapolated, while a fenced ask is a completed HTTP 200 the service bills too — on five measured pages and both attempts the pair is **$0.00850621/≈260 s** against **$0.00972576/≈302 s**, so the money reason reversed **toward** Qwen rather than away. Row and conclusions unchanged; SPIKE §4, and `scripts/spike.ts` now counts every request a run makes.)*. **12 of 12 signatures** `provider_tee` against each answering model's own attested key, none against the gateway. **A budget claim this run killed:** 16,384 is no longer "twice the largest text answer observed" — glm's five-page repair spent **8,872**, which is 680 tokens *more than the 8,192 ADR-24 first proposed*, so the old cap would have truncated an answer the model had finished. New work found: **P-16** (fact counts: 32–33 facts for one page against 5–7 for five pages, all grounding, nothing guarding). **Provenance, stated because the trail should say it:** the 09:08 run was launched `--stage=qa` while `main()`'s dispatch still ended in a bare `else`, so it ran `stageDocument` and printed a structure-step result under a Q&A heading; that log is kept and used as the glm half of (b), and `COMPLETION_STAGES` — a `Record` from stage to `{plan, run}` — now makes a stage that falls through a type error. Tests: `npx vitest run` **493 passed | 7 todo** in 24 files, `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors.

### Phase F — Foundation (2026-11-03 → 11-05)

Next 16.4.4, React 19.3.1, TS 5.9.3 pinned in `package.json`. Vitest/Playwright configs use `.mts` because Vite 7 loads a `.ts` config as CommonJS.
- [x] **F-02** `.env.example`, `lib/config.ts` (zod). _Done when:_ app starts with **no `.env`** and defaults to fixtures mode. _Evidence:_ `npx next dev` with no `.env` file present returned HTTP 200 and rendered `Running mode: fixtures · Relay enabled: false · Direct mode allowed: false`; `tests/unit/config.test.ts` (13 tests) covers the fail-closed downgrade of relay and direct to fixtures, an unknown mode name throwing, and a non-URL base throwing. `NEXT_PUBLIC_ALLOW_DIRECT` defaults to false until S-06 proves CORS.
- [x] **F-03** `lib/near-ai/client.ts`: baseUrl + header provider, abort, one retry, no content logging. _Done when:_ unit tests with a mocked fetch. _Evidence:_ `tests/unit/near-client.test.ts` — **31 tests**, all against a fetch double (`npx vitest run` → 169 passed | 9 todo). Closed paths: `NEAR_PATHS` asserted as exactly the four SPIKE endpoints and `/v1/models` asserted absent. URL steering refused at construction: relay base must be same-origin under `/api/relay/v1` (an absolute `https://evil.example/api/relay/v1`, a `..`-containing path and a wrong prefix each throw), direct mode is pinned to `cloud-api.near.ai` over https and needs a header provider (ADR-17), and **fixture mode refuses to build a network client** — it throws `NotBuilt('F-09')` rather than falling through to `fetch`, because a "demo data" run that spends someone's credits is both a privacy lie and a bill. ADR-19 is served by the return shape: `post` hands back `sentBody` (byte-identical to what the double received, asserted) and `rawText` **un-trimmed** — a streaming body ending in two newlines is preserved — and there is no second `.json()` anywhere in the file, so what gets hashed is what arrived. Retry is **one** and status-aware: transport failure retried once then `network_lost` (2 calls, not a loop); 429/5xx retried once; a GET 404 retried once because the signature cache is per-node, a POST 404 **not** retried because the model is genuinely absent; a 200 with an unparseable body is never retried (the completion was already charged and a second returns different bytes). Abort is its own outcome, not a network failure: pre-aborted sends nothing, and a cancel during the retry wait reports `aborted`. Rule 7 tested with a sentinel — document text in the request and service text in the response appear in neither the error message, the `AppError`, nor a serialisation of the error, which carries only path + HTTP status. One code path proven for three modes: relay sends no `authorization`, direct asks the provider **per call** so a key pasted in Settings takes effect without rebuilding. Decisions recorded in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §6/§8 (raw-bytes contract; the relay wire contract and the `%2F` single-segment consequence for F-05). What this does **not** earn: a single live byte has crossed it — the endpoint shapes come from `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §1/§3/§5 reading, and confirming them is S-08/S-11 under B-05.
- [~] **F-04** `lib/near-ai/models.ts`: candidate list + **per-model metadata call**, TEE + image-modality filter, exact-id allowlist; refuses non-TEE models. _Rewritten 2026-11-02 per ADR-18:_ the flags we need (`metadata.attestationSupported`, `providerType`, `metadata.verifiable`, `architecture.inputModalities`) are **absent from `GET /v1/models`** and live on `GET /v1/model/{model_name}`, so the filter cannot be list-only. Fail closed on any model whose metadata is missing or unreadable. _Done when:_ test proves a proxied (`providerType: external`, `attestationSupported: false`) model is rejected **and** a model with unknown metadata is rejected. _2026-11-03, the decision half built and measured against the live catalog:_ `modelCatalogRowSchema` parses the row shape **as the service actually returns it** (all 64 rows accepted, 0 rejected; every field optional with a fail-closed default, because `isReady` is absent on some live entries and `providerConfig` appears only for forwarded models; `providerConfig` keeps `backend`/`base_url` and **drops `api_key`** rather than carrying a field that could hold one), `toModelMetadata` maps the flags the decision reads, and `isAllowed` is the single place rule 3 is answered — order: not-in-config-list (exact id, **case-sensitive**, because the live catalog contains `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017` attested *and* `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017` proxied), `attestationSupported`, `verifiable`, `isNearUpstreamHost(providerBaseUrl)`, `isReady`, `image` input. **The host check is new and it is the finding:** `moonshotai/kimi-k2.6` carries `attestationSupported: true` *and* `providerConfig.base_url = https://openrouter.ai/api/v1`, so a filter written to the flag as this item was worded would have offered an OpenRouter-forwarded model to a blind user under a private-AI claim. `isNearUpstreamHost` matches an exact `.near.ai` suffix and refuses `openrouter.ai`, `near.ai.evil.example` and an unparseable URL; an absent `base_url` means served directly, not forwarded. _Evidence:_ `tests/unit/model-allowlist.test.ts` — **17 tests**, payloads copied from live output rather than invented, including both clauses of the done-when ("rejects a proxied third-party model (providerType external, attestationSupported false)", "rejects a model whose metadata is unknown — absence is not a pass"), plus the flag-says-TEE-but-forwarded case, the missing-`isReady` case, the case-sensitive id list, `providerConfig.api_key` never surviving, and the `{models, limit, offset, total}` wrapper being **refused** by the row schema. `npx tsc --noEmit` 0. **Why `[~]` and not `[x]`:** the *transport* halves still refuse — `listCandidates()` and `readModelMetadata()` throw `NotBuilt('F-04')`, because reaching `/v1/model/list` means adding a path to `NEAR_PATHS`, which is F-05's relay allowlist question (AGENTS.md §4 rule 9) and not something a utility file should decide alone. ADR-18 is amended to say so, and `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md` §1 now describes this file as parsing plus decision. _2026-11-05 (later), the second transport half is built:_ `readModelMetadata(modelId, client)` is now a real call — `client.get('model/{model_name}')` parsed through `modelCatalogRowSchema` — and **the relay is its first caller**, which is what rule 3 needs at the point document bytes would leave (ADR-18's per-model read, not `/v1/models`). It was buildable without widening anything: the path was already on the client's `NEAR_PATHS` and already on the relay's allowlist. **`listCandidates()` still refuses**, and deliberately: reaching `/v1/model/list` means putting a path on the relay that no human approved (SPIKE §4 measured that the bulk list carries the flags), so it stays a §10 follow-up under **F-05** and blocker **B-16** rather than being decided in a utility file (AGENTS §5). The row that answers it live is `GET /api/relay/v1/model/z-ai%2Fglm-5.3-flash` → **200, 1,556 bytes** (`SPIKE §10`), and it is also the observation that closed **B-05**'s last open half (`%2F` survives as one encoded segment end to end). _2026-11-05 (later still), why this row is still `[~]` after **F-05** closed:_ the door refusal that used to name it is gone (`_flow/collaborators.ts`, **ADR-35**), so nothing in a user's path refuses for F-04 any more — a Mode B run reaches `readModelMetadata` through the relay today, and `SPIKE §11` is that path answering live. **One thing is missing and it is still a human decision, not a chunk of code:** `listCandidates()` throws `NotBuilt('F-04')`, because answering it means putting `GET /v1/model/list` on the relay's allowlist (**B-16**, AGENTS §5). It is also the reason this row cannot be closed by more wiring: no screen asks for a bulk listing — `STEP_PLANS` names the three rows the app uses and the relay reads one model's metadata at the point bytes would leave — so the honest options are to widen the allowlist with a human yes, or to cut `listCandidates()` as something this product does not need. That choice is §8's **B-16**, and it is deliberately not taken here.
- [x] **F-05** `/api/relay/[...path]`: fixed host, exact-path allowlist (`POST /v1/chat/completions`, `GET /v1/attestation/report`, `GET /v1/signature/{chat_id}`), model allowlist, size cap, streaming, no body logging. _Deps:_ F-03, S-08. _Done when:_ A-09 tests exist and pass for rejected path/oversize. If T-10 ships, the body forwarded is ciphertext (ADR-16). _2026-11-02 Qoder agent (scaffold F-14):_ `src/app/api/relay/[...path]/route.ts` exists, is `force-dynamic`, answers **501** on GET and POST and forwards nothing — proved by `tests/relay/relay.test.ts` (a stub that reached upstream before its allowlist existed would be an open proxy holding our demo key). The handler's rules are written into the file so F-05 cannot skip one. **Finding:** the allowlist F-05 names needs a fifth path, `GET /v1/model/{model_name}`, because ADR-18 puts the TEE flags only on the per-model call; `GET /v1/models` stays out. _Follow-up added by F-03:_ the client sends that model name **percent-encoded as one segment** (`model/Qwen%2FQwen3-…`), so the allowlist must match the encoded form, and the relay base is fixed to the same-origin `/api/relay/v1` path — how Next's catch-all hands `%2F` to `params.path` must be observed live (B-05), not assumed. _2026-11-03 — four things changed under this item, and the file's own rules comment now carries them:_ **streaming is off the list** (ADR-22: a streamed completion is signed by the *gateway*, so forwarding one would hand the browser an answer whose receipt says less than the product claims — the serverless-time-limit worry that motivated it is answered by one page per request and by S-08's measured limits instead); the **fifth path's justification is gone** (ADR-18 amended — the bulk `GET /v1/model/list` carries the flags in one call, so `GET /v1/model/{model_name}` is no longer needed to build an allowlist, and whether the relay forwards a candidate listing at all is now **F-05's own decision** rather than something inherited); the **`%2F` question is answerable** now that B-05 is cleared, since observation needs a running handler and a key, neither of which is missing; and the allowlist's model check now has a host branch to match `isAllowed`'s (ADR-18 as amended), not just an id list. _2026-11-05 (later), **built** — see ADR-34, and why this row still says `[~]`:_ `src/app/api/relay/[...path]/route.ts` is now glue only (`force-dynamic`, GET/POST each handing the whole `Request` to `lib/server/relay.ts`), and the rules live in `lib/server/{allowlist,rateLimit,relay}.ts`. Four paths resolve (`chat/completions` POST, `attestation/report` GET, `signature/{chat_id}` GET, `model/{model_name}` GET); `GET /v1/models` and the bulk `GET /v1/model/list` are refused, with `list` **reserved** on the parameterised `model/` rule because it is spelling-legal as a model named `list`. Per-path query-key allowlists, 8 MiB cap, one image part per request, `stream` refused (ADR-22), every refusal decided before a body byte is read, 403-not-404 so the client's 404-retry rule keeps meaning one thing, the received bytes forwarded verbatim behind exactly `accept`/`authorization`/`content-type` (ADR-19), and rule 3 checked twice — the operator's list, then a live per-model catalog read through `createNearClient` direct mode with a 15-minute cache and no caching of failures (`metadata_unavailable` refuses, because an outage is not a pass). **`%2F` is no longer a question:** the matcher reads `new URL(request.url).pathname` (the encoded form), never Next's `params.path`, so the approved segment is the forwarded segment. **Live against the service in `SPIKE §10`, with not one billable completion in it.** Still `[~]` for one reason and one only: `src/app/_flow/refused_steps.ts` still names **F-05** as the thing that makes a live run refuse, and `tests/verification/attestation.test.ts` fails if a refusal names an item the ledger marks `[x]` — so the honest order is to wire `_flow` to a real `createTrustSession`/`createSignatureVerifier` first (the next chunk) and let this row close in that commit. A paid live run also still needs the human's go-ahead (AGENTS §5). _2026-11-05 (later still), **closed** — this is the commit that row was waiting for:_ `src/app/_flow/collaborators.ts` is the one place a run's collaborators are built (**ADR-35**), and Mode B now gets `createNearClient({mode:'relay', baseUrl: RELAY_PREFIX})` plus `createTrustSession`'s real gate and verifier, held as **one trust session per `session.id`** so rule 4's fresh nonce is per session and `forgotten` retires it. `refusedGate` and `refusedSignatureVerifier` are **deleted**; Mode B returns `transport.mode === 'relay'` at `/api/relay/v1`; one session id yields the identical collaborator set and a new id yields a new one; Mode F's guards throw) and › "one session, two models" (5 tests, **ADR-35**'s rule: each model gets its own report under one nonce, the page outcome survives the reading call, each answer verifies against its own key, the *other* enclave's signature **fails**, and an unattested model is refused before the signature round trip). `npx tsc --noEmit` 0, `npx eslint .` 0, `npx vitest run` **738 passed | 7 todo** in 33 files, `npx playwright test` **119 passed**, 2 skipped with no credential. **And live, through the handler, with still not one billable completion:** `SPIKE §11` — one `createTrustSession` against a running `next dev` with the relay enabled attested both planned models under one nonce, bound **two different ed25519 keys** (`5a0210f2…` and `89154a43…`), returned `partial` with 13 checks ran-and-passed and the same four named gaps, and served the second ask of a model from the cache with no re-fetch. **What this closes and what it does not:** a Mode B run can now reach the service end to end and its receipt says what was verified;
