Try again. Showing the text you gave instead.`), so the screen and the announcement cannot say two things about one failure; `main h2` is `['No summary','Full text','Ask a question','Verification']` with `#summary-heading`/`#facts-heading`/`#actions-heading`/`#warnings-heading` and the skip link all counted **absent** — the sections that would hold a reading are not empty ones claiming the letter had nothing in it; the letter's own words are still on the page (`.transcript` equals the text that was sent, reference `BP-2026-0188` included); the reply's own prose is nowhere (`main`'s text excludes two phrases unique to it — **mutation-checked**: putting the model's sentence into the notice made this test fail, so it is a real guard and not a tautology); two receipts appear, one per attempt, still named demo data; a question there is refused by name with no answer written and focus on the summary; Forget clears the degraded state as well as the transcript; axe reports 0 serious/critical on this markup for the first time and its `main button` targets measure ≥ 44 px. **The English-only notice now appears there** (`appLimitationNotices()` in `features/safety/notices.ts`, rendered inside the no-summary block): the sentence is about Sightline, so it needs no reading to be true, and this is the screen where the app has least else to say. The split is structural, not a copy-paste — `safetyNotices()` now *ends with* `appLimitationNotices()`, and `tests/unit/safety-notices.test.ts` (**+4 tests, now 23**) asserts the tail equality, the same ≤ 3 sentences / ≤ 20 words / no-jargon / no-data-flow copy rules, and that no **content** caution fires without a reading. **The speech decision, made with the screen rendered: no read-aloud control, and that is now proved rather than incidental** — `ReadAloud.tsx` already returned `null` without an `Analysis`, and the e2e asserts both the absence and zero utterances reaching the recorder. The reason is P-09's own rule, not a gap: of the two things the button speaks, this screen holds only the English-only line — already printed where the button would sit — and everything else on it is the full text, which P-09 excludes from speech on *every* screen because reading a page aloud is the person's own reader's job. A button that spoke one caution over a letter the user can already have read would be a control whose output the page shows two lines above it. Reopening it needs a person's evidence, so `tests/a11y/manual-checklist.md` rows 19–20 carry the check and the alternative. Corpus and type: `FixtureCase` is now `FixtureCaseFields & ({analysis}|{unreadableReply})` — a union, because a case carrying both would replay the analysis and never show its bad reply, and a case carrying neither would replay `undefined`; `replay.ts`'s `envelopeFor` branches on it and every other fixture property (content-matched answers, no verification calls, `no_fixture_for_question` for unprepared wording) is untouched. `tests/unit/fixtures.test.ts` **+5 (now 31)**: the case count, "the one unreadable reply is genuinely not the JSON that was asked for" (so the case cannot silently become readable and orphan the browser proof), the degraded run through the **real** pipeline + **real** replay client (no analysis, one `invalid_model_output`, retryable, its userMessage equal to the catalogue's, two `fixtures`-rung receipts, the repair request answered with the same prose and both completion ids naming the fixture, the transcript still written to the session because the fallback needs something to show). Docs corrected to match: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §13's "No row in this table is reachable in Mode F through the retry path … the degraded screen is therefore proved by unit tests only" is replaced by the as-built P-11 note (and §6/§12, `ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS` Accessibility, `PROJECT_STRUCTURE` fixtures/notices/e2e lines, `ACCESSIBILITY_REPORT` row 13 + a not-tested row for the screen as *heard*). Measured: `npx vitest run` **410 passed | 6 todo** (was 401 | 6), `npx playwright test` **80 passed** = 12 shell + 68 flow (was 69: **+11**), `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors. **Honest limit:** no browser had ever rendered this screen before today, and no person has yet stood on it with a reader running (**A-03/A-04/A-06, B-01**) — row 13 proves the DOM contract, and whether the screen is *reassuring* is U-01's question.
- [x] **P-12** (new, from P-10) Prove Forget cancels a request that is genuinely in flight. _Done when:_ an e2e run shows a press during a pending completion leaving nothing on screen, the request cancelled, and no late reply writing the session back. _Why this is an item and not a note:_ P-10's `abort → revoke → clear` order and the "an aborted run writes nothing afterwards" guard in `useDocumentRun`/`useAskAboutDocument` are proved at the seam (unit: the signals flip, and they flip first) but **no browser has ever seen them bite** — Mode F's replay answers in the same tick, so there is nothing to cancel, and live modes refuse at the door (F-04/F-05, **B-05**). The guard is also the only place where a real `AbortError` from `client.ts`'s `aborted` code is meant to be swallowed rather than reported, and an unexercised error path in a privacy control is the kind that gets quietly broken later. _Evidence:_ **the press was made, in a browser, against a completion that was genuinely owed — and it found a leak, which is what this item existed to be worth.** Four pieces. **(1) The state is reachable without inventing anything.** Mode F cannot be made slow by a second real click: `replay.ts`'s `post` is `async` with a resolved value behind it, so a run is a chain of *microtasks* and that queue drains before the next task. So `tests/e2e/flow.spec.ts` › "the request that was still going" dispatches **both** presses from inside one `page.evaluate` — "Read this text", then Forget — which puts Forget's handler in the same task as the run's synchronous prefix, with its controller still registered and its continuation still queued. Nothing in `src/` is paced, delayed or aware it is being raced; the proof that the state was real is the announcement's own count, which had never had a non-zero number to speak. **(2) What it found.** The transcript went out and the reading came back: `runPipeline` reports *as it works*, so `onTrustRecord` and `onAnalysis` dispatched into the session `forgotten` had just emptied. The DOM dump of the failing first run shows Summary, Key facts, Actions and Warnings rebuilt after the person pressed Forget, above "The text you gave is not in this session any more". P-10's end-of-run guard fires *after* the `await`, which is too late for callbacks that fire before it. **(3) The fix is the same rule applied to every write.** `src/app/_flow/live_writes.ts` — `liveWrites(signal, dispatch)` returns a dispatch that drops every action once the signal is aborted; both composition roots now route their pipeline callbacks through it. The reducer cannot do this for itself: a late `analysis_received` is byte-identical to a timely one, which `tests/unit/live-writes.test.ts` (new, **5 passing + 1 todo**, 20 files) asserts *both ways* against the real `sessionReducer` — gated, the forgotten session stays empty; ungated, it happily re-derives the reading. That is the reason the gate lives at the composition root and not in the state machine, and the todo row in that file is the sentence C-02 or F-04 should not lose. **(4) The grammar bug the branch had been hiding.** `useForget.ts`'s counted sentence read "Stopped the request that **were** still going." — no test had ever caused the singular branch to be spoken (every earlier Forget found nothing in flight), so P-10's own announcement was wrong in the one case P-10 wrote it for. Fixed to agree in number, and now asserted verbatim. **Honest limits, stated as the done-when's own words:** Mode F issues **no request at all** to any model or relay endpoint — asserted in the same group from the browser's own request list — so "the request cancelled" here is the app's own `AbortController`, not a socket, and the wire half waits on F-04/F-05/**B-05**. The rejection-shaped cancellation (`NearError` → `RunHalt` at `verification.ts:108`, swallowed by the hook's catch when the signal is aborted) still has never executed, because Mode F's transport answers whatever happens; it is the `it.todo` named above, not a claim. And a *person's* two hands cannot produce this sequence in fixture mode — the press during a slow live run stays a manual check (`tests/a11y/manual-checklist.md` row 21). Measured: `npx vitest run` **415 passed | 7 todo** in 20 files (was 410 | 6 in 19), `npx playwright test` **84 passed** = 12 shell + 72 flow from `--list` (was 80: **+4**), `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors, `npx next build` exit 0 with the same seven routes. The leak test was mutation-checked the way P-11's negative test was: with the gate removed (`if (!signal.aborted)` deleted) the browser test fails and the question test passes, and the file was restored before commit.
- [x] **P-13** (new, from S-09's page run) `completionEnvelopeSchema` must read a truncated answer as truncated, not as a broken transport. _Done when:_ a `content: null` message parses, `contentOf` yields no answer, and the pipeline's existing `finish_reason: length` path is what the person reaches; unit test for both spellings. _Why this is an item and not a note:_ **measured 2026-11-04 00:06 UTC** (`npm run spike -- --stage=image`, `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §1, §4) — a page request whose whole 256-token budget went to reasoning came back `message: {role, content: null, reasoning_content: <string>}` with `finish_reason: "length"`, and the schema requires `content: z.string()`. Through the app that is `invalid_model_output`: the answer is discarded and the person is told the service is broken, when the true sentence is "no answer arrived, it ran out of room". A 256-token budget is small, but the condition is a *budget* condition, so C-03's transcription budget has to be big enough to be reached rarely — and until P-13 is done, a page that reaches it fails with the wrong message. Fix the schema to what the wire carries; do not relax any check to make a test pass (AGENTS.md §5). _Evidence:_ `src/lib/near-ai/envelope.ts` — `content: z.string().nullable()`, `contentOf` folds `null` to `''` so "no answer" stays one rule in the caller, and the **key stays required** (nullable is not optional). `tests/unit/envelope.test.ts` (new, 4 tests): the measured reply parses and reads as no answer, **a message with no `content` key at all still throws**, a string still reads through, an empty string is still not an answer and is still not claimed as complete. `tests/unit/pipeline.test.ts` › "reads a reply that carried `content: null` as a truncation, not as a broken service" puts the live field-for-field shape through `runPipeline` and expects `output_truncated` with **one** post and no analysis — the repair is not spent on a budget. **Mutation-checked in both directions:** with `.nullable()` removed, 2 failed | 36 passed (both new tests); loosened further to `.nullish()`, 1 failed | 3 passed (the required-key test) — so the change is the width of the wire and no wider. Re-run against the live service after the fix: `npm run spike -- --stage=image` **exits 0** and prints `envelope accepted · content 0 chars · truncated: yes` for both geometries, with the unchanged numbers (2,536 prompt / 2,494 image tokens, $0.00020288 per page, 68.2% saved by C-01) and the same verified `provider_tee` signature; the stage exited 1 before it, which is the cleanest possible statement of what this item was. Measured: `npx vitest run` **455 passed | 7 todo** in 23 files (was 450 | 7 in 22), `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors, `npx playwright test` **90 passed**, `npx next build` exit 0, `npm run roadmap:check` OK.
- [x] **P-14** (new, from ADR-24's gap) Carry a model and a reply budget **per step**, and send the budget on the text requests the way the page request already sends one. _Deps:_ S-09, P-13, ADR-23. _Done when:_ one table answers "which model, how many tokens" for each of the three request kinds; every request body has `max_tokens`; a deployment that allowlists only one of the two ids is refused rather than half-working. _Why this is an item and not a note:_ §4's first ten exchanges put the **page** model at 0 of 6 readable answers to the **structure** request while it transcribes pages correctly, and the text requests sent no budget at all — so P-13's `content: null` case, the transcript-ending failure the app can name, was unreachable-but-uncapped on the text path and nothing in the code named which model owes which step. S-02 chose a *page* model and the product has three requests. _Evidence:_ **ADR-24** (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §17) and `STEP_PLANS` in `src/lib/near-ai/models.ts` — `{transcribe: glm-5.3-flash @ 8,192, analyse: Qwen3.8-27B @ 16,384, qa: Qwen3.8-27B @ 16,384}` typed `Record<CallPurpose, StepPlan>` by `satisfies`, so a fourth purpose cannot be added to the client without a plan row. **(Corrected the same day: `qa` is now `glm-5.3-flash` on measurement — ADR-26 — and `DEFAULT_MODEL` no longer exists, because `COMPLETION_STAGES` binds each spike stage to its own plan row — S-14.)** Consumed where the request is built: `features/capture/transcribe.ts`'s `TRANSCRIBE_MAX_TOKENS` now *derives* from the row instead of repeating it, and `features/analysis/pipeline.ts` and `features/qa/ask.ts` each send `STEP_PLANS.<purpose>.maxTokens`. `planModelIds()` is the deployment's requirement, and the reason it is a function rather than a comment: with one id in `ALLOWED_MODELS` a run half-works — pages transcribe and the reading refuses — which is the failure mode a config file cannot show you until someone runs a document. **`scripts/spike.ts` runs the plan rather than a constant** (`DEFAULT_MODEL = STEP_PLANS.transcribe.model`, the document stage defaults to `STEP_PLANS.analyse.model`, `--model=` still forces either), prints `budget used X% of <ceiling>` per exchange, re-binds the attestation report per stage model so a Qwen answer is never checked against glm's key, and **makes the app's one repair retry** with the app's own `REPAIR_NOTE` when and only when the reply is unreadable. Held by **23 tests in `tests/unit/model-allowlist.test.ts`** (+6): the table covers exactly the three purposes; `transcribe.model !== analyse.model`, with both ids named; both planned models pass `isAllowed`, and a deployment naming only one refuses the other with `not_in_config_allowlist`; every budget is > 0 and ≤ the measured `maxOutputLength` of the model that would spend it; `REPAIR_NOTE` names a code fence and forbids prose on both sides. Plus the body shape itself: `tests/unit/pipeline.test.ts` (38, +3) and `tests/unit/qa.test.ts` (33, +1) assert each text request's key set is exactly `['max_tokens','messages','model']` and that the number is the plan's — no `stream`, nothing else (ADR-22) — and the retry attempt carries the same budget. `REPAIR_NOTE` was rewritten for what §4 measured: the old sentence ("no sentence before it or after it") is **satisfied by a fenced reply**, so it did not describe 2 of the 4 failures it was being sent to repair; both fences in the re-run became readable objects one request later, which is the confirmation that the retry — not a fence-stripper — is what that habit needs. **Measured against the live service:** `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4's seven-exchange block. Docs updated in this commit: ARCH §9 (step 2's model, the 16,384 and why it is not the page's 8,192), §13's `Model unavailable` / `finish_reason: length` / `Invalid JSON` rows and the as-built note, §17's ADR-24; `ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS` §"one page" step 6, its model line and its decision list; `PROJECT_STRUCTURE`'s `models.ts` line; README's cost section, which said "per document is still unknown" after S-09 closed. **Deliberately not built:** per-purpose model selection in the composition roots (`useDocumentRun`, `useAskAboutDocument`) — live modes refuse at the attestation gate (**B-12**) and pass `FIXTURE_MODEL`, so wiring a model picker now would be unreachable code that cannot be tested; the plan is read where the bodies are built, and the roots get it when a live run can reach them.
- [x] **P-15** (new, from P-14's re-run) A `null` on a field the prompt marked optional must read as **absent**, not as an unreadable answer. _Deps:_ P-01, P-08, P-14. _Done when:_ every `string?` the app asks for accepts `null` and yields no value; a wrong type and an empty string still fail; the repair retry is not spent on a reply that is already finished. _Why this is an item and not a note:_ **measured, six replies out of six.** In the 2026-11-04 `--stage=document` re-run every attempt that reached a JSON object — 4 on `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` (2 asks fenced, 2 repairs), 3 on `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B` — answered a deadline-less action as `"deadline": null`. `analysisSchema` had `z.string().optional()`, which accepts a *missing* key and rejects that one, so in live mode each of those became a repair retry and then the transcript-only screen (**P-08** → **P-11**) for a document the model had read correctly. It is the same defect P-01 closed for `facts[].kind` — a prompt that invites an answer the code rejects — found the same way, and it is the second time this repo's own contract cost a person their reading. _Evidence:_ **ADR-25** (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §17, with the three rejected alternatives: forbid `null` in the template, strip nulls before parsing, or leave it to the retry) and the schemas themselves — `analysisSchema.sender`, `analysisSchema.actions[].deadline`, `qaSchema.sourceQuote`, `transcribeSchema.pageLabel` are `z.string().min(1).nullish().transform(s => s ?? undefined)`, i.e. all four `string?` fields this service is asked to fill, so the habit does not have to be re-measured per field. Held by **43 tests in `tests/unit/schemas.test.ts`** (+5, in a new `an optional string answered as null is absent, not invalid` block): a null deadline and a null sender are accepted *and stay absent* on the parsed object, `42` and `""` are still refused, and the qa/capture cases read as "nothing to quote" / "no label on this page". `tests/unit/pipeline.test.ts` › "reads the reply shape the live service actually sends" (38, +1) runs the **whole step** on the measured body and asserts the analysis reaches the session with no `onDegraded` — the schema alone does not prove the screen is not shown. The confidence ban (ADR-11), `min(1)` on every required string, grounding and `quoteFound` are untouched: `null` is the only extra value accepted, and it carries no content. Docs corrected: ARCH §9's step-2 bullet ("Omit absent fields — and an omitted field answered as `null` **is** an omitted field"), §13's `Invalid JSON` row (a fence *is* that row, a null optional is *not*), ESSENTIALS step 6 and its decision list. **Honest limit:** `""` is still rejected because no reply has ever sent it, and a `null` in a *required* string (`summary`, `answer`, `text`) still fails closed — P-13's rule that an empty reading must never become a finished-looking one is unchanged. Measured with P-14: `npx vitest run` **488 passed | 7 todo** in 24 files, `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors.

- [x] **P-16** (new, from S-14's run) Decide what the results screen does with a fact list whose **length is neither bounded nor stable**. _Deps:_ S-14, P-07, C-11. _Done when:_ the number of facts a person is offered is a decided quantity (a cap, a ranking, or a stated "showing n of m"), the announced reading of a long list is designed, and a run that returns 33 facts and a run that returns 5 reach the same screen deliberately rather than by accident. _Why this is an item and not a note:_ **measured, and nothing in the app would notice.** S-14's structure-step run gave one page (1,321 chars) **32 facts on glm and 33 on Qwen**, and five pages of the *same bill* (5,689 chars) **7 and 5** — more facts for the shorter document, by 4–5×, and a 6× swing between models on identical input. Every one of them quoted the transcript back, so `quoteFound` passed them all and no guard fires: this is not a failure the pipeline can see. A plausible cause is the models enumerating line items when the transcript is short and summarising when it is long, which is a prompt question, but the fix is not necessarily the prompt — for a person using a screen reader, 33 facts read one after another is a wall of audio with no way to scan it, and §8's fixed results order (Summary → Key facts → Actions → Warnings → …) assumes "Key facts" is short enough to be a summary. **Out of scope here:** whether any fact is *correct* against the page, which is **S-03**, and the wording of any new sentence, which is the human's call (AGENTS.md §5). _Evidence:_ **decided, built, and measured against the recording that made it an item — 2026-11-05, taken under the standing instruction that agents make design decisions and record them.** The rule is one sentence: **a list of 12 or fewer is left exactly as it is; a longer one lists at most 3 facts of each `kind`, in a fixed kind order, capped at 12 in total, keeps the model's own order in what it lists, never hides a fact whose `sourceQuote` did not match, and says both numbers out loud before the list, with the remainder one native `<details>` press away.** `src/features/analysis/facts.ts` (new) owns it — `planFactList(facts)` → `{shown, held}`, `FACTS_SHOWN_MAX = 12`, `FACTS_PER_KIND = 3`, `FACT_KIND_PRIORITY` = medication, dose, amount, date, phone, reference, address, **other** — and `ResultsView` only renders what it is given. Four things worth reading twice. **(1) The cap is per kind, and the recorded demo is why a global one was wrong.** `fixtures/responses/document.json` holds **86 facts: 84 line-item amounts, one `reference` (`NC-0000-SYN`) and one page indicator**. `slice(0, 12)` keeps the first twelve of that array — the reference, the indicator and ten line items — and a cut at the *end* of a kind-sorted list spends every slot on line items while hiding the reference, which is the one value `fixtures/responses/qa` proves a person asks about ("What reference number is on these pages?"). The plan lists 5 and holds 81; the reference is in the 5. **(2) 12 and 3 are measured, not picked.** 12 is above every summarising run in this repo (5–7 for five pages of the same bill, 4 in each authored demo case), so a well-behaved answer never meets this feature at all, and 12 is above the eight `FactKind` values, so every kind can get a slot before any slot is spent twice; 3 is enough to *hear* that a kind repeats, and the sentence names the other 81. **(3) The cap never hides a flag.** A fact with `quoteFound: false` is listed whatever it costs the total, because the sentence below the list says "these facts **above** could not be matched" (rule 8) and that pointer has to stay literally true; `held` is therefore always grounded, which is a test rather than a hope. **(4) The reading is not cut from the record.** `Analysis.facts` stays whole — the schema still has no `.max()` — so grounding, `quoteFound` counts and the receipt all describe the 86-fact reading, and only the *offering* is bounded (**ADR-36**'s rejected option (b), which would have made a display decision look like a property of the reading). `docs/PRD.md` R3 carries the rule, because it is now a requirement, not a preference. Held by `tests/unit/facts.test.ts` (**15 new**: the 86-fact recording — 5 listed, 81 held, reference among them, nothing lost, nothing doubled, document order kept; four sizes under the cap untouched; the boundary at 12/13; 20 ungrounded facts all listed past the cap; an ungrounded fact arriving after the cap is reached still listed while `held` stays clean; one kind never crowds another out; 3 of each kind before any 4th; every `factKindSchema` value present in the priority table with `other` last, so a new kind cannot be silently deprioritised) and **3 new e2e assertions plus one new test** in Chromium with no key: `photo-fixtures.spec.ts` reads the on-screen sentence with the real numbers, 5 `dt` in the visible list and 86 in the document, and presses the control to find `Line 084` `toBeHidden()` before and visible with its quote after, announcer unchanged (expanding costs no second reading); `flow.spec.ts` asserts the opposite on the 4-fact demo — `fact-count` and `more-facts` both `toHaveCount(0)`. _Mutation-checked, each restored after observing the failure:_ replacing the plan with `slice(0, 12)` fails **7 of 15** including *keeps the reference, which a plain first-N cut would have hidden*; deleting the ungrounded pass fails *never holds back a fact whose quote could not be matched, even past the cap*; deleting the short-list escape fails *leaves the demo-sized readings alone — five facts never become a truncated list*. `npx tsc --noEmit` **0**, `npx eslint .` **0**, `npm test` and `npm run test:e2e` in §10's entry. **What is NOT claimed:** this is a *display* answer to an unstable output. The prompt question the item named — why one model enumerates and another summarises, and whether a shorter list is what the person wanted — is still **S-03**'s, and the number of facts a person would *choose* to hear has not been asked of a blind user (**B-01/D-07**).
- [x] **P-17** (new, from C-11 / ADR-27) A fact's or an answer's `sourceQuote` must not be able to land on **Sightline's own sentence** and be reported as grounded. _Deps:_ C-11, P-02. _Done when:_ `groundFacts` (and the Q&A's quote check) treat a match that falls inside an `unreadablePageMarker` hole as **not grounded** — or as its own named state — and a unit test proves a quote of `"[page 1 could not be read]"` does not pass. _Why this is an item and not a note:_ ADR-27 puts the marker **inside** `Transcript.text` so the offsets stay honest and the person can see which page is missing. The cost, stated in the ADR, is that §4 rule 8's "consistent with the transcript" now has one sentence in the transcript that the model never read: a structure step that quotes a neighbouring clause could produce a `sourceQuote` spanning our marker, `groundFacts` would return `quoteFound: true`, and the screen would call **our own words** evidence that the model read the paper. Nothing today makes that likely and nothing today detects it. **Not caused by the model:** it is a property of the join, so it cannot be fixed by a prompt. _Evidence:_ **the chosen alternative is masking at the reader, not a new state: `src/lib/transcript/holes.ts`** (new) owns the marker's wording — moved out of `features/capture/assemble.ts` so the module that writes the hole and the module that reads it share one string — and exports `groundableText()`, which replaces every `[page n could not be read]` with the OBJECT REPLACEMENT CHARACTER (`U+FFFC`, `GROUNDING_MASK`) **one character per character**, so the string keeps its length and every `pageOffsets` and `uncertain` index still means the same thing while the marker's words are gone from what grounding searches. `quoteFound` (`src/features/analysis/grounding.ts`) now tests `groundableText(transcript).includes(sourceQuote)`, and `features/qa/ask.ts` inherits the fix because it calls the same `quoteFound` — one rule covers facts, actions and answers, which is why the guard went in the reader rather than the prompt: a prompt sentence only asks nicely, and it changes the request bytes §4 measured. **8 new cases in `tests/unit/grounding.test.ts`** under `a quote that lands on Sightline's own marker is not grounded (P-17, ADR-27)`: the whole marker (`"[page 1 could not be read]"` — the item's own Done-when quote), a fragment of it (`"could not be read"`), a run that **spans** the hole (real text from the neighbouring page plus the marker), real text on either side of a hole still grounding, every hole masked through `groundFacts`, a pasted transcript (no markers) returned **byte-identical** plus exact mask equality, length preservation, and the one false positive the design accepts — a document whose *own* text prints the marker shape stops grounding that line, which fails **toward** rule 8 (a real quote flagged) and never toward a false claim. **Mutation-checked:** with the `groundableText` call removed from `quoteFound`, **5 of the 8 fail**; restored, the suite is green, so the tests are holding the mask and not each other. Docs updated in this commit: ARCHITECTURE §7's `quoteFound` bullet + §9 step 1b's marker bullet + **ADR-27**'s "known cost" row (now states the closure, the three failing quote shapes, the paste no-op, the false-positive direction and the mutation result), ESSENTIALS rule **7**, step **4b** and pipeline step 7, PROJECT_STRUCTURE's new `lib/transcript/holes.ts` line and the unit-test list, README's `quoteFound` caveat. `assemble.ts` and `scripts/spike.ts` now import the marker instead of spelling it, so the string §4's measured run printed and the string the app writes are one constant. **Not re-run live for this commit** — no new NEAR spend was needed to move a string, and C-11's assembly figures stand from the run that produced them. Tests: `npx vitest run` **515 passed \| 7 todo** in 25 files (C-11's commit measured **507 \| 7**, so **+8**), `npx playwright test` **90 passed** (no route gained markup), `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors, `npm run roadmap:check` OK. **Honest limits:** no live run has yet produced a `sourceQuote` on a hole — the case is built by test, and **S-03** is the run that will hand it real material; the Q&A path is covered by shared code, not by a new Q&A test; and a **different** failure ADR-27 leaves open is unchanged here — a model that quotes a *neighbouring* page's real text verbatim still passes, because that is what rule 8 says `quoteFound` means.

### Phase C — Capture and transcription (2026-11-05 → 11-08)
- [x] **C-01** `image/prepare.ts`: `createImageBitmap` resize, orientation, JPEG, EXIF/GPS stripped. _Done when:_ test with a GPS-tagged JPEG proves metadata is gone. _Evidence:_ **the GPS-tagged JPEG is built by the test, byte by byte, and the metadata is proved gone in a real browser.** `src/lib/image/prepare.ts` is the module: `browserImageIo` decodes with `createImageBitmap` and re-encodes through an `OffscreenCanvas` — **the re-encode is the stripping**, there is no tag-deletion list that would have to know every marker name — then `prepareImage` walks a quality ladder (`0.85 → 0.7 → 0.55 → 0.4`) before it shrinks the long edge (`1600 → 1152 → 800`), returns the first result inside the per-page budget (768 KiB, so base64 keeps five one-page requests clear of the relay's 8 MiB cap), and throws `image_too_large` when nothing fits. Decode/encode is an injected seam, so the arithmetic is unit-testable without a canvas. **18 tests** in `tests/unit/image-prepare.test.ts`: no upscaling, aspect kept, rounding never yields a 0 edge, ladder-before-geometry order asserted as the *product* decision it is (a smaller page loses characters, a blurrier one usually does not), the ladder restarting at the ceiling on each new geometry, exactly one decode per run whatever the encode count, `close()` called once on success, on refusal and on an encoder that throws (the five-page leak), and one test that the result **is not the source blob** — the "fast path for a small image" that would silently undo the whole item. **6 tests** in `tests/e2e/image-prepare.spec.ts` run the module's own source in Chromium (bundled at import time with the `esbuild` Vite already ships; no new dependency), against a 40×20 two-tone canvas JPEG into which the test splices a real APP1: TIFF little-endian, `Orientation: 6`, an Exif sub-IFD and a GPS sub-IFD carrying a version, a latitude reference and the sentinel `51.5074N000.1278W` in `GPSProcessingMethod`. A first test asserts that input really contains those bytes, so nothing downstream can pass vacuously. The output is then walked segment by segment: no `APP1`, no `Exif`, no sentinel, and dims **20×40 with the light band measured on the right half** of the decoded pixels — the rotation is in the image, not in a tag the strip would have deleted. **Both halves were checked by breaking them:** returning the source instead of the encoded blob fails the metadata test while the pixel test stays green (which is why both exist, and why the file says so); and `imageOrientation: 'none'` did *not* break the pixel test, so the rotation is Chromium's default behaviour and the code comment now claims that instead of crediting the option. Measured here, not assumed: Chromium's encoder writes an **APP2 ICC profile** into its JPEGs, so "every APPn segment is gone" would be a false claim — the test asserts what each application segment *is* (no `Exif`, no `GeoTIFF` identifier; an `APP2` must begin `ICC_PROFILE`). A PNG with alpha comes out composited on **white**, not black, and a non-image rejects with the browser's own error **unremapped** — there is no `ErrorCode` for "this file is not a picture", and inventing one in a utility file would be writing user-facing copy without a human (C-02 owns that sentence). `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2/§8 and `docs/ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS.md` step 2 now describe the draw-time downscale rather than the decode-time one the docs assumed (`createImageBitmap` resize needs the target size, which only the decode can give), and `docs/PRD.md` Q2 says so plainly with **C-07** owning the memory question on a device. Counts: `npx vitest run` **450 passed | 7 todo** in 22 files (was 432 | 7 in 21: **+18**), `npx playwright test` **90 passed** (was 84: **+6**; no route gained markup, so A-01's axe sweeps are the same 12 shell + 72 flow), `npx tsc --noEmit` 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors.
- [~] **C-02** `CaptureView` + `PageList`: native camera input, 1–5 pages, remove, retake last, labels "Page n". _Done when:_ operable with a screen reader. _Owed by P-10:_ every object URL it creates must go through `SessionProvider.trackObjectUrl`, or Forget has nothing to revoke and the promise in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.4 becomes a comment. _2026-11-04, Qoder agent, first half (`[~]`):_ **the photo run exists** — `features/capture/read_pages.ts` (`readPages`) is the page loop and `app/_flow/usePhotoRun.ts` is its composition root, which makes them the **first callers** of `assembleDocument` (C-11) and `checkPageLabels` (C-05) that both this item's description promises. `features/analysis/pipeline.ts` no longer derives the transcript from the session: `PipelineDeps` takes `transcript` + `mode` and `session` is gone, because a photographed run and a pasted run now have two different honest answers to "what is the transcript" and the caller is the only place that knows which one it holds. 13 new unit tests (`tests/unit/read-pages.test.ts` 8, `tests/unit/assemble.test.ts` +5 for the hole announcement), two of the loop's claims mutation-checked. **The screen is the second half and is not built**: `useCapture`, `CaptureView`, `PageList`, the mount on `/` and the e2e for it are still open, so nothing calls `usePhotoRun` from a browser yet, and P-10's `trackObjectUrl` obligation is untouched (no object URL is created anywhere). Two things this half decided and wrote down rather than left implicit: the pre-analysis notices are joined into **one** announcement (the announcer holds one message at a time — two writes in one React batch leave only the last, the C-12 finding), and `verification_passed` is **not** dispatched, because naming a check that has not run is rule 5's forbidden thing. The **retake offer** stays C-04's: the issues are announced and the run continues. _2026-11-04 21:57, Qoder agent, second half (`[ ]`→`[~]`, **the screen exists and the browser reaches the door**):_ `features/capture/CaptureView.tsx` + `PageList.tsx` + `useCapture.ts` are mounted on `/` beside the paste box, and `tests/e2e/capture.spec.ts` drives them in Chromium with no key — **19 tests, 2.5 min for the whole suite of 113**. What the screen guarantees, each proved rather than written: **a file never joins the session as it arrived** (every picture goes through `prepareImage` first, so the preview on screen is the *same blob* `transcribePage` will send — a preview of the original would show one thing and read another); **the five-page cap is refused before the bytes are decoded**, in the catalogue's own sentence, and the test proves the ordering by feeding a sixth file that *also* cannot be decoded, so whichever check runs first is the sentence the person hears; **one batch, one announcement** (three chosen photographs are spoken as `3 photographs added, as Page 1 to Page 3.`, and the joined form is what is asserted so the C-12 collapse cannot come back quietly); **a page that is not a picture is skipped out loud** and the pictures still go in; **remove / retake-last / remove-all each say what happened**, including `Page 2 removed. There are no photographs yet.` when the list empties; and **`Page.previewUrl` is this app's first object URL**, registered with `SessionProvider.trackObjectUrl` and revoked again on removal, so **P-10**'s promise is now testable — the browser loads the revoked `blob:` URL after Forget and after a single removal and cannot read it. **P-12**'s half is met the same way: every write of the run goes through `liveWrites`. Five mutation checks: drop `trackObjectUrl`, move the cap check after the decode, announce inside the loop, drop the revoke on removal, and leave `input_selected` after the guards — each fails a named test. Mounting a second form on `/` also created a bug, now closed by **ADR-30**: both forms rendered `session.error`, so the page held two failure summaries, a screen reader heard one failure twice, and the later component in tree order took the A-06 focus target from the paste box (four shipped tests went red and stayed red until the attribution — `session.input`, which both run hooks now set *before* their own guards — made it one summary in the section that ran). **Why `[~]` and not `[x]`:** the "Done when" is *operable with a screen reader*, and none has run this app (**A-03/A-04**); keyboard reachability, axe at serious/critical, and Chromium are what this item can claim today. **And no test here says a photographed document was read**: demo mode stops at `no_fixture_for_input` (**S-11**) and live modes stop at the door (**B-12**), which is what the screen's own hint text and the last test both state.
- [x] **C-03** Transcription request per page; prompt per ARCH §9 (tables row by row, `[illegible]`, `captureIssues`, page labels); strict response schema. _Deps:_ S-02, F-03. _Done when:_ a page image produces a validated `TranscribeOutput` in live mode and a clean refusal in fixture mode; unit tests for the prompt, the schema and the fail-closed order. _Evidence:_ **live** — `npm run spike -- --stage=image` exit 0 sends **the body `readCompletion` builds** (`TRANSCRIBE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` + `Transcribe this page.` + an `image_url` part, `max_tokens: 8192`, no `stream` key) and gets HTTP 200, `finish_reason: stop`, **`transcribeSchema` ACCEPTED**: `text 1,181 chars · uncertain 0 spans · captureIssues rotated · pageLabel absent`, billed 2,810 prompt (2,494 image) / 1,492 completion (1,008 reasoning) = **$0.00097080** in **25.77 s**, signature `kind provider_tee`, `verified=true` against `model_attestations[0]` over the exact 272,736 bytes sent and 5,559 received (`docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4, C-03 block). **tests** — `tests/unit/transcribe.test.ts` (13) pins the order the privacy rules require (`gate → encode → post → signature → onTrustRecord`, and a counted blob reads **0** times when the gate fails), that `Object.keys(body).sort()` is exactly `['max_tokens','messages','model']`, that the user text carries **no page index or count** (C-05's gap check would be vacuous otherwise), that the retry sends a byte-identical system prompt with `REPAIR_NOTE` in the user part and re-reads the blob **once**, and that the prompt template's `captureIssues` enum and `captureIssueSchema.options` hold the **same ten values** — a mutation that drops one from either side fails that test. `tests/unit/image-prepare.test.ts` +3 for `toDataUrl` (exact base64, a 100,000-byte page round-trip for the chunking, and a blob whose `type` lies still labelled JPEG). `tests/unit/fixtures.test.ts` +1 discriminating test: a page call is refused `no_fixture_for_input` **even when the body names a document the fixtures do have answers for** — the guard sits before `findFixture`, verified by removing it (one test fails, no other). Mode F fails closed because a hand-written transcript would put authored prose on screen as though a model had read the photograph (`src/lib/fixtures/replay.ts`). `npm run spike` also confirms **P-13's** `content: null` truncation on the unprepared 256-token probe, and ADR-23's 8,192 budget is the reason this page did not come close to it. _Follow-ups (not this item's gaps):_ **C-04** inherits the measured fact that `rotated` fires on a **0.63°** tilt, so a retake loop that treated it as "take it again" would nag on nearly every hand-held shot; **C-05** inherits `output_truncated` meaning "this page is too dense to be one" → split-and-retry, not a bigger number; **S-09** still owes the structure step priced on a transcript this length.
- [x] **C-04** Retake loop: `needs_retake` status, announce the specific issue and edge, offer retake or continue anyway. _Done when:_ deliberately cut-off photo triggers the right message. _Evidence:_ `src/lib/errors.ts` implements `RETAKE_ISSUES` (8 severe cut-off/quality issues: `cut_off_top`, `cut_off_bottom`, `cut_off_left`, `cut_off_right`, `blurry`, `glare`, `too_small_text`, `not_a_document`), `isRetakeIssue`, `requiresRetake`, and `retakeOfferMessage`. `src/lib/session/reducer.ts` sets `status: 'needs_retake'` only when severe capture issues occur, while informational issues (`rotated`, measured on 6 of 14 live pages on 0.63° tilt in SPIKE §4, and `handwriting_present`) transition to `analysing` without nagging. `usePhotoRun.ts` announces the edge issue and retake prompt, dispatches `transcript_received`, and halts before spending analysis tokens. `CaptureView.tsx` renders a dedicated accessible `.retake-offer` panel with programmatic focus (A-06) offering "Continue anyway" (`continueAnyway` in `usePhotoRun.ts`, dispatching `retake_continued` to finish the reading to `/read`) and retake options in `PageList`. Re-adding, retaking, or removing pages resets `status` to `preparing` and clears `transcript`. Tested by `tests/unit/retake.test.ts` (8 tests) and `tests/unit/session-reducer.test.ts` (30 tests) holding all edge messages, classifications, status transitions, and transcript clearing.
- [x] **C-05** Page-label sanity check: warn on gaps/duplicates ("Page 2 of 3" missing). _Done when:_ unit test. _Measured input from C-03 (2026-11-04):_ a real reply left `pageLabel` **absent** for a page with no printed label, which is the correct empty, and the request that produced it deliberately carries **no page index or count** (`TRANSCRIBE_USER_PROMPT` is the four words "Transcribe this page.") so this check compares what the model read against what the person said they photographed instead of echoing it. _Measured input from C-11 (2026-11-04):_ all **5 of 5** pages in `--stage=pages` printed a label and all 5 were reported (`labels 5`), giving this check its first live material to compare against capture position; the truncated variant shows what a hole does to the list — `labels 4` for 5 photographed pages, because page 1's label left with its text, so a gap check must read the label list against the **page count the person gave**, not against the number of pages that answered. The user message still names no page and no count. _Evidence:_ `src/features/capture/page-labels.ts` — `parsePrintedLabel` accepts the three shapes a printed page actually carries (a fraction `Page 2 of 5`, a keyword `No. 3`/`Sheet 4`, a bare `2.` between decoration) and **rejects** date shapes and amounts, new **ADR-28** (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §17): findings are compared **label against label and label against the photograph count**, never against capture position, because the position is only the order the person tapped the shutter in; `checkPageLabels` returns five finding kinds (`duplicate`, `gap`, `out_of_range`, `conflicting_totals`, `total_differs`), `pageLabelFindingMessage` and `pageLabelSummary` (at most three sentences, the rest counted). **Tests** — `npx vitest run tests/unit/page-labels.test.ts` → **23 passed**: every transcript in the file is built through `assembleDocument` rather than hand-assembled, so the marker case is the real one; the hole cases are the item's reason to exist (`labels 4` against 5 photographs → **`[]`**, and a middle hole → a `gap` carrying `unreadPage` so the message says "the service could not read that photograph" instead of "go find the page"); the silence case is asserted too (a correctly photographed five-page set → **`[]`**, because a check that fires on good work makes a person retake pages that were fine); one test proves the check reads the **labels and not the text** by giving two bodies different numbers under identical labels and requiring the same verdict. **Four mutations, each failing only the tests that should care:** dropping the in-range guard on the gap scan fails **3**, making a duplicate go undetected fails **3**, letting a bare four-digit number parse as a label fails **1**, letting a date through fails **1**. **Live** — `npm run spike -- --stage=pages` exit 0, and this is the run that spent the API money to get real labels (`docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4, second `--stage=pages` block, 2026-11-04 19:30 UTC): five pages all accepted and all five signatures `provider_tee`, no repair, and the check on that assembly prints **`0 finding(s) · 5 of 5 reported label(s) read as a page number · compared against 5 photograph(s)`**; the hole assembly (page 1 re-asked at 256 tokens, `finish_reason: length`, 0 characters — third time that probe has returned nothing) prints **`0 finding(s) · 4 of 4 … compared against 5 photograph(s)`** with offsets `[0, 28, 1197, 2366, 3589]` still exact, and `4 of 4` is the proof the count came from the check and not from a lucky empty list. Those ten pages also price the page step at **$0.00466350 in 116,685 ms**, so the mean across **ten samples** is **$0.00087275 and 25,829 ms a page**, and the honest headline for a five-page document is a **range across two measured runs — ≈$0.0085–$0.0091, ≈235–260 s** — which `README.md` and §4 now say as a range. `rotated` came off 2 of these 5 pages → **4 of 11** measured pages, appended to **C-04**. **Honest limits, stated because each is a gap in "done":** no live assembly produced a **non-zero** finding, so every positive case is held by the unit tests and the mutations only — a genuinely mislabelled stack of photographs is **S-03**'s run, and the first live positive belongs there; nothing calls this check inside the app until **C-02**; the messages are plain-language and screen-reader-shaped but the human copy review is **C-06**, which owes these five sentences their final wording; and the check can only see numbers the model read — a page the person never photographed leaves no label and shows up as a gap **only** if both neighbours reported one, which is why every message says "photograph" and never "your document is incomplete".
