Evaluates 6 synthetic benchmark cases (`case-1-northgate`, `case-2-riverside`, `case-3-meadow`, `case-4-table-heavy`, `case-5-adversarial`, `case-6-recorded-photo`) across bills, bank statements, prescriptions, table-heavy layouts, adversarial injections, and multi-page photographs. Verified in `npm run golden` (exit 0) and listed in `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`.
- [x] **C-10** Measure fact accuracy on the golden set, **numbers separately**; decide T-11. _Done when:_ numbers in README draft with the small-sample caveat. _Evidence:_ `npm run golden` measures 100.0% fact recall (25/25 expected facts across 6 test cases), 99.1% transcript grounding consistency (107/108 facts grounded), 24 numeric assertions evaluated (currency, dates, drama-range phones, account numbers, decimal charges) with 0 numeric misreads detected. Because zero numeric misreads were detected, T-11 condition was not triggered. Results and small-sample caveat published in `README.md` lines 280–286.
- [x] **C-11** Assemble a multi-page document: joined transcript with per-page offsets so `uncertain` spans and grounded quotes still point at the right page, and the truncation decision. _Deps:_ C-03. _Done when:_ unit tests for the offsets and for `output_truncated` on a page; five prepared synthetic pages → one transcript. _Found by C-03 (2026-11-04):_ `TranscribeOutput.uncertain` holds **character offsets into that page's** `text`, so joining pages without remapping them silently mislabels which spans are unreadable — and P-13's `output_truncated` is no longer an unknown: with ADR-23's fixed 8,192 budget a truncation is evidence the "page" is too dense to be one, so the answer is split-and-retry, not a bigger number (ADR-23). _Evidence:_ **the module is `src/features/capture/assemble.ts` (`assembleDocument`, `PAGE_JOINER`, `unreadablePageMarker`), and `npm run spike -- --stage=pages` exited 0 on 2026-11-04 with the offsets proved by a cut rather than by arithmetic** — five pages drawn in Chromium, prepared by C-01's real `prepareImage`, sent as C-03's body **once per page**, and handed to the app's own `assembleDocument`: joined text **6,007 chars**, `pageOffsets [0, 1165, 2334, 3559, 4784]`, **5 of 5** `transcribeSchema` accepted with **0 repairs**, **5 of 5** signatures `provider_tee`, one capture issue (`rotated`, page 3), **5 page labels** read off the paper, `usable true`, `unplaceable spans 0`. The stage then cut the joined string at every offset for each page's own length and compared the pieces to the text that page reported — "each page's region … is that page's own text: yes". That is the property this item exists for: a joiner not counted gives a short region, one counted twice gives an overlapping one, and both would still print a plausible total length. **The hole path ran on a truncation the service produced, not one asserted in a test:** page 1 re-asked at `max_tokens: 256` answered HTTP 200 with `finish_reason: length` and **0 characters of content**, and assembling with that page yields offsets **[0, 28, 1197, 2422, 3647]** (`28` = the marker plus the joiner), `missing 1:output_truncated`, `labels 4` (page 1's label left with its text), `usable true`, region cut still exact for all five. **What a failed page means is now decided — ADR-27** (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §17): it leaves a marked hole **in place** (`[page 1 could not be read]`) instead of being dropped silently, halting the run, or keeping a truncated reply's partial text (there is not one — `content` was 0 characters, which is P-13's finding reused). The ADR's known cost — our own sentence sits inside a transcript that is otherwise verbatim, so a `sourceQuote` can land on the marker and `quoteFound` would call it grounded — is opened as **P-17**. `Transcript.missingPages?: MissingPage[]` (`src/types/index.ts`) carries each missing page's `ErrorCode` and is **absent** for a complete document and for pasted text, so no document-derived string is added anywhere. **18 tests in `tests/unit/assemble.test.ts`** (new file) hold the join and the offsets (byte offsets not line numbers, strictly increasing, capture order kept, a paste-shaped single page is offset 0 and nothing else), the span remapping (shift by page start, clamp to that page's own length, a span that cannot be placed is **counted in `unplaceableSpans`, never dropped silently**), the reported fields (every `captureIssues` entry tagged with the page it came from, labels kept beside capture position), and ADR-27's hole path (1-based page number inside the marker, offsets still usable with a hole, 4-of-5 stays `usable`, all-failed is `usable false` with `''`, `missingPages` absent when nothing is missing). Refused pages never reach the assembly: `PageOutcome` is `Exclude<CompletionResult<TranscribeOutput>, {kind:'refused'}>` and the spike stage filters them and prints that it did, because §4 rule 6 halts the run before a fourth kind of question about a refusal arises. Docs corrected in this commit: ARCH §7's `Transcript`, a new **step 1b (assembly, no model)** in §9, §13's `finish_reason: length` image row, ADR-27; ESSENTIALS step **4b** and its decision list; SPIKE §4's measured block; PROJECT_STRUCTURE's capture line; README's cost section. **Measured, replacing C-08's estimate:** five pages are **$0.00406400 / 141,600 ms** of model time in series — **$0.00081280 and 28.3 s per page on average**, cheaper and slower per page than §4's single sample, which is what five samples of a real spread (607,800–914,300 nano) shows — so a five-page photograph is **≈$0.0085 and ≈260 s** including the structure step as the plan runs it, superseding S-14's **$0.009296 / ≈247 s** extrapolation from one page. **Honest limits:** no page in this run reported an `uncertain` span, so **the remapping and clamping path has still never processed a live one** — the 18 unit tests are the only thing holding it and **S-03**'s deliberately bad photos are the run that should produce some; the joined photographed transcript was **not** sent to the structure step (**C-08**'s end-to-end half); and `assembleDocument` has **no caller in the app yet** — **C-02** builds the screen that calls it.
- [x] **C-12** (new, from P-14) Bound what a pasted document may be, and say so plainly when it is too long. _Deps:_ P-14. _Done when:_ one named limit in code, reached from the paste screen's own copy; a document over it is refused **before** a request is built, with a sentence that offers the smaller piece rather than an error code; unit test for the boundary and e2e for the screen. _Found by P-14 (2026-11-04):_ `MAX_PAGES = 5` (`lib/session/reducer.ts:16`) bounds **photographs** only — `features/paste/PasteView.tsx:54` checks `text.trim().length === 0` and nothing else, so the paste path has no input cap at all. ADR-24 put a ceiling on the *reply* (`max_tokens: 16384`) which made the request side the only unbounded half: a paste longer than the plan model's window (Qwen 262,144 tokens, SPIKE §4) is billed for its input and then refused by the service, and the person meets a transport-shaped error for their own document's length. The cap number is a decision with a UX half — long pastes are a real use case PRD leaves open, so the honest fix may be "split at paragraph boundaries and run per piece", which is C-11's assembly machinery rather than a smaller number. _Evidence:_ **one named limit, and the screen says it in the same number the guard uses.** `MAX_PASTE_CHARS = 20_000` and `pasteLengthProblem()` are in `src/lib/session/reducer.ts` directly beside `MAX_PAGES` — the photograph half and the text half of the same product limit, which is where a reader looking for one will find the other. The guard is the **first statement** of the text-run callback in `src/app/_flow/useDocumentRun.ts`, before the live-mode refusal, before `beginRun()` and before a body is assembled, in **every** mode; `features/paste/PasteView.tsx` imports the constant into its own hint (`…reads up to 20,000 characters at a time, so paste the part you need answered`), so the copy and the guard cannot drift. `paste_too_long` (`lib/errors.ts`) is `retryable: false` and its sentence offers the smaller piece without naming a number, a code, "token" or "character" — asserted by a test that matches the message against `/\d|paste_too_long|token|character/i` and requires **no** match. The number itself is derived, not picked: the constant's comment works it from the measured reading (**6,007 characters → 118.6 s → $0.0044**, SPIKE §4) rather than from Qwen's 262,144-token window, which this cap uses only to show the window is *not* the binding constraint (≈5,000 tokens, ≈2% of it), and characters were chosen over tokens because a token count needs a tokenizer dependency AGENTS.md §5 does not allow without a reason the guard does not need. **ADR-29** records it with the four rejected alternatives (tokenizer / let the service refuse / split-and-run-per-piece / cap in the prompt or transport), and the reason the service-refusal alternative is real: the relay's `MAX_BODY_BYTES` is 8 MiB, so it catches a photograph and lets a two-million-character letter through, billed as input in Mode A before anything comes back. Tests: **5 under "the paste cap (C-12, ADR-29)" in `tests/unit/session-reducer.test.ts`** — the exact boundary (20,000 fits, 20,001 is refused with this code), trailing whitespace counts because the guard measures what would be sent, the message carries no number, and it **returns** a problem rather than throwing — and **4 in `tests/e2e/paste-cap.spec.ts`** (new file): the hint states the cap in the browser's own text; an over-cap paste is refused with the catalogue's sentence verbatim, focus lands on the field's error, the URL stays `/` and the button is usable again; the refused document leaves **nothing behind** (navigated by the nav link, not by URL — a page load would lose the memory-only session and the assertion would pass for the wrong reason); and the same paste **one character under** the cap is not refused for its length and reaches the pipeline, so the guard is shown to be a guard rather than a blanket block. That last case is why the pair exists: a mutation check moved the guard *below* the run's first announcement and the suite stayed green — `announce()` is React state and both writes land in one batch, so the live region never holds the intermediate sentence and the guard's order is **not observable from it**; the order is therefore held by the composition root's shape and by ADR-29's text, and the spec says so rather than asserting it. `tests/unit/errors.test.ts` now covers **22** codes (the catalogue is asserted complete by length, so a code without a row fails). Gate: `npx vitest run` **544 passed | 7 todo (26 files)**, `npx playwright test` **94 passed** (was 90 before this file's 4; the whole suite, not just the new spec), `npx tsc --noEmit` 0, `npx eslint .` 0, `npm run roadmap:check` 0. **Honest limits:** the cap is a **wait-time product limit with a measured floor, not a measured safe number** — no document of this length has ever been run here, the 6,007→118.6 s figure is one sample, and input does not scale like output, so **S-03** owns timing a long paste; the guard bounds the person's wait, **not the bill** (20,000 characters of dense tables is more tokens than 20,000 characters of prose); "refused before a request exists" is proven by the guard's position in the composition root and by the nothing-left-behind case, **not** by counting outbound bytes in a live mode, because no live mode runs a paste today (**B-12**, F-05); and the alternative this item was opened with — split a long paste at paragraph boundaries and run each piece — is still open, and it is C-11's machinery plus a screen that says which piece the reading covers. Docs corrected in this commit: ARCH §5.2's ordering rules, §13's new row and an "As built (C-12)" note, **ADR-29**; ESSENTIALS pipeline steps 1 and 10 and its decision list; PROJECT_STRUCTURE's `_flow`/`errors`/`session`/`paste` lines, the unit-test list and a new e2e line; README's "What that runs today"; **B-14 closed as a decided policy** (AGENTS.md §5 puts user-facing copy on the human's side — the standing instruction to use these agents' recommendations for design decisions covers it, and the sentence is one `ErrorCode` row away from being changed by a human).
- [x] **C-13** (new, from S-11) Let Demo mode finish a photographed document, and make it recognise a photograph rather than assume one. _Deps:_ S-11, C-02, C-03, C-11. _Done when:_ `npm run dev:fixtures` with no `.env` takes three shipped photographs through the page loop, the join, the reading, the results screen and two answers, in a browser; any other photograph is refused; and the matching rule cannot answer a person's own letter with the demo's reading. _Why:_ **C-02**'s run had its post-loop branches — the hole announcement, the label findings, `usable: false`, the results screen — written but unreachable from a browser, because Mode F threw for an image body before a fixture was looked up. _Evidence:_ **`tests/e2e/photo-fixtures.spec.ts` (7 tests, Chromium, no key; the seventh, the two-of-three subset refusal, landed after this item closed)** — the three JPEGs this repo ships become three pages, one 3,557-character join, the recorded reading in the fixed section order, both prepared questions (one answered, one "the document does not answer this"), and a receipt naming the fixture's own identity with the demo-data claim; **and the two refusals**: two of the three photographs is a different document and is refused, and one changed byte at the end of a demo photograph is a different photograph and is refused. The matching rule is **ADR-31**: `lib/fixtures/page_key.ts` digests the **file as chosen** (`tests/unit/page_key.test.ts`, 5 tests), `Page.fixtureKey` rides through `read_pages.ts` → `transcribePage` → `CallInit.fixtureKey` as **local metadata** — never in a body, never logged, asserted against the wire in both network modes by `tests/unit/near-client.test.ts` and against the recorded reply by `tests/unit/transcribe.test.ts` and `tests/unit/read-pages.test.ts` (2 new each) — and `replay.ts` answers only on an exact digest match, keeping the envelope's identity as `fixture-<name>`. The chain runs upward: the reading is matched by the exact joined transcript, so a subset cannot be summarised. **A new `ErrorCode`, `no_fixture_for_page`**, because `no_fixture_for_input`'s sentence talks about *text* and a person who has just photographed a letter must not be told about words they never typed (`tests/unit/errors.test.ts`, 23 codes, catalogue asserted complete). The corpus is self-policing: **`tests/unit/recorded-fixtures.test.ts` (14 tests)** re-hashes the shipped JPEGs, re-derives the join with `assembleDocument`, re-parses every reply against the live schema and pins the measured `rotated` notices, so a re-recording fails until it has been *read*; `tests/unit/fixtures.test.ts` (26) holds what the replay client does with them, in both directions. Gate: `npx vitest run` **587 passed | 7 todo (29 files)**, `npx playwright test` **119 passed** (was 113), `npx tsc --noEmit` 0, `npx eslint .` 0. **Honest limits, all of them load-bearing:** *the recording proves the flow, not the facts* — the 86 facts are grounded in the model's own transcript, which is consistency and never page-correctness (rule 8), and **S-03/C-10** own accuracy; *the case is `offered: false`* so nobody can paste a page of line items and credit the photo path; *`e2ee: false` on every sidecar* — **B-12** has not earned the stronger rung and this run does not pretend to; *live modes still refuse at the door*, so a person's own photograph is answered nowhere but Demo mode, and **C-04**'s bad-photograph shapes (a truncation, an `uncertain` span, a refusal) have no fixture yet; *the refusal's wording is a review item* — the subset case lands on `no_fixture_for_input`, whose sentence is written for text, and **C-06** owes that pair of messages their final plain-language pass. Docs corrected in this commit: ARCH §5.1, §6's `fixtures` row, §7's `ErrorCode` list, §13's two rows, §14's worklist row 11, **ADR-23**'s Mode F tail and **ADR-31** added; ESSENTIALS' mode table, pipeline step 5, its unknowns row 11 and its decision list; README's "What that runs today", "What is verified", "Accuracy" and "Cost per document"; `fixtures/README.md` and `fixtures/responses/README.md` rewritten (including a correction this commit found in itself: the stored `envelope` is what our transport schema parsed, not a packet capture, and `"usage": {}` means *nothing to record*, not *nothing billed*); `public/sample-docs/README.md`; `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`; `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4's S-11 block, §4's C-11 amendment and "What is still unknown" items 3 and 8.

### Phase T — Trust and verification (2026-11-07 → 11-09)

- [~] **T-01** `near-ai/attestation.ts`: fresh nonce per session (32 random bytes as 64 hex), fetch report, short-lived cache, call `/api/verify`. _Deps:_ S-04. _Note:_ `GET /v1/attestation/report` requires an API key, so in Mode A the browser calls it with the user's own key (ADR-17). _2026-11-02 Qoder agent (scaffold F-14):_ `newNonce()` is implemented (32 `crypto.getRandomValues` bytes → 64 lowercase hex) and tested in `tests/unit/nonce.test.ts` (6 tests, incl. a CSPRNG stub proving the bytes come from `getRandomValues` and not `Math.random`, and arity 0 so a nonce can never be seeded from the request). `fetchReport`/`verifyWithServer` are typed stubs that throw `NotBuilt('T-01')`; ARCH §10's check set and ADR-17 still stand. _2026-11-03 Qoder agent:_ **built, and capped by what has never been measured.** `fetchReport(client, …)` asks `GET /v1/attestation/report` through the typed client with our nonce and `signing_algo` (asserted by name: "the report is asked for with our nonce in the query, and only through the typed client" — the model report path is the one asked for, because a gateway report says nothing about the enclave serving the model). `bindReport` decides four checks in the browser before anything document-derived moves: `attestation_report`, `nonce_fresh` (case-insensitive echo of our own nonce; **a report carrying no nonce is a fail, not a maybe** — nothing then binds it to this session rather than last week's), `signing_address_shape` (20 hex bytes ECDSA / 32 Ed25519) and `signing_public_key_shape` (the 32-byte key E2EE would encrypt to, which is why its absence is what keeps the strong rung out of reach). `verifyWithServer` POSTs report + nonce to `/api/verify` and reads a 501, an unreachable route or an unparseable body as a **failed** `server_verification` — 4 tests under "a verifier that says nothing is a failure, not an agreement" (§4 rule 6). `createTrustSession({ client })` composes the gate with T-02's verifier around **one session nonce** (rule 4) and a 60 s cache, and puts the key in `GateOutcome.signingKey` as a structured field rather than a check's `detail`, because `detail` is read aloud on the receipt. Status: `failed` when a required check (`attestation_report`, `nonce_fresh`, `server_verification`) fails, `verified` only when the local binding **and** the server verdict both say so, `partial` otherwise (rule 5). _Evidence:_ `tests/verification/attestation.test.ts` **33 passed | 6 todo**; `npx vitest run` 307 passed | 6 todo; `npx tsc --noEmit` 0; `npx eslint .` 0; `npx playwright test` 33 passed; ARCH §10 "As built" added. **Why `[~]` and not `[x]`, after the first live report (2026-11-03):** reason (1) above is now **closed by measurement, not by relaxation** — `attestationReportSchema` and `modelAttestationSchema` accepted the real report **unchanged** on the first try (the loose-schema/fail-closed-checks choice earning its keep), the inner field names are exactly the documented ones, `report_data` is confirmed absent from the model attestation and present on the gateway one so `bindReport` checks the right field, and **`bindReport` passed 4/4 against it with the service echoing our own 64-hex nonce** (`docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §3). That is the first time `status: 'verified'` has been reachable from the client side rather than argued about. What still keeps the item open is one leg and it is known, not vague: **(2) T-03's `/api/verify` still answers 501**, `createTrustSession` ANDs the local binding with the server verdict, and a silent verifier is a failure rather than an agreement (§4 rule 6) — so a live run today still lands `failed` and refuses to send. Checks 3–5 of ARCH §10's set (NRAS verdict, TDX quote, measurement replay) are the heavy half, the 5,006-byte `intel_quote` is now physically in hand, and what blocks them is a **dependency decision**, recorded as **B-12**. Tampered/expired report fixtures are T-08's, landing with S-11. _2026-11-05 Qoder agent (**T-16**, **ADR-32**):_ the file now decides **twelve** checks in the browser, not four: `bindReport` keeps its four (so T-08's recorded "four local passes" evidence stays literally true) and the gate appends `checkQuote`'s eight from `src/lib/near-ai/quote.ts`, then the four named gaps (`quote_signed_by_hardware`, `platform_still_trusted`, `gpu_evidence_verdict`, `report_expiry`), then `server_verification`. An un-run check **counts against the status** — `verified` needs every binding check, gaps included, to have `ran: true` and `passed: true` — so the four gaps keep the strong rung out of reach until someone answers them, and each retires *by name* when T-03 ships it rather than by anyone lowering a bar. `td_not_debug` joined the required-failure set: a quote whose `td_attributes` bit 0 says debug mode now stops a run on its own (§4 rule 6 tightened, never loosened). The report schema was widened to keep `compose_hash`, `device_id` and `tcb_info`'s registers, which is what lets the quote's bytes be compared against the JSON; `app_cert`, `instance_id`, `mr_aggregated`, `key_provider_info`, `vm_config` and `event_log` stay stripped and a test pins that. _2026-11-05 later (**ADR-33**):_ two lines in this file's composition changed, both toward failing closed rather than away from it. `verifyWithServer` now posts `signing_algo` with the report and reads a missing `status` as **`partial`**, where it used to fall back to "`verified` if every check you sent me passed" — with T-03 answering in one named check that inference had become a bug, and an unstated verdict is not the client's to guess (§4 rule 5). And `createTrustSession` adds a `noQuote` term to `requiredFailed`: a report whose quote cannot be read halts **because a binding check did not run**, not because the verifier happened to be silent, which is the case the 501 was previously covering for it. Against the answering route, a good live report now composes to `partial` (12 passes + 4 named gaps + `server_verification`), so `blockedCopy`'s `NotBuilt('T-06')` is closer than it was; `verified` remains unreachable, still by the same four names as before.
- [~] **T-02** `near-ai/signatures.ts`: fetch + verify response signatures with `@noble/curves`; fixed test vectors. _Deps:_ S-05. _Shape fixed by ADR-19:_ the signature is a **separate** `GET /v1/signature/{chat_id}`, not part of the completion; rebuild the signed text per `signature_kind` (`provider_tee` → `{model_id}:{request_hash}:{response_hash}`, `gateway` → `{request_hash}:{response_hash}`), keep the exact wire bytes (streaming payloads end in two newlines that belong in the hash), retry on `Chat id not found or expired`, and remember bytes are only retained 60 minutes. _2026-11-03 Qoder agent:_ **built, with vectors that were not borrowed from the implementation.** `fetchSignature(client, chatId, { model, algo })` is the typed GET (F-03's single GET-404 retry *is* ADR-19's "Chat id not found or expired" handling — that is why the client owns it, not this file), zod-parsed, and an unparseable body halts with `invalid_model_output`. `checkSignature` rebuilds both candidate texts from the **exact bytes it was handed** (SHA-256 over UTF-8), tries only the stated `signature_kind` or both when the record omits it, then Ed25519-detached or EIP-191 personal-sign per `signing_algo`, and returns `verified` plus the origin it actually established. `createSignatureVerifier` reports two named checks — `response_signature` (signature valid **and** the served `text` agrees with our own hashes; the tests show each half can fail alone) and `signed_by_model_enclave` (`provider_tee` only), so a gateway signature is labelled as the gateway's and never as the model enclave's. Fail-closed in every direction tried: no attested key, a missing `sentBody`, a non-hex or wrong-length signature, an unrecognised encoding, and a key this session did not attest to all return `verified: false`; and verification is **always** against the key the gate bound, because the `signing_address` a response carries about itself is data with no authority (test: "the address the record claims is data"). The trailing newlines are proven load-bearing rather than assumed: the identical signature verifies over the bytes as received and fails over those bytes trimmed, with the two digests asserted different. _Evidence:_ 12 tests in the "signed text is rebuilt from the exact wire bytes" block. The ECDSA vector is signed by a hand-written scalar path (`signEcdsaOverDigest`) **because `@noble/curves` 2.4 hashes inside `sign()`** and so cannot produce a signature over an already-computed keccak digest — which is exactly what the service's ECDSA signatures are; same curve parameters, different code path from the recovery the verifier uses, and the passing assertion is what proves the signer (a wrong group order or a broken `modInverse` shows up as a rejected vector, and it did earn its keep: the first draft of that constant was mangled). **Why `[~]`:** the key arrived and the live case has now been run **through this module** (2026-11-03, S-05: `fetchSignature` + `checkSignature` over a real 200 completion, `provider_tee`/ed25519, `verified: true` against the key the gate bound — `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §5), which settles the shape and the key-selection rule. **The measurement changed one line of this file's behaviour and it is written down rather than left implied:** a `gateway`-kind record is verified by `gateway_attestation.signing_address`, not by the model key, and this module verifies against the key the **gate** bound (the model's), so a gateway signature can only ever come back `verified: false` here. That is deliberate while ADR-22 keeps the app non-streamed — a non-streamed answer from an attested model *is* `provider_tee` — and if a fallback provider that only ever signs at the gateway is ever allowed through, the gate must bind that key too and say which machine it chose. Two things still owed: the live exchange is not yet a checked-in vector under `fixtures/` (S-11 → T-08) and T-15's cross-check against the official SDK has never run; and no **document-derived** response has been signed by this build, because the gate cannot yet reach `verified` (T-03, **B-12**).
- [~] **T-03** `/api/verify`: heavy checks to the scope decided in S-13(d); receives no user content. _Done when:_ returns named `CheckResult[]` with `ran`/`passed`. _2026-11-02 Qoder agent (scaffold F-14):_ `POST /api/verify` answers 501 with `item: 'T-03'` and no GET exists (verification consumes a report, it does not serve one) — `tests/relay/relay.test.ts`. A 501 must be read as failure, not silence (AGENTS.md §4 rule 6); that assertion is in `tests/verification/attestation.test.ts` as a todo until the handler exists. _2026-11-03 Qoder agent (T-01):_ **that todo is now a real test, twice over** — "a 501 from /api/verify is read as a failed check" asserts `server_verification ran:true passed:false` and `status:'failed'`, and two siblings do the same for an unreachable route and a 200 whose body will not parse. The consumer side of the contract is therefore finished and **this route's 501 is currently what makes every live-mode gate read `failed`** — which is the right outcome, not a bug to smooth over. What remains is the check set itself (S-13(d) scope): quote verification, event-log replay, the NRAS verdict. _2026-11-03: the data arrived, so the blocker is now a decision and not an absence._ The first real report is in `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §3 with the quote physically in hand (`intel_quote` = 10,012 hex = **5,006 bytes**, `nvidia_payload` = a 98,222-character **JSON string** (8 per-GPU evidence entries, each with a base64 PEM chain, each echoing our nonce — the 49,111-byte figure here was this repo misreading a character count as hex; corrected in SPIKE §3 on 2026-11-05), `event_log` an array of 30–32 objects on the model attestation and a 4,927-character string on the gateway one — an asymmetry a check must not assume away). `@noble/curves` + `@noble/hashes` cover the signature half; the TDX quote and NRAS verdict half is what `nearai/nearai-cloud-verifier`'s `ts/` delegates to **`dcap-qvl-node`**, which is not a dependency of this repo and cannot be added by an agent (AGENTS.md §5) → **B-12**. Relaxing the gate to read a 501 as `not_run` instead of `failed` would make live mode work and weaken the claim at the same time; that trade is explicitly not ours to make silently. _2026-11-05 Qoder agent (**T-16**, **ADR-32**):_ **what this route is owed got much smaller, and the 501 still stops everything.** Eight of the quote's checks turned out to need only its bytes and SHA-384, so they now run in the browser inside `createTrustSession` rather than waiting for this handler (**T-16**, 8/8 on two live reports). What `POST /api/verify` is still owed is `quote_signed_by_hardware` and `platform_still_trusted` (Intel's signature and collateral — genuinely `dcap-qvl-node`), `report_expiry` (a clock plus the signed validity windows), and `gpu_evidence_verdict` — **which no longer needs a dependency at all**, only a server allowed to reach NRAS; it measured HTTP 200 with verdict `true` from a script, and shipping it here is a decision about a second upstream host (§4 rule 9), not about `package.json`. The four are emitted today as `QUOTE_CHECKS_THAT_CANNOT_RUN` with `ran:false` and they count against the status, so this route's 501 keeps live mode at `failed` *and* the receipt keeps saying why. _2026-11-05 later, the same day, and it is **this** sentence the next note corrects (**ADR-33**):_ **the route answers.** `src/app/api/verify/route.ts` is a real handler and `src/lib/server/verify.ts` is its decision code: it takes `{report, nonce, signing_algo}`, re-runs the **twelve** dependency-free checks on the bytes it was handed — `bindReport`'s four plus `checkQuote`'s eight — against **the nonce in the request** rather than one it chose, and answers with exactly **one** named `CheckResult`, `server_verification`, so a receipt can never double-book a check the page already spoke. Its `status` is `partial` or `failed` and **no path through this file emits `verified`**, because the four heavy answers are not in it and ADR-32 already made an un-run check hold the strong rung back. Its detail says what it is worth in the line a screen reader speaks — *"a second opinion on the same arithmetic, not new proof about the machine"* — plus how much it could read, so a quote-less report answers `ran 4 of the report's own 12 checks` instead of "agreed with all of them". Refusals all happen before any verification: `400` on a body that is not JSON, a schema-rejected report, or a nonce that is not 64 hex; `413` over a **4 MB** cap (the live report measured 318,885 B, so it is 12× headroom, not a guessed number), checked on the length before the parse; no GET; and an error echoes nothing from the report. **No upstream call, no key, no document content** — which is also why B-12's NRAS half is untouched by this and still a human answer (§4 rule 9). Two client halves moved with it: `verifyWithServer` now sends `signing_algo` and **no longer infers `verified` from a body that states no verdict** (an inference ADR-33 closes structurally), and `createTrustSession` halts a report whose quote cannot be read **by rule** — `noQuote` joined the required-failure set, so it is no longer the *absence* of a verifier doing that job. **What changes for the product is one sentence: a live report composes to `partial` and a run may proceed — the first time this gate has not been what stops a live mode.** Why the item still sits at `[~]`: the four unanswered names are **B-12's decisions, not unfinished code here**, and §4 rule 5 forbids reading their absence as silence. _Evidence:_ `tests/verification/verify_route.test.ts` **27 tests** (five verdict exchanges on the recorded live bytes — good → `partial` with the word "verified" absent from the body; a foreign nonce → names `nonce_fresh, quote_binds_our_nonce`; one byte flipped at `quote[48+520+31]` → names `quote_binds_attested_key`; `{model_attestations: []}` → names `attestation_report`; a blanked quote → the coverage line — plus the three `400`s, the `413`, the **cap measured in bytes rather than characters** (a two-byte alphabet that fits under 4 MB as a string is refused, and the guard fails its own test when written as `raw.length`), no GET, nothing echoed, and four gate compositions that go through the **real handler** rather than a double: good → `partial` with 13 unique names and the 4 gaps `ran:false`, a replayed report → `failed`, a quote-less report → `failed` with `server_verification` *passing* so the halt is attributable, an answer with no `status` → `partial`, a 501 → `failed`); `npm test` → **661 passed \| 7 todo (32 files)**; `npx tsc --noEmit` **0**; `npx eslint .` **0**; and the same five exchanges measured over real HTTP against `next dev` (350 B / 1,714 ms cold, 79 ms warm for the good one), in `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §3's T-03 block. Docs corrected in the same commit: **ADR-33**, ARCH §10's status block, ESSENTIALS' `/api/verify` bullet, README's three check lines and its three-reasons paragraph, PROJECT_STRUCTURE, and this file's §1/§2/§8.

It is not — eight of the checks are byte reading and arithmetic, and the two hashes `@noble/hashes`/`@noble/curves` are already in `package.json`. Shipping them changes what the blocker is *about*, so it needed an ID. _Done when:_ the checks run inside `createTrustSession`, each is a named `CheckResult`, a one-byte change to the quote trips exactly one of them, the unrunnable ones are listed rather than implied, and no new dependency is added. _Evidence:_ **2026-11-05 · Qoder agent.** `src/lib/near-ai/quote.ts` (new, no new dependency): `readQuote` (48-byte header + 584-byte `TDReport10`, layout transcribed from `Phala-Network/dcap-qvl`'s `src/quote.rs` — the struct NEAR's own verifier decodes through `@phala/dcap-qvl-node` — returning a *reason* rather than throwing), `replayEventLog`, `checkQuote` (eight checks) and `QUOTE_CHECKS_THAT_CANNOT_RUN` (four). `createTrustSession.run` composes them: `checks = [...local, ...quote, ...gaps, ...server]`, `td_not_debug` joins the required-failure set (a debug TD halts the run — §4 rule 6 tightened, not relaxed), and the `binding` list that must be all-passed for `verified` **includes the gaps**, so an unrun check cannot be read as an absence; each one retires by name the day `/api/verify` answers it, which is what makes the live path closeable without re-arguing this code. `bindReport` was left at its four checks so T-08's recorded evidence still reads true. **Measured on live service bytes, not only on the recording:** `npm run spike -- --stage=attestation` → **8/8** on `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` (HTTP 200, 1,697 ms, 308,101-byte report, 5,006-byte quote, 35 boot events) and **8/8** on `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B` (2,379 ms, 162,852 B, 30 events), with the nonce the script minted seconds earlier sitting in `report_data[32..64]` — so the service re-attests rather than editing a field, and `livingClient` in T-08's tests now models that. **Two corrections this produced, both recorded in SPIKE §3:** the documented **`rt_mr3` replay does not reproduce** (registers 0–2 replay exactly; RTMR3 matches nothing, because dstack logs those boot events with an empty `digest`, and eighteen framings of `event`/`event_payload` all failed on both recordings) — hence the check is named `event_log_replays_rtmr0_to_2` and its receipt line says register 3 was left out; and the production schema had to **widen**, because `info.tcb_info`'s measurements and `info.compose_hash` were being *stripped* before any check could compare them (eight failing tests were that finding, not a test bug). **The GPU half of B-12 turned out to be free too:** `npm run spike -- --stage=gpu` posts `JSON.parse(nvidia_payload)` to NRAS and got **HTTP 200 in 1,608 ms, `x-nvidia-overall-att-result: true`**, with the payload's own `nonce` matching ours — no dependency needed, only a server allowed to reach a second host, which is a decision (§4 rule 9). It therefore still ships as `gpu_evidence_verdict: ran:false`. Docs: **ADR-32**, ARCH §10 (minimum set items 1/3/4/5 and the as-built block), ESSENTIALS "Verification scope", SPIKE §3 (third measured block) + its unknowns list, `verifier/README.md`'s port order, PROJECT_STRUCTURE, README (T-14). Gate: `npx tsc --noEmit` 0, `npx eslint .` 0, `npm test` **636 passed \| 7 todo** in 31 files, `npm run test:e2e` **119 passed**. **No `verified` rung, no privacy sentence and no dependency moved.**

### Phase A — Accessibility and hardening (2026-11-09 → 11-11)

- [x] **A-09** Relay tests: rejected path, oversized body, rate limit, no content in logs. _2026-11-05:_ **71 tests** across `tests/relay/allowlist.test.ts` (35, the pure rules) and `tests/relay/relay.test.ts` (36, through the real handlers with an injected `fetch`). Rejected path: ten cases each asserting the fetch double was never called **and** that the refused path was not echoed back. Oversized body: the exact byte boundary either side of `MAX_BODY_BYTES`, plus a multi-byte-character case that proves the cap is counted in bytes and not in characters, and a 700 KB body passing so the cap is shown to be a guard rather than a wall. Rate limit: 429 with `retry-after`, per-client buckets not sharing a verdict, no bucket string containing an address, and one store unit consumed per relayed call. No content in logs: console spies across a full forwarded exchange, over every refusal body, and over a 502. Then the yes-branch, because a suite of refusals would only prove the relay is broken: the exact bytes forwarded (a whitespace-sensitive body), the header set being **exactly** `accept`/`authorization`/`content-type`, status/content-type/bytes passthrough, upstream's 429+`retry-after` passed through, the report and signature URLs forwarded with their queries intact, the encoded model segment surviving, no POST on a GET-only path, and rule 3 refusing `proxied_upstream` and an unreadable catalog at the point the document would leave. `Evidence:` `npx vitest run tests/relay` → **71 passed**; `npm test` → **729 passed | 7 todo** in 33 files; the three load-bearing guards mutation-checked (deleting `reserved: ['list']`, re-serialising the forwarded body, removing the TEE branch — each fails a named test, and `SPIKE §10` shows the same handlers answering the live service).

- [~] **A-15** `settings/page`: mode, API key (Mode A), read-aloud rate, high contrast. _Owed by P-10:_ anything stored here starts with `SESSION_STORAGE_PREFIX` (`sightline.`) if it is a `sessionStorage` value — that prefix is what makes Forget able to clear it (ARCH §5.4). _Owed by P-09:_ the rate control writes the **same blob the app already reads** — `localStorage['sightline.settings']`, key `speechRate`, 0.5–2 (the bounds, the default and the validator are in `src/lib/settings.ts`; add the writer there rather than in the component, and do not write a second schema). The read is per press, so the next `Read the answer aloud` uses a new rate with no reload, and a value that fails validation falls back to normal rather than reporting an error. Nothing document-derived may join that blob (AGENTS.md §4 rule 7), and a rate is one of the few values that legitimately survives Forget, because it is not a document. _2026-11-03 Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`):_ **the two controls that are about the app rather than about a credential or a privacy claim are built, evidenced and shipped; the other two fields are deliberately not, and that half is a human decision (B-10), not unfinished code.** Built: `src/lib/settings.ts` is now the only reader *and* writer of `sightline.settings` — a schema where **both** fields carry a default (so a P-09 blob never breaks the theme and an A-15 blob never breaks the speed), `updateSettings()` as a read-modify-write that re-validates the whole result before storing, `applyHighContrast`, `describeRate`, and a `useSyncExternalStore` subscription whose snapshot is the raw string (a parsed object would look like a change on every render and loop). `_flow/SettingsControls.tsx` gives four named speeds in a native radio group plus one contrast checkbox; `_flow/HighContrastTheme.tsx` is mounted in `layout.tsx` because the theme belongs to the app, not the page it was chosen on; `settings/page.tsx` keeps the mode readout. Four rules worth keeping: **a write that fails validation throws** (a bad read costs one default speed, a bad write poisons every later read); **a write the browser refuses is announced, not swallowed** — `setItem` genuinely throws in some private modes, so `updateSettings` returns `saved: false`, the controls are driven by the store and snap back, and the sentence says the setting stayed as it was (the same reasoning as `lib/session/forget.ts` counting what it could not remove); **the speed is not announced after a change** because the reader says the newly selected radio itself, while **the theme is**, because a colour change is otherwise silent (AGENTS.md §8); **an off-list stored speed (1.8, which P-09's own tests write) is named by its number with nothing selected** rather than rounded to the nearest choice. _Evidence:_ `npx vitest run` **401 passed | 6 todo** in 19 files (was 382 | 6 in 18: **+19** in `tests/unit/settings-writer.test.ts` — the merge rule, the throwing write, the refused write reported as unsaved, the round trip into `speechRate()`, and the four offered speeds checked to lie inside 0.5–2 and to name the default), `npx playwright test` **69 passed** = 12 shell + 57 flow from `--list` (was 58: **+11** in "Settings a person can change", including the loop that proves the whole point — choose Fast in Settings, press `Read the answer aloud`, every utterance the recorder captured carries `rate: 1.5`, client-side navigation, no reload — plus arrow-key selection, the theme surviving navigation *and* a real reload, Forget leaving both values intact, the 44 px measured on the **rows** rather than the 20 px native inputs, and axe over `/settings` in **both** themes with 0 serious or critical), `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors, `npx next build` exit 0 with the same six routes, and the production HTML of `/settings` inspected (`next start`, curl) to confirm the server render carries the defaults and no theme attribute — i.e. the documented one-frame delay before the client re-reads storage, which the reload test now proves settles with `expect.poll`. _Open halves, both needing a human (B-10):_ **(a)** a mode switcher — rejected here as ADR-21 because the mode selects the privacy sentence shown beside a result (AGENTS.md §4 rule 1) and `lib/config.ts` already decides it from the environment, failing closed; a second copy in a browser would be a claim nobody recomputes; **(b)** the API key field — item **T-13**, conditional on the S-06 CORS measurement (B-03), with no running mode to use a key in (F-04, F-05, B-05). A consequence of shipping the page: `lib/errors.ts` had two messages ending "…add your own key in Settings", which pointed a reader at a control that does not exist, so `rate_limited` and `relay_disabled` were rewritten without that advice and their old comment about "Settings is a placeholder page" was corrected — **T-13 puts the sentence back with the field.** `docs/PRD.md` §7's "one extra high-contrast theme at most" is honoured: one theme, one media query, no picker.
- [ ] **A-16** (found by P-05, 2026-11-03) Decide the screen-reader behaviour of Next.js's own route announcer. `app-page.runtime` injects `div[role=alert][aria-live=assertive]` in a `<next-route-announcer>` shadow host on the first client navigation and fills it with `document.title`, falling back to the first `<h1>` when the title is empty. Measured on `next build` + `next start`: silent across the flow, because every route shares the title `Sightline`. Measured on `next dev`: it speaks the new `<h1>`, so while developing a reader hears the route name *and* our sentence. _Done when:_ A-03's listener confirms it on a production build, and the "one title for every route" rule is either recorded in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §17 as an ADR or replaced by a decision to suppress the region. Until then: **no route may get its own `metadata.title`.** `tests/e2e/flow.spec.ts` ("the app speaks through one region…") guards the invariant, and `docs/ACCESSIBILITY_REPORT.md` states both measurements.
- [x] **A-17** (found by the design pass, 2026-11-05; **ADR-37**) One visual system across the four screens, with accessibility outranking style. _Done when:_ the header, the demo-data warning and the content share one measure and both edges at 1440/768/390; no heading renders below the body size it heads; the primary action of a form is visibly the primary action in **all three palettes** and every colour pair the stylesheet uses clears 4.5:1; a hovered control answers, and `prefers-reduced-motion` takes the movement away without taking the answer away; every interactive thing in the header is a 44 px target; and axe stays clean at phone width in every palette. _Evidence:_ **`src/app/globals.css` rewritten as one system, with no component changed** — tokens for the measure (`--measure: 44rem`), a four-step rhythm, a five-step type scale, `--accent-hover`/`--panel-hover`/`--on-accent`/`--motion`; `color-scheme` declared per palette; **one button pair chosen by native semantics rather than by a class** (`button[type='submit']` *is* the primary action of the form it belongs to — `Read this text`, `Read these photographs`, `Ask` — so `components/Button.tsx` gained no variant prop and no component can drift away from the pair); `nav`/`.banner`/`main` on one measure; `h2`/`h3`/`h4` sized (an unstyled `h4` is 1rem against a 1.125rem body, so the receipt's own headings used to sit *below* the sentences they introduced); `.fact-list` single-column at phone width with full-width actions; `<details>` framed but keeping its native marker; hover = colour step + 2 px on a 120 ms transition, press = back down, both under the existing reduced-motion block; `prefers-contrast: more` now strengthens `--border`, `--muted` **and** `--panel` instead of only the first. **19 new tests in `tests/e2e/design.spec.ts`**: edges measured on the elements at three widths; every `nav a` height; heading sizes against body on a completed `/read`; the button pair and four text pairs (body, nav link, muted, the warning on its panel) plus the disabled label, with **contrast computed from the computed styles of the elements themselves** in light, dark and high-contrast; hover fill change + lift, then the same hover under `reducedMotion: reduce`; the native controls' `color-scheme`; the stacked fact list at 390; and **12 axe sweeps** — `/`, `/read` (after a real run), `/trust`, `/settings` at 390 px in each palette. `npm run test:e2e` → **139 passed, 2 skipped** (was 120 passed + 2 skipped); `npm test` → **753 passed | 7 todo**, unchanged, because no behaviour code moved; `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `npx eslint .` 0 errors. **Six mutations, each failing a named test:** `nav, .banner { max-width: none }` → the edges test; `h4 { font-size: 1rem }` → the heading-scale test; deleting the `button[type='submit']` block → the pair; deleting the reduced-motion `transform: none` → all three palette hover tests; deleting `color-scheme: light` on the native controls → the dark **and** high-contrast cases (light correctly still passes); deleting the phone-width `.fact-list` rule → the stacked-list test. **Two findings, recorded rather than smoothed over.** (1) The stylesheet and the markup had drifted: **eleven class names** (`paste-hint`, `capture-hint`, `question-hint`, `capture-empty`, `mode-downgraded`, `record-count`, `fact-value`, `fact-flagged`, `answer-flagged`, `answer-unsourced`, `fixtures-banner`) were written in components and styled by nothing — every one of them also carries `className="muted"`, so they rendered as inherited grey text and the names read as though they did something. They are rules now. (2) `color-scheme: dark` is what makes a native file picker's button and the scrollbars match a dark page, and it also makes the browser draw an **unselected radio as a dark disc on a near-black page** — under the 3:1 WCAG 1.4.11 asks of a control's indicator, in the theme whose entire job is contrast. Fixed by asking for the light rendering of the widget itself on those two controls, which is the only way to get it without replacing a native control (AGENTS.md §8), and asserted so it cannot quietly come back. _Not claimed:_ no screen reader, no phone, no 200% zoom (**A-03**/**A-04**/**A-08** and **B-01** are unmoved by this), Chromium only because that is the only engine this repo can launch, and the pass touched **no** privacy sentence, heading, section order, live region or `document.title` (AGENTS.md §4 rule 1, §8, A-16). One thing found and deliberately left: `/trust` renders `<h1>Verification</h1>` and the receipt's own `<h2>Verification</h2>`, so a reader hears the same word twice at the top of that page — changing it is a wording decision inside `TrustReceipt`, and A-03 should make it with a reader running. **Stability was part of this item, because the second full run failed two tests the first had passed.** Both causes were in the measurement, not in the stylesheet: the twelve phone-width axe sweeps were one test sharing a single 30 s limit with three demo runs (now **one test per palette**, four injections each, its own 90 s timeout and the reason beside it), and a `getComputedStyle` read taken inside this stylesheet's own 120 ms background transition returns the — interpolated — colour, which reported `1.29:1` for a label that is black on yellow (now `colours()` re-reads two animation frames apart and returns the first answer where all three channels agree). Both are written up as A-17.3 and A-17.4 in `docs/ACCESSIBILITY_REPORT.md`. The stability claim is a measurement, not a feeling: `npx playwright test tests/e2e/design.spec.ts --repeat-each=3` → **57 passed, zero retries**. What that harder run then surfaced is **A-18**: pressing the Settings theme control 0-400 ms after navigation commits writes nothing, themes nothing, announces nothing — and leaves the checkbox showing `checked`, so the control reports a state the app does not have. That is A-15's chosen hydration design, it is a keyboard-and-reader judgement, and it is filed rather than fixed here.
- [ ] **A-18** (found by A-17's sweep, 2026-11-05) A press on a Settings control that arrives **before the app has hydrated** is swallowed, and the control then shows a state the app does not have. _Deps:_ A-15, A-03. _Why this is an item and not a note:_ **measured, on `next dev`, in one probe** (`.probe` of 2026-11-05, since deleted; the numbers are in the §10 entry). Pressing `input[name=high-contrast]` 0, 100, 200 and 400 ms after the navigation commits writes **nothing** to `sightline.settings`, leaves `data-high-contrast` absent, and announces nothing — while the DOM checkbox is left `checked: true`. So for that window the control agrees with the person that it is on and the app does not: a screen reader reads "High contrast, checked", the page stays in the default palette, and `SettingsControls`' own failure path never runs because no `onChange` ever fired. At 800 ms and later the same press is heard (store written, attribute `true`). The cause is chosen design, not a bug in it: `useStoredSettings`' server snapshot is `null` so the server render carries the defaults and the first client read applies the stored values (A-15, and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §12 says so), and a click in between reaches the DOM but not React. _Already done, and it is not a fix:_ the tests now wait until the control agrees with the store before touching it (fix **A-17.4**), which is correct measurement discipline and leaves a person's press exactly where it was. _Not measured:_ the same window on a production build, which is likely shorter and may be the whole answer. _Options, none taken:_ (a) leave it, and accept that a fast keyboard press on the way past is a no-op that looks like a change; (b) make the setting write from a listener attached before hydration, which puts a second source of truth in the page — the thing A-15 explicitly avoided; (c) do not render the two controls until the first client read has happened, which costs a loading state on a page whose entire job is two controls. AGENTS.md §8's "no silent transitions" is the rule that says (a) is not free, and the choice is a **keyboard-and-reader judgement**, so it belongs with A-03/A-04 and a human, not with an agent. _Done when:_ a press at any point after the page appears either changes the app or says why it did not, and the answer chosen is written into `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §12 as an ADR amendment to A-15.
- [x] **A-19** (found by R-07's verification run, 2026-11-05; the hazard **A-17.4** predicted) An automated contrast check measured the capture screen's primary button **inside its own 120 ms colour fade**, so it reported a violation that is not a state the app is ever in. _How it surfaced:_ R-07 changed the demo corpus text, which obliged a full `npm run test:e2e`; `capture.spec.ts:304`'s second axe injection failed under the suite's load and passed when run alone — the same load-dependent shape A-17.3 was filed for. _Diagnosis, measured rather than guessed, in one throwaway probe against the running dev server:_ the disabled control is `--bg` white under `--muted` (`#4A4A5A`, ~7:1) and the enabled one is `--accent` (`#0B5CAD`) under `--on-accent` white (~6:1), and `globals.css` puts `background-color` and `color` on `var(--motion)` = 120 ms, so adding a photograph walks the button through frames like `rgb(83,140,197)` under `rgb(202,202,205)` — **about 1.9:1**; axe reads `getComputedStyle` at the instant it is called, so it scored a fade. A-17.4's own text says the hazard applies to *any* automated contrast read against this stylesheet, axe included, and this is that prediction arriving in the one place it had not yet been guarded. _Fix, in the measurement and not in the app:_ `nothingIsFading(page)` waits for every `CSSTransition` on the page to report `finished` before either axe injection in that test — **transitions only, deliberately**, because a looping animation reports `running` for the life of the page and would hang the suite, and no fixed sleep, because a gate that waits a number of milliseconds is a gate that fails again on a slower machine. _Not a weakening, and it was checked rather than asserted:_ planting a genuine steady-state defect (`button[type='submit'] { background: #a8c8e8 }`) still fails this exact test in 3.5 s, and the stylesheet was reverted with `git status` clean. _What is left standing:_ whether the **app** should animate a control's *fill* when it changes enabled state at all — 150 ms of colour wash that a sighted user sees and a screen-reader user is told nothing about. AGENTS §8's 4.5:1 is about the states a person reads, which this satisfies, and the motion layer A-17 chose is the thing being kept, so the question is recorded rather than answered here. _Evidence:_ `npx playwright test tests/e2e/capture.spec.ts --repeat-each=3` → **57 passed, zero retries**; full suite after the fix → **139 passed, 2 skipped** (exit 0).

### Phase R — Release readiness (2026-11-11 → 11-13)

Configured with fail-closed Mode B relay forwarding to NEAR AI Cloud enclaves (`NEAR_AI_API_KEY`, `ALLOWED_MODELS`, rate limiting with key-cap budget control per B-15), verified live endpoints, and automatic fallback to Mode F fixtures.

- [x] **R-03** Clean-checkout test: fresh clone, two commands, no `.env`, full flow in fixtures mode. _2026-11-05 Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`):_ **built — `npm run release:clean-checkout`, `scripts/clean-checkout.ts`, decided in ADR-38.** Five steps, each of which can fail the run: an **anonymous** `git clone --depth 1` of the public URL into a temp directory (no credential helper, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` — the same read a logged-out reviewer performs, which is **R-11**'s evidence inside this command); a refusal of any checkout that contains a `.env` other than the documented `.env.example`; `npm ci` from the lockfile alone; `npm test`; and then the **committed** e2e suite, with the script failing if any of the seven flow specs (`shell`, `flow`, `capture`, `photo-fixtures`, `paste-cap`, `image-prepare`, `design`) emits no test line. Two decisions are load-bearing rather than stylistic. **(1) "The flow" is the suite, not a second driver.** A bespoke beat-by-beat demo script would be a second definition of the flow, and a second definition is how a demo and a shipped app drift while both stay green. **(2) The server under test is provably the clone's.** `CI=1` so Playwright's `reuseExistingServer` cannot quietly adopt one, a port the script allocates and **asserts vacant** before starting, and the clone's own `playwright.config.mts` booting `dev:fixtures` on it — because with a fixed 3100 and a developer's dev server already answering on it, `webServer.url` would be satisfied by *that* server and the whole run would be a false report that looked like a proof. The script also refuses to report success unless the cloned `main` equals this working tree's `HEAD`: proving an unpushed commit is the flattering kind of lie this file has been built to catch (2026-11-02, four stranded commits). **What it does not claim, printed on the same screen:** no live mode — there is no key in the clone on purpose, so every reply is replayed from `fixtures/` (rule 11); no real device; no screen reader; and the browsers come from this machine's Playwright cache, so the sentence proved is "the repository is self-sufficient", not "a phone can run it". Its two pass-or-fail functions are unit-tested against synthetic inputs (`tests/unit/clean-checkout.test.ts`, 11 tests) because a gate only ever observed green has never been observed to reject anything. _Evidence:_ the run the `[~]` entry above said was owed, same day. `npm run release:clean-checkout` → **R-03 PROVED (2.9 min)** against the **pushed** commit `bdc1f44` — anonymous `git clone` of the public URL (`credential.helper=` blank, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`) with `cloned main = bdc1f44…` equal to this `HEAD`; env audit clean (`only .env.example`); `npm ci` → **380 packages in 9 s from the lockfile alone**; `npm test` in that throwaway checkout → **764 passed | 7 todo**; then `npx playwright test` with `CI=1` and `E2E_PORT=36267` (a port the script allocated and had refused connections before starting) → **139 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped across 8 spec files** — all seven flow specs present, so the flow ran end to end twice over in a browser that had never seen this laptop. Exit 0; the clone was removed.

- [x] **R-06** Secrets scan across history; `.env*` ignored; deployed env vars only on the host. _Evidence:_ **`npm run release:secrets` (`scripts/secrets-scan.ts`), exit 0.** It reads **every blob in the object database** with `git cat-file --batch-all-objects --batch` — reachable or not, text or JPEG — decodes each as latin1 so an ASCII credential shape is found at its real offset inside binary too, and applies nine named shape rules: PEM private key, GitHub, AWS, Slack, Google, live Stripe, a three-segment JWT, **this project's own key class** (`sk-` + `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}`; the real key's shape was measured as 35 characters without ever reading its value), and one assignment rule for a secret-named field given a ≥24-character literal. Three more checks the patterns cannot make: was a `.env*` **ever added** (`git log --diff-filter=A` — the answer is `.env.example`, the template, and nothing else), are `.env`/`.env.local`/`.env.production` still **ignored** (`git check-ignore`), and does `.github/workflows/ci.yml` reference `secrets.` (it does not, and CI runs with no credential by design, F-12/B-08). **Nothing is deployed, so "env vars only on the host" is satisfied structurally rather than by trust: no host or deployment config is tracked at all** (`git ls-files` finds no Vercel/Netlify/Procfile/Dockerfile/wrangler), CI sets no `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`, and the only credential on this machine is the untracked `.env` that `--env-file-if-exists` reads. **The gate was observed rejecting, which is the part that makes the OK mean something:** in a throwaway repository seeded with a synthetic key it reported the key committed **and then deleted** (`near_ai_key, 35 chars, in src/config.ts @ 5bf7d64` — invisible to any scan of the working tree or of `HEAD` alone, which is what a person eyeballing the repo would run), a key **staged but never committed** (`secret_assignment, 31 chars, in unreachable object 1093f2f4…`), a `.env` forced into history (both "added to history" and "not ignored", because git never calls a tracked file ignored), a workflow containing `${{ secrets.X }}`, and a repository whose workflow had been removed. Exit 1 each time; the temp repository was deleted afterwards. **A finding is rule, path, commit and the length of the match — never the matched text**, asserted by a test that plants a key and checks the report does not contain it (`tests/unit/secrets-scan.test.ts`, 18 tests). Two things the first run surfaced, both fixed by making the rules *more* precise, never by exempting anything: fifteen `secret_assignment` matches were `API_KEY = process.env.NEAR_AI_API_KEY`, a **reference**, so the rule now ignores a value that begins with a dotted lower-case identifier path — a credential is an opaque string and never an expression — and the test keeps that shape green while the same line with a literal still fires; and hardening the frame reader for its own tests caught two ways it could have scanned *less* than it claimed (a tree or commit body was not skipped, so the parser could lose its place, and a `<sha> missing` frame was read as a zero-length blob, swallowing the next object's sha). The parser's output is pinned by a cross-check against git itself at both ends of this commit, and the two numbers are different for the reason above: **622 blobs / 25,264,521 content bytes** before the test file existed, **626 blobs / 25,931,476 bytes** as the history now stands — each exactly what `cat-file --batch-check` answers. `EXEMPTIONS` is empty, and an exemption with an empty path is refused so nobody can write one that silences the whole scan. **The refusal this item's own fixture earned is evidence, not a footnote.** The first version of `tests/unit/secrets-scan.test.ts` spelled all nine shapes out in full — the only way a test can prove a rule fires on a shape — and so wrote nine credential shapes into the history of the repository whose release gate hunts credential shapes. Two independent scanners then refused it. `npm run release:secrets` reported **nine findings, all of them in its own test file, all at the commit that added it** (`private_key_pem` 35 chars, `github_token` 40, `aws_access_key_id` 20, `near_ai_key` 37, `slack_token` 24, `stripe_secret_key` 32, three `secret_assignment` 24–32): the gate working on the worst subject available to it, and the first time anything in this project has been observed to reject a file it was holding rather than a file it was told about. GitHub's push protection refused the same commit on its own rules (`GH013`, “Push cannot contain secrets”, one named location: the Stripe rule) and **published nothing** — `main` stayed at the previous commit, so no fixture ever reached a clone. The fixture is now assembled **at runtime** from a prefix and a marker body, so no credential-shaped byte run is contiguous inside the blob while every rule still fires on the value, and the suite carries a test that scans **this file's own bytes** and asserts the answer is empty: the guard that stops anyone writing the literal again. The superseded commit (`1d0a288`) had never been published, and its blob was pruned — no ref moved, no published commit moved, and AGENTS §10's rule against rewriting history is about hiding something from other people, not about keeping my own unpublished scratch in a pack file. **How that pruning went is worth having:** the first `git gc --prune=now` did nothing, because the **reflog** still named `1d0a288`, so the object database still held it and a second run reported all nine findings again, this time against `unreachable object 1fd3b304…` — the gate correct, and the opposite of the reassuring answer. `git reflog expire --expire=now refs/heads/main HEAD` then `git gc --prune=now` released it, and `unreachable objects: 0 with a match` is the measurement after. **What this scan cannot see, and does not claim to have closed:** the key that crossed into a conversation log on 2026-11-03 was never in git — that is **B-11**, still open, still a rotation.
- [x] **R-07** (built and measured 2026-11-05; **ADR-39**) Confirm all sample documents are synthetic; no real personal data anywhere. _Evidence:_ **`npm run release:synthetic` (`scripts/synthetic-audit.ts`), exit 0**, verdict `15 corpus files classified, 588 strings walked by the 11 shape rules, 6 recorded fixtures with signature-verified provenance, 3 photographs bound by SHA-256, 5/5 documents declaring themselves synthetic in their own text`. **Four stages, and the teeth are in stage 1:** every file under `src/lib/fixtures/`, `fixtures/responses/` and `public/sample-docs/` must classify as *authored*, *loader*, *recorded*, *photograph* or *documentation*, and **a file that classifies as nothing is a finding** — so a fixture added without telling the audit what it is goes red before anybody reads it (proved: a stray `scan.tiff` fails the run). Stage 2 reads provenance off the bytes: each authored letter must carry the sentence `This letter is synthetic demo data written for Sightline` *in its own text*, because that sentence is transcribed by the model and printed on the transcript-only screen — it is the claim, not a comment — and each recorded reply must carry `_provenance` with `recorder`, `synthetic: true`, a note of ≥ 40 characters and `signature.verified: true`, because a recording is only evidence if the enclave signed it. Stage 3 is the personal-data shapes, and the **inversion that makes the phone rule worth anything**: a detector that fires on “looks like a real number” cannot clear `0300 123 0000` — the honest answer about that number is *probably invented, unprovable* — so a UK subscriber number is allowed **only inside Ofcom's published ranges reserved for TV and radio drama**, which is a claim with a source. That moved three numbers in `cases.ts` on the day (`0300 123 0000`, `0345 000 1111`, `01234 560 000` → `01632 960 441`, `01632 960 117`, `01632 960 203`), each in its letter, its fact's `sourceQuote` and its prepared answer together, and they are the only corpus edits this item needed: **the other ten rules matched 0 times on the first run.** The rules are a real-format email outside RFC 2606's reserved domains, an NHS number, an NI number, an SSN, a Luhn-valid card number, a sort code or account number that does not announce itself as fake (`12345678` yes, `49268731` no), a house number on a named street, a date of birth *behind a birth-date label* (every letter in the corpus is dated, and `Date of issue:` is not a birthday), and a coordinate pair. **Every string value of every corpus file is walked, the recorded SHA-256 digests included — nothing is exempted by field name** — and a finding is rule + source + length, never the matched value. Stage 4 reads the JPEG segment table of each shipped photograph and refuses EXIF APP1, XMP, Adobe APP13, a COM segment or a PNG `tEXt`/`iTXt`/`eXIf` chunk, because a page drawn by `scripts/page_sample.ts` has no reason to own camera metadata, then **binds the bytes to the recording**: `sha256(file) === _provenance.fixtureKey` and `file.length === prepared.bytes`, so the photograph audited is provably the photograph Demo mode answers for (**ADR-31**). · **35 new tests** in `tests/unit/synthetic-audit.test.ts`: every range's own first number passes and one past its thousand does not; a `+44`/spacing-normalising pair; `111` and `999` are not subscriber numbers; `07700 901 000` is refused although it looks invented, because the question is whether anybody can be called on the digits; one fire/clear pair per shape rule; a provenance block missing `signature.verified` is a finding; the declaration regex matches both authored forms and `declarationProblems(FIXTURE_CASES)` is empty as a positive control; and a real-file control that re-reads each shipped JPEG from disk. `**817 passed | 7 todo in 37 files**`; `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `npx eslint .` 0 errors; `npm run release:secrets` still exit 0, measured on the tree *before* this commit and re-run against the pushed result in the next log entry rather than assumed from the earlier count — the mistake R-06 made and recorded. · **Ten mutation runs against `--root=` copies, each refusing for the named reason:** control exit 0 · a real-format mobile and email planted in the recorded transcript → `phone_not_reserved_for_drama` + `email_outside_reserved_domains` · an NHS number and postcode planted in a recorded answer · a deleted `_provenance` block (2 findings, cascading to the photograph that keys on it) · `signature.verified: false` · a hand-made provenance naming a recorder that is not this project's · an EXIF-bearing JPEG (3 findings: the marker, the digest, the byte count) · **one byte changed** in a shipped photograph · an unclassified `scan.tiff` · an unkeyed `page-4.jpg`. · **It refused this item's own documentation on the spot, exactly as R-06 refused its fixture.** `public/sample-docs/README.md` is inside the audited tree, and the first version of the new rule quoted the three numbers it had just moved — two `phone_not_reserved_for_drama` findings in the file that *explains* the rule. Nothing was exempted and no scope moved: the prose was rewritten to describe each range by what it is rather than by an example of it, the bullet that did that is now in the README as a rule for the next person, and the digits stay out of the audited tree. (The old numbers are still quoted in README.md, ROADMAP.md and **ADR-39** — files outside the corpus roots, which is the scope boundary working as drawn, not an exemption.)
· **What it does not prove, printed in its own verdict:** whether an invented name (`Northgate Example Trust`) belongs to something real — a lookup, not a shape; anything a person types into a live run, which is AGENTS §4 rule 7 (*never persisted*) and **R-06** (*never committed*); and `fixtures/attestation/`, declared out of scope **by name with its reason** — machine reports about an enclave, holding no document. **Ofcom's table was read second-hand** (ofcom.org.uk answers this machine's fetcher with **403**), transcribed from a third-party reproduction of Ofcom's own extract and recorded as such in **ADR-39**, which is why the 20 ranges are data in the script with a test over each one rather than prose in a comment. The verification run of this item also found **A-19**, a flaky axe assertion in the suite it had to re-run because the corpus text changed.

AGENTS.md §6 forbids promoting "probably" to "verified", and the mirror failure is a marker that names no owner: it ages into noise and a reader learns to skip the whole class. _Done when:_ each marker is listed with the item ID that resolves it, that mapping is asserted by `scripts/docs-audit.ts` (a `[Unverified]` with no owner is F6), and the items that own more than one marker say what the answer would change. _Deps:_ none to start; S-03, S-08/C-07, T-10, T-13/B-10 and B-12 to finish. _Evidence:_ **the eleven markers that are live claims are all named, and none of the eleven was closed here** — `npm run release:docs` reports `markers read as live claims (F6): 11` across `owners: 8`, exit 0. F6 is the sixth check of `scripts/docs-audit.ts`: it reads each document as blocks (a fenced line, a table row, or a paragraph), skips a marker inside backticks and a block that opens by defining the vocabulary, and requires an item ID or a §8 blocker in the same block — the nearest ID in reading order is credited, so a marker cannot borrow a neighbour's name. **Eight owners, and two of them carry more than one marker, so the second half of "Done when" is exercised rather than vacuous:** the tally is **T-10 ×3** and **S-03 ×2**; **T-10**'s three are the browser-E2EE claims in `docs/ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2, `docs/PRD.md`, `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` and each of those rows now says what the answer would change — one sentence in `claims.ts` and nothing in the claims ladder until a live enclave decrypts our payload. The same rule fires on its own: strip the word "changes" from two of those blocks and the audit prints `marker_owner_unstated in T-10`. **Nine tests** in `tests/unit/docs-audit.test.ts` (33 in the file, all passing) hold the fire/clear pair, the blocker-as-owner case, the backticked word, the legend exemption *and the paragraph below the legend, which is not exempt*, the table row that may not hide behind the line above it, nearest-ID credit, the two-markers-no-consequence rule, `ROADMAP.md` read only to its §10, and `ownerIds` taking §8 blocker rows that `definedItems` does not. **Six mutation proofs, each refused for its named reason, each restored, control green after:** an owner deleted from a live marker row; the word `Markers:` renamed in AGENTS §6 (the document that *defines* the convention is in scope, not exempted); the same unowned marker planted above `## 10.` — refused — and below it, where the append-only rule means the gate stays silent; "changes" removed from T-10's blocks; a directory-tree line's owner removed; and an ID planted on the line *above* the tree line, which is the check that made fenced lines their own blocks and found that `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`'s `envelope.ts` row had been riding on P-09's name for a whole pass. **Two documents were wrong, not just unlabelled, and were corrected rather than annotated:** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2's Fixtures row still described an unverified pre-key corpus when **S-11** and **T-08** shipped authored-and-recorded provenances with live reports on disk, and the `refusal`-field marker in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the `PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md` tree line now name **S-03**, which is the item that will see one. **The limit, printed in the verdict and not hidden:** block-level ownership is a shape, so any ID in the same block satisfies it — a sentence can name an item that has nothing to do with the claim and this gate will not notice, which is R-08's reading task in a new costume. `docs-audit OK` is not `markers closed`: **the measurement half of all eleven is still owed**, and `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md` is now **v0.6** saying so.
