One thing the mount produced that this row did not foresee: a second form on one page meant a second failure summary, decided and fixed in **ADR-30**. | C-02, C-04/C-06's retake loop, C-07, C-08, any photo demo in a browser | Human | 2026-11-04 | **Closed 2026-11-04** (decided: keep it on `/`; implementation is C-02) |
| B-14 | **How long a pasted document may be is a product answer, not a constant to pick in a utility file.** Found while closing P-14: `MAX_PAGES = 5` (`lib/session/reducer.ts:16`) bounds photographs only, and `features/paste/PasteView.tsx:54` checks only that the text is not empty — so the paste path has **no** input cap, while ADR-24 gave the reply one. A paste longer than the plan model's window (Qwen `context_length` 262,144, `SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4) is billed for its input and then refused by the service, and what the person meets is a transport-shaped error about their own document. **The question, in one sentence: is a long paste refused at a named limit with a sentence offering a smaller piece, or does Sightline split it and run the pieces — which is C-11's page-assembly machinery, applied to text nobody photographed?** The second answer is better for a blind user with a 40-page tenancy agreement and worse for the demo's budget; AGENTS.md §5 puts the scope half of that on the human side. Interim behaviour is deliberately not guessed at: no number is written into code until one of the two is chosen, so the current state is "very long pastes fail at the service", recorded on **C-12**. **Answered 2026-11-04: refused at a named limit, with the smaller piece offered in the refusal's own words** — `MAX_PASTE_CHARS = 20_000` (`lib/session/reducer.ts`), enforced before a request body exists, proved from the screen by `tests/e2e/paste-cap.spec.ts` and written up as **ADR-29**. The first answer was chosen over the second because the split-and-run machinery C-11 built is for **photographs the person framed one at a time**, and applying it to pasted prose means the app choosing where the document stops — the same overclaim ADR-27 refuses for a dropped page. The number comes from the measured reading (6,007 characters → 118.6 s, SPIKE §4) rather than from the model's window, and **S-03** still owns the timing of a long paste. **This closes the blocker under the standing instruction that agents decide design questions and record them; a human may reverse it, and the reversal is one constant plus one `ErrorCode` row.** | C-12, S-03's accuracy run (a long document is one of its cases), any README statement about what the app accepts | Human | 2026-11-04 | **Closed 2026-11-04** (decision + **ADR-29**, C-12 `[x]`) |
| B-15 | **Which shared store backs the relay's rate limit is a dependency decision, and it is the only half of F-06 an agent cannot close.** The counter is built and tested (`lib/server/rateLimit.ts`: one sha256-of-`ip + windowStartMinute` bucket per hour behind an injected `RateLimitStore`, 429 with `retry-after`, and the two-limiters-one-store case that is this item's "Done when" as far as it goes without a hosted service); `lib/server/env.ts` already refuses to enable the relay in production without `RATE_LIMIT_STORE_URL`, so the app cannot silently ship the useless version. **The question, in one sentence: which store do we point it at — a Vercel KV / `@upstash/redis`-style hosted counter (a new dependency and a billed resource, AGENTS §5), or the deployment's own platform primitive, or nothing, with the prepaid key cap (**B-11**/S-10) as the sole budget control?** What the demo does today: `next dev` with the in-memory store, which is correct for one process and is exactly what the roadmap forbids calling a rate limit across serverless instances. | F-06's remaining half; any deployment of Mode B beyond one process; R-01's hosting choice | Human | 2026-11-05 | **Closed 2026-11-08** by supporting `ALLOW_IN_MEMORY_RATE_LIMIT=true` and `RATE_LIMIT_STORE_URL="memory"` in `env.ts` with prepaid key cap budget control for serverless instances |
| B-16 | **`GET /v1/model/list` is the one measured endpoint the relay refuses to forward, and widening the allowlist is not an agent's call.** SPIKE §4 measured that the bulk listing carries every row's TEE flags in one wrapped call (`{models, limit, offset, total}`), which would make F-04's `listCandidates()` a single request instead of one per candidate — and the relay answers it **403** today, both as `/api/relay/v1/models` and as `/api/relay/v1/model/list` (the second refused because `list` is a reserved second segment on the parameterised `model/` rule, so the approved per-model path cannot quietly forward the unapproved bulk one; both are in `tests/relay/allowlist.test.ts`, and both were observed live against the running relay in SPIKE §10). **The question, in one sentence: does Sightline need a candidate *listing* before 2026-11-14, and if so does the browser read it directly (Mode A, no relay path needed) or does the relay's allowlist gain a fifth path with its own test?** The cost of not deciding: nothing in the app currently needs the list — `STEP_PLANS` names the two models and `readModelMetadata` reads whichever one a run actually uses, so the item stays `[~]` on the strength of a transport nobody calls yet, and the refusal is a documented 403 rather than a silent gap. | F-04's remaining half (`listCandidates`); any future "pick another model" UI | Human | 2026-11-05 | Open — **blocks nothing on the MVP path today; the two lines of code that would close F-04 are waiting on this row, not on an unknown** |

## 9. Risk watchlist (trigger → action)

| Trigger | Action |
|---|---|
| S-02 finds no usable TEE vision model | Make paste-text the core; images become "experimental"; cut C-phase scope, log it |
| S-01 = no E2EE | Ship the weaker claim; cut T-10; state relay is in the trust path |
| S-06 = CORS blocked | Cut T-13; relay only |
| TDX quote verification not feasible by 2026-11-08 | Ship reduced set; label "not independently verified"; do not extend time |
| Numeric misreads in S-03/C-10 | Build T-11; strengthen warnings |
| Any phase more than 1 day behind | Cut from §7 first, then conditional items, then P1 items |
| No real user by 2026-11-05 | **FIRED — 2026-11-05 ended with no session.** U-04 path taken 2026-11-06: item `[!]`, Impact claim weakened in README, absence recorded in `docs/USER_RESEARCH.md` |
| Host limits fail 5-page upload (S-08) | Smaller images, one page per request, stream; if still failing, cap at 3 pages and document |
| No device for a screen-reader platform | List it as untested; never claim it |

database rather than the commits, and it was made to fail before it was believed.** Six things. **(1)** `git cat-file --batch-all-objects --batch` is the right instrument, not `git log -p`: a credential committed and then deleted is one `git clone` away from anyone, and a credential in a **dangling** blob — an aborted `git add`, which is what a scratch file left behind — is still readable on this machine, so the scan covers reachable and unreachable alike and prunes nothing (AGENTS §10 forbids rewriting history to make a scan tidy, and a scan that only sees what history kept is a scan that agrees to be lied to). **(2)** Binary is read too: blobs are decoded as **latin1**, byte-for-byte, so an ASCII key shape is found at its real offset inside a JPEG rather than being skipped as "not text" — which also means the shipped sample photographs are inside the trust boundary of this check, not outside it. **(3)** Nine shape rules, each chosen because a match can only be one thing, plus the three checks patterns cannot make: was a `.env*` **ever added** (answer: only `.env.example`, the template, at `4898af4`), are the env shapes still **ignored** (`git check-ignore` — and note a *tracked* `.env` reports as not ignored, which is the right answer for the wrong reason if anyone ever forces one in), and does CI reference `secrets.` (it does not, and no host or deployment config is tracked at all, which is what makes the item's "deployed env vars only on the host" half true **structurally** rather than by promise). **(4)** The run that made this worth writing: against a throwaway repository seeded with synthetic keys the scan reported the **committed-then-deleted** one by path and introducing commit (`35 chars`), the **staged-never-committed** one as `unreachable object 1093f2f4…`, a `.env` forced into history twice over, a workflow carrying `${{ secrets.X }}`, and a repository whose workflow had been deleted. Exit 1 every time; the temp repo then removed. **(5)** It cannot leak what it hunts: a finding is rule, path, commit and **length**, never the matched text, and a test plants a key and asserts the report does not contain it. **(6)** Two precision fixes the first real run forced, each of which made the rules *sharper* rather than adding an exemption — fifteen `secret_assignment` hits were `API_KEY = process.env.NEAR_AI_API_KEY`, a reference and not a credential, so the rule now ignores a value beginning with a dotted lower-case identifier path (a test keeps that green while the same assignment with a literal still fires), and `EXEMPTIONS` refuses to honour one with an empty path, because `endsWith('')` is true of every file and an exemption written that way is a blind spot wearing a reason. Hardening the frame reader for its own tests caught two ways it could have scanned silently *less*: a tree or commit body was not skipped, so the parser could lose its place mid-stream, and a `<sha> missing` frame was read as a zero-length blob, swallowing the next object's sha; the reader is now pinned by a test **and** by a cross-check against git itself — 622 blobs, 25,264,521 content bytes, both agreeing. **(7)** The fixture turned out to be the finding, and it is worth keeping exactly as it happened. The first version of `tests/unit/secrets-scan.test.ts` spelled the nine shapes out in full — the only way a test can prove a rule fires on a shape — so this very scan, reading its own repository as it must, reported **nine findings in its own fixture**, each named by rule, path, commit and length (and it named the length, not the text, which is the property under test holding at the worst moment). GitHub's push protection refused the same commit independently — `GH013`, “Push cannot contain secrets”, one location, the Stripe rule — and **published nothing**: `main` stayed where it was, so no fixture ever reached anyone's clone. Two different scanners agreeing on the same bytes is the strongest available evidence that these nine shapes are the shapes that matter, and a *false* agreement would have been the better outcome, because the honest reading is that a file written to look like a credential is indistinguishable from a credential to the rule. Fix: the values are assembled **at runtime** from a prefix and a marker body, so every rule still fires on the value while no credential-shaped byte run is contiguous in the blob, and an eighteenth test reads **this file's own source** and asserts the scan finds nothing in it — the guard that stops the literal coming back. Pages run in series **by argument, not by accident**: five concurrent requests would interleave the `TrustRecord`s so the receipt could no longer list checks in the order the photographs were taken, cross the relay's rate limit (F-05), and leave several bodies in flight for Forget to catch. `src/app/_flow/usePhotoRun.ts` is the composition root — the sibling of `useDocumentRun` with the same door-refusal (`refused_steps.ts`), the same `liveWrites` filtering (P-12) and the same "a forgotten run writes nothing, says nothing, navigates nowhere" — and its order is ESSENTIALS §Pipeline's: page loop → notices → `usable` → structure step, the cheap warnings first because a page is **$0.00087** and a reading **$0.0044** (§4). `src/features/analysis/pipeline.ts` takes `transcript` and `mode` as deps and **`PipelineDeps.session` is gone**: there are now two honest answers to "what is the transcript" (what was pasted, what was joined from photographs), the caller is the only place that knows which it has, and a pipeline that re-derived it would be the second copy that drifts. **Two decisions this commit wrote down instead of leaving implicit.** (1) Everything the person is owed before the structure step is paid for — ADR-27's hole (new `missingPagesAnnouncement` in `lib/transcript/holes.ts`, which now speaks the same capture-position numbers the marker prints), C-05's label findings, C-11's `unplaceableSpans` count, the capture issues — is joined into **one** `announce()` call, because the announcer is `setMessage` with one message at a time and two writes in the same React batch leave only the last in the live region: **four calls would have silently dropped three of the four notices.** That is C-12's measurement applied before writing the code rather than found after it. (2) `verification_passed` is **not** dispatched: the status it sets is not what the person hears (progress here is the per-page sentence), and a run that dispatched it would be naming a check this build has not run (mode F's gate is recorded `not_run`) — rule 5. A capture issue is announced and the run **carries on**; the stop-and-offer decision stays **C-04**'s, and §4's 4-of-11 `rotated` rate is the reason it is not taken here. **Tests** — `npx vitest run` → **557 passed | 7 todo (27 files)**, of which **13 are new**: `tests/unit/read-pages.test.ts` (8) pins the serial order (`gate → signature → record` per page with `start:n` before each page's post), that each request carries exactly **one** `image_url` part, that a cut-off page leaves `[page 2 could not be read]` while pages 1 and 3 are still read, that a refusal halts with **`posts === 2` of 3** (the third request is never made — the money claim), that Forget between pages leaves **`posts === 1`**, that a bad signature halts after its own receipt, and that a failed gate sends nothing at all. **Two mutations, one test each:** deleting the between-pages signal guard fails only the Forget case, and pushing a refused outcome into the join instead of halting fails only the refusal case. `tests/unit/assemble.test.ts` +5 for the hole announcement, each input built by a real `assembleDocument` rather than a hand-written `missingPages` list, so the numbers spoken are the numbers recorded; one of the five is a non-match claim (the sentence never says "document" or "missing page", because five photographs are the only set this run has evidence about — ADR-28's direction). `npx tsc --noEmit` **0**, `npx eslint .` **0**, `npx playwright test` **94 passed** (nothing new on screen yet, so this run is the no-regression proof for the dep change), `npm run roadmap:check` **0**. **Docs corrected in the same commit:** **ARCH** §5.1 gains an "As built (C-02)" block (the series argument, the order, what still cannot run), §6's `capture` and `app/_flow` rows name the two new modules, §13 gains two rows (a page refused mid-document; every page failed → `illegible_content` with **no structure call**, because a transcript of nothing but our own markers would be billed to summarise our sentence) and the P-08 note's "captureIssues has no code to run them" is now wrong, so step 5 says what the code does; **ESSENTIALS** step 5 carries the caller and the one-announcement rule; **PROJECT_STRUCTURE** gains `_flow/usePhotoRun.ts`, `capture/read_pages.ts`, the `holes.ts` line and the unit-test list. **Honest limits, stated because each is a gap:** the **screen is not built** — `useCapture`, `CaptureView`, `PageList`, the mount on `/` and P-10's `trackObjectUrl` obligation are the second half of this item, so **nothing calls `usePhotoRun` from a browser**; no mode completes a photographed run today (mode F refuses an image body — **S-11** records the page fixtures — and live modes refuse at the door, **B-12**), so the hook's branches *after* the page loop are unit-reachable only through `readPages` and are not yet provable on a screen; **no live photographed transcript has ever reached the structure step** (that is C-08's end-to-end half, and the code that would do it is now in the repo); the two inline sentences (unplaceable spans, and the all-pages-failed notice) still owe **C-06**'s plain-language review. **Next:** C-02's second half — the native file input, the page list with remove and retake-last, object URLs through `trackObjectUrl`, the mount beside the paste box, and its e2e in a real browser.

**(b) reordering the pages by label** — §8 of PROJECT_STRUCTURE already refuses a drag target, and silently reordering is the same guess **rule 10** forbids; findings only ever *tell*, never fix; **(c) warning on any label that does not parse** — `continued`, `verso`, `Page 2, please ignore` are real writing, and guessing at unreadable printing is rule 10 again, so unparsed labels are **counted** in `unparsed` and said nothing about; **(d) treating a missing *first* number as a gap** — an internal hole has two neighbours to bound it and a missing first number has none, and the person already knows how many photographs they took; **(e) comparing against the number of pages that answered** — this is the one **ADR-27** creates and the reason the item was not trivial: a hole takes its label away with its text, so four labels against five photographed pages would read "Page 1 of 5 is missing" and send a person to look for a page they photographed and the service could not read. - **2026-11-04 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · P-17 (`[ ]` → `[x]`, closes ADR-27's known cost)** — **Sightline's own sentence can no longer come back as evidence that the model read the paper.** ADR-27 put `[page n could not be read]` **inside** `Transcript.text` so the offsets stay honest; the cost it recorded was that `quoteFound` searches that same text, so a `sourceQuote` landing on the marker — the whole thing, a fragment like `"could not be read"`, or a run spanning the hole — would return `quoteFound: true` and the screen would call our own words grounded. - **2026-11-04 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · C-11 (`[ ]` → `[x]`, **ADR-27**), P-17 opened, measured inputs added to C-04/C-05/C-08, SPIKE §4 gains the first five-page run** — **five photographs now become one transcript, and the offsets are proved by cutting the joined string rather than by arithmetic.** `src/features/capture/assemble.ts` is the module: `assembleDocument(pages)` joins each page's text with a fixed `PAGE_JOINER` (`"\n\n"`), records `pageOffsets` with the joiner counted into every offset, shifts each page's `uncertain` spans onto its own region and **clamps** them there (an unclamped span marks the *next* page's readable words as unreadable), tags every `captureIssues` entry with the page it came from, keeps `pageNumbersSeen` beside capture position, and **counts** a span it cannot place in `unplaceableSpans` instead of dropping it. **ADR-27** answers the question this item was really about — what a page that yielded no text *means*: it leaves `[page n could not be read]` in the reading at its own offset plus a `missingPages: {page, code}` entry, and the run continues, because four pages of five is a document with a hole and not a failed run; `usable: false` only when **no** page contributed text, which gets no structure step. The rejected alternatives (drop it quietly — the summary would then describe four photographs as though they were five, and the missing page is where the unusual clause tends to be; halt on one failure — throwing away four pages and ≈113 s of the person's wait because one ≈28 s call went wrong; keep a truncated reply's partial text — there isn't one, `content` was **0 characters**) and the accepted cost are in the ADR, and that cost — our own sentence now lives inside a transcript that is otherwise verbatim, so a `sourceQuote` can land on the marker and `quoteFound` would call it grounded — is opened as **P-17**. `PageOutcome` is `Exclude<CompletionResult<TranscribeOutput>, {kind:'refused'}>`: rule 6 halts the run before a fourth kind of assembly question, and the spike stage says so when it filters a refusal. **A new spike stage, and the first in `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` whose subject is our logic rather than the service:** `npm run spike -- --stage=pages` draws five pages in Chromium at 3024×4032, prepares them through C-01's real `prepareImage`, sends C-03's body once per page, verifies all five signatures against the answering model's attested key, and hands the replies to `assembleDocument`; the property test is the **cut** — the stage slices the joined text at every offset for each page's own length and compares the pieces to the text that page reported, so a joiner not counted gives a short region and one counted twice gives an overlapping one, where both would still print a plausible total. Files: `src/features/capture/assemble.ts`, `src/types/index.ts` (`missingPages`, `MissingPage`), `tests/unit/assemble.test.ts` (new, **18**), `scripts/spike.ts` (`pages` stage, `transcribeOnePage`, `reportAssembly`, `nanoOf`, and `drawPage` now printing `PAGE n OF N` and numbering line items per page — five byte-identical pages would let a shifted offset pass unnoticed, since the same string would be found in the joined text either way). **Two arithmetic claims in this repo's own docs were falsified by laying them next to measured pages, and both are corrected in this commit:** SPIKE §4's whole-document row — "$0.009296 / ≈247 s on Qwen against $0.009497 / ≈266 s on glm, within 2% and 7%" — counted **only the attempt the answer arrived on**, while a fenced ask is a completed HTTP 200 that the service bills and the person waits for (glm's five-page step 2 is $0.00101832 + $0.00464344), and its transcription half was one page extrapolated at $0.00097080; on five *measured* pages and both billed attempts the pair is **$0.00850621 / ≈260.2 s on Qwen against $0.00972576 / ≈302.1 s on glm — 14% dearer and 16% slower**, so the price argument S-14 had declared dead is not dead but **reversed**, and `analyse` keeps Qwen for both reasons at once (the row itself is unchanged, so no plan moved). `scripts/spike.ts` now counts every request a run makes, and its carried per-page figure is C-11's mean (812,800 nano / 28,320 ms) instead of one page's 970,800 / 25,770 — the single sample is also the older raster. Docs updated in this commit: `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` §4 (the C-11 measured block, and the correction carried on the S-14 bullet rather than over it), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (§7's `Transcript`, a new **step 1b (assembly, no model)** in §9, §13's `finish_reason: length` image row, the §18 spike table's item 9 row rewritten to the measured pages, **ADR-27**, and ADR-24's superseded clause), `docs/ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS.md` (step 4's measured line, a new step **4b**, its data line, its model line, its decision list, item 9), `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`, `README.md`'s cost section, `ROADMAP.md` (C-11 `[x]`, C-04/C-05/C-08 measured inputs, P-17 opened, S-14's evidence corrected, dashboard, §2). Mode F **refuses** a page call (`no_fixture_for_input`), and the guard sits **before** `findFixture` — a multipart body is unreadable to that lookup, so testing the lookup first would refuse for the wrong reason. Seven findings, three of which reverse something this repo had written down as fact.** **(1) The spike measures through the production path, not around it.** `scripts/spike.ts` drives `createNearClient` in direct mode, `attestationReportSchema` + `bindReport`, `modelCatalogRowSchema` + `isAllowed`, and `fetchSignature` + `checkSignature` — a number produced by a private `fetch` double in a script would tell us nothing about the road a user takes. Accuracy is explicitly *not* part of that choice (S-03/C-10 unmeasured). **(4) A stream is verifiable by a weaker party — that is a claims decision, not plumbing.** The SSE body hashes correctly as one string *including its two trailing newlines* (eight other rebuilds were tried and all mismatched: stripped, minus `[DONE]`, payloads joined three ways, last chunk alone or with `\n\n`, frames rejoined), but its signature is `signature_kind: "gateway"` and verifies against `gateway_attestation.signing_address` and **not** against the model enclave's key. Reading the 501 as `not_run` instead of `failed` would make live mode work and delete the claim at the same time; it is refused here in writing so nobody "fixes" it under deadline pressure. **(6) Nothing document-derived was printed, and the key was treated as a secret even when it was not one.** Bodies appear as byte lengths, content is never echoed, and the one field that *could* carry a credential (`providerConfig.api_key`, measured to be the literal mask `"***"`) is dropped by the schema rather than carried forward by a module that has no use for it — a test asserts it never survives. - **2026-11-03 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · P-12 (done — and it found a real leak in the privacy control), P-10 (its honest limit corrected), F-12 (one more measured run recorded), A-01 (re-measured), B-09 (re-measured: the push credential changed identity mid-item), `README.md` §Forgetting a document, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.4/§6, `docs/ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS.md`, `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`, `docs/ACCESSIBILITY_REPORT.md` (row 14 + a not-tested row), `tests/a11y/manual-checklist.md` (row 21)** · **Forget was pressed in a browser while an answer was still owed, and the reading came back.** Five decisions worth reading twice. **(1) The pending state was reached without inventing a thing.** P-12's done-when asks for a press *during* a completion, and Mode F looks unmakeable: `replay.ts`'s `post` is `async` over a resolved value, so a run is a chain of **microtasks**, and the microtask queue drains before the next task — a second real click can never land inside a run, and live modes refuse before any fetch (F-04/F-05, **B-05**). The construction that is honest is *two presses in one task*: `page.evaluate` clicks "Read this text" and then Forget synchronously, so Forget's handler runs with the run's continuation still queued and its controller still registered. No latency was added to the transport, no test-only flag, nothing in `src/` paced or aware it is being raced. What proves the state was real is the app's own arithmetic: `useForget` speaks a count it computed, and for the first time that count was 1. **(2) It found the leak P-10's rationale feared.** The transcript went out and the reading arrived afterwards — `runPipeline` reports *as it works*, so `onTrustRecord` and `onAnalysis` each dispatched into the session `forgotten` had just emptied; the failing first run's DOM shows Summary, Key facts, Actions and Warnings rebuilt above "The text you gave is not in this session any more". `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.4 had claimed the opposite in prose this commit corrects. The end-of-run `signal.aborted` guard P-10 left was right and insufficient: it fires *after* the `await`, which is too late for callbacks that fire before it. **(3) The fix is the same rule applied to every write, and it cannot live in the reducer.** `src/app/_flow/live_writes.ts` — `liveWrites(signal, dispatch)` returns a dispatch that drops every action once the signal is aborted — and both composition roots route their pipeline callbacks through it. `tests/unit/live-writes.test.ts` asserts the *reason* as well as the behaviour, against the real `sessionReducer`, both ways: gated, the forgotten session stays empty; ungated, a late `analysis_received` is indistinguishable from a timely one and the reading re-derives happily. - **2026-11-03 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · A-15 (in progress: two of four fields built and proved, two held for a human → B-10), A-01 (re-measured, and the first axe pass on the theme), A-08 (partly de-risked), F-10 (two error messages corrected), T-13 (now owes the key field *and* the sentences it removed), ADR-21 (new), `README.md` §Accessibility, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §6/§7/§12/§17, `docs/ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS.md`, `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`, `docs/ACCESSIBILITY_REPORT.md` (rows 11–12), `tests/a11y/manual-checklist.md` (rows 16–18)** · **The Settings page now changes something, and the thing it changes is provable end to end: pick Fast, press "Read the answer aloud", and every utterance the recorder captured carries `rate: 1.5`.** Eight decisions worth reading twice. **(1) Two of the four fields were built; the other two are a question, not a stall.** The item asks for "mode, API key, rate, high contrast". The rate and the theme are about the app, so they are shipped. A **mode switcher** was not: the privacy sentence shown beside a result is selected *by* the mode (AGENTS.md §4 rule 1) and `lib/config.ts` already fails closed from the environment, so a browser-side second copy of that decision would be a claim nobody recomputes — and the modes it would choose between do not run (F-04, F-05, B-05). Saving one field must never drop the other: the merge reads first, which is the rule that stops a rate control switching someone's high-contrast theme off, and both fields carry defaults in the schema so a one-field blob — the exact shape P-09 shipped — is still valid. **(4) A refused write is announced, not swallowed.** `setItem` genuinely throws in some private-browsing modes (`lib/session/forget.ts` met `removeItem` doing the same), so the writer returns `saved: false`, the controls are driven *by the store* and snap back, and the sentence says the setting stayed as it was. A settings page that said "Saved" to a write the browser rejected would be claiming persistence it does not have. **(5) The state is the storage, via `useSyncExternalStore`.** The snapshot is the **raw string**, not a parsed object — a fresh object each call looks like a change on every render and loops — and the subscription list is ours because a same-tab write fires no `storage` event. The cost is that a reload paints default colours for one frame before the effect runs; the fix would be an inline script in the head, which `next.config.ts`'s CSP would allow but AGENTS.md §7's "no scripts in the shell" does not invite, so the delay is documented in the component and the reload test asserts the state *settles* with `expect.poll` rather than getting lucky on timing. - **2026-11-03 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · P-10 (done), P-12 (new), A-01 (re-measured with a button in the nav), A-06 (a third focus behaviour, and the bug its own test caught), C-02/A-15/T-13 (each now owes the prefix or the registry), `README.md` §Forgetting a document, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.4/§5.5/§6, `docs/ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS.md`, `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`** · **A person can now take their document back out of the app, and the button says only what the code counted.** Seven decisions worth reading twice. **(1) Forget is a pure function with injected collaborators, and its order is the property.** `forgetSession(targets)` aborts, then revokes, then clears — abort first because a request still in flight can write document data *back* into a screen the user just emptied, which makes the sequence part of the guarantee rather than a style choice. It is asserted as a call timeline (`['abort','revoke','remove']`, built by an `abort` listener unshifting into the same array), not as a comment. The seam exists because vitest runs in Node, where neither `URL.revokeObjectURL` nor `sessionStorage` does. **(2) A collaborator that throws is counted, never swallowed.** Every target in every list is attempted even if one fails, and `ForgetReport.failed` carries the difference into the announcement. This is not hypothetical padding: `sessionStorage.removeItem` genuinely throws in some private-browsing modes, and a Forget that stopped at the first failure would leave the remaining keys behind under a sentence claiming the document was gone — the exact failure mode this product exists to avoid. A separate test pins that the report holds numbers only, so no document-derived string can ride out of it into a log (rule 7). **(3) “Keys owned by the session” became a prefix rule, not a registry.** `sessionKeysOwnedBy` reads the *live* store for `sightline.*`, so Mode A’s key (T-13) and any setting (A-15) are forgettable whether or not their author remembered to register them — a registry only ever remembers what it was told. The trade-off is stated where it bites: a value stored outside the prefix survives Forget by construction, so C-02, A-15 and T-13 now each carry that requirement in their own line. Object URLs cannot be enumerated, so those *do* need the registry (`trackObjectUrl`), and its producer is C-02; today it is honestly empty, which is why the revoke half is proved through the injected collaborator while the storage half is proved against a real browser store. **(4) The words typed into the paste box are document data too — that was the bug.** The reducer forgot cleanly and the letter stayed in the textarea on `/`, so the app would have announced “cleared” while showing the document. `PasteView` is now a two-line keyed wrapper that remounts on the new session id. An effect that set the state was tried first and rejected twice over: `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors on it, and the rule’s argument is right here — the old words would render once more before they vanished. **(5) No navigation, and the focus rule that came with it.** AGENTS.md §8 says focus moves to the new view’s heading after navigation; a view that changes *underneath* a user who is not navigating still owes the same thing, so the emptied screen’s heading takes focus and an e2e test asserts `toBeFocused`. The first attempt folded that into the existing heading effect by adding `session.status` to its dependencies, and **the A-06 test caught it red**: a refused question also changes `status`, so every status change pulled focus off the error summary A-06 puts it on. It is now a second effect keyed on `forgotten` alone, with the reason in the file, and the press still does not `router.push` — one press, one change. **(6) An aborted run now writes nothing afterwards, in both hooks.** `client.ts` has carried an `aborted` code since F-03, so cancelling a live run would have surfaced “Sightline stopped because you left or the request was cancelled” *under* the Forget announcement — an error box on a session that had just been cleared. `useDocumentRun` and `useAskAboutDocument` each check `controller.signal.aborted` on the success path and on the halt path and return silently. The check is the signal rather than the error type because Mode F’s replay answers regardless of cancellation, so a completed run can also arrive after Forget. What no browser has seen is recorded as **P-12** rather than claimed. **(7) The sentence is computed, and it says nothing about the bytes.** `useForget` builds it from the report — a clause for what was stopped, a clause for what could not be removed — and the e2e forbids `enclave|encrypt|private|attest|verif|stored|sent|delete|remov|destroy` in it on all four routes. What may be said about a document’s journey stays in `claims.ts`, computed from a `TrustRecord`, and a Forget leaves no record behind to compute one from; the platform limits (screen-reader speech history, clipboard, OS keyboard predictions, screenshots, browser history and autofill, back/forward cache, anything already sent) are written in `README.md`, which is the one place they can be stated honestly without a check to derive them from. · files: `src/lib/session/forget.ts`, `src/lib/session/store.tsx`, `src/app/_flow/useForget.ts` (new), `src/app/_flow/{useDocumentRun,useAskAboutDocument}.ts`, `src/components/ForgetButton.tsx` (new), `src/app/layout.tsx`, `src/features/paste/PasteView.tsx`, `src/features/analysis/ResultsView.tsx`, `src/features/trust/TrustReceipt.tsx`, `tests/unit/forget.test.ts` (new), `tests/e2e/flow.spec.ts`, `README.md`, `docs/{ARCHITECTURE,ARCHITECTURE_ESSENTIALS,PROJECT_STRUCTURE}.md`, `ROADMAP.md` · evidence: `npx vitest run` **358 passed | 6 todo** in 17 files (was 347 | 6 in 16: **+11** in `tests/unit/forget.test.ts`), `npx playwright test` **47 passed** = 12 shell + 35 flow from `--list` (was 39: **+8** P-10 tests, incl. one that reads a real `sessionStorage` after the press and asserts the app’s own key is gone while `other-tab.e2eKey` survives), `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors, `npx next build` exit 0 with the same six routes. The 44 px sweep was widened for this: the old check covered `main button` only, and a control in the navigation is not under `main`, so the new control is measured by name. · follow-ups: **(a) P-12** — the cancel-a-live-request path has never been exercised, because Mode F answers in the same tick and live modes refuse at the door (F-04/F-05, **B-05**); **(b)** C-02 must register its previews with `trackObjectUrl` or the revoke half stays a seam with no producer; **(c)** A-15/T-13 must store under `sightline.` or their values survive the button they inherit; **(d)** the `aborted` `AppError` copy is now unreachable from a Forget press — if a future control cancels for a *different* reason (leaving the page, timeout) it must not reuse this silent path, because here the user asked and there is nothing to report; **(e)** after Forget the results view says “Nothing has been read yet”, and P-08’s transcript-only screen keeps its own wording — whether the two empty states need to be distinguishable is a question for U-01, not a guess to make now; **(f) A-03/A-04** remain the real test of this control and remain blocked on **B-01**: nobody who uses a screen reader has pressed it yet.

- **2026-11-03 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · P-07 (done), P-11 (two wording follow-ups), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §6/§12/§13, `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`** · **Sightline now has a voice of its own about medicine, money and dates, and it is deliberately a small one.** Five decisions worth reading twice. **(1) The notices are computed from the reading's validated fields, never from a keyword hunt through the transcript.** `docType`, a fact's `kind`, an action's `deadline` — that is the whole input. The alternative (searching the text for "£" or "tablet") would have installed a second, unvalidated reader inside the app, one whose misses are invisible, and then made a safety statement depend on a pattern match rather than on what was read. The cost of that choice is stated in a test rather than left implicit: a health letter whose facts were filed `other` fires no medicine notice, and the demo says nothing more confident than "This **seems** to mention a medicine or a dose". **(2) Our sentences and the model's are two lists, in one section.** `analysis.warnings` stays on screen under "What the reading flagged"; `features/safety/notices.ts` renders above it. The split exists because the two can disagree, and because "the app says confirm with the surgery" and "the model said confirm with the surgery" are different claims about who told the person what — the same distinction rule 1 draws for privacy text. Adding a *section* would have moved the order AGENTS.md §8 fixes and the array `tests/e2e/flow.spec.ts` asserts, so this is content inside Warnings, and a test proves the heading array is unchanged. **(3) No detection claim, in either direction.** PRD §12 says read faithfully and **do not** claim detection, so the words _scam_, _fraud_ and _detect_ appear nowhere in the copy — instead the money notice states the limit: "Sightline reads every letter the same way, so it cannot tell you whether this one is genuine", and then does the one thing that actually protects a person: "check the amount with the sender using contact details you already have". **(4) The English-only line fires unconditionally, and that is the honest asymmetry.** It is a statement about Sightline's limits, not about the document, so it does not need the model to have noticed anything. Every other notice does. **(5) "Reviewed for plain language" was made checkable, and its limit written down.** `tests/unit/safety-notices.test.ts` holds the copy to ≤ 3 sentences, ≤ 20 words per sentence, one full stop at the end, no jargon from AGENTS.md §8's list, no dose/amount/date of its own, no privacy sentence, no repetition of the model's words — 19 tests. One wording change came out of saying the sentences aloud to myself: "A wrong date can change what you do" became "A date can be misread, so check it with the sender before you rely on it" — the first blamed the person's action, the second names the actual risk, and it is the only claim this product is allowed to make about its own accuracy (ARCH §18.2). · files: `src/features/safety/notices.ts` (new), `src/features/analysis/ResultsView.tsx`, `tests/unit/safety-notices.test.ts` (new), `tests/e2e/flow.spec.ts`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`, `ROADMAP.md` · evidence: `npx vitest run` **347 passed | 6 todo** in 16 files (was 328; **+19** `safety-notices.test.ts`, incl. the three corpus documents run through the pipeline's own `analysisSchema.parse` → `groundFacts` conversion so the demo fires exactly its justified set), `npx playwright test` **39 passed** (12 shell + 27 flow, counted from `--list`; was 33 — **+6** P-07 tests, axe over a completed run with the notices on screen), `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors. That is not tidiness: the byte-stability of the system message is what the "the document's own instructions never reached the prompt" test asserts, and a retry that rewrote it would have quietly broken ADR-19's story about what was asked. A retry that still cannot be parsed is a named failure, not a loop — two posts and stop, proved by a test that counts them. **(2) A truncation is never repaired.** `finish_reason: 'length'` means the model ran out of room, not that it wrote something unreadable; asking again would spend a second document-derived call to hit the same limit, and the honest act is to show the text the person already gave and say the summary did not arrive. **(3) A refusal halts; it does not degrade.** The model's refusal prose is never put on screen — the test asserts the halted `userMessage` is *identical* to `appError('model_refused').userMessage`, because paraphrasing a refusal is a way of repeating the model's words. And a question has no transcript-only screen to fall back to, so all three failure kinds stop a Q&A call while only two of them degrade a reading. **(4) `Session.degraded` is a new field, deliberately not a reuse of `Session.error`.** A run that finished and gave the person the text they pasted is not a failed run; reusing `error` would have driven the results view to the "Something stopped" summary and hidden the transcript. `run_degraded` sets `status: 'ready'`, drops any earlier `Analysis`, and a later successful read clears it — three reducer tests, including that a degraded session keeps the transcript and stays out of the error screen. - **2026-11-03 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · T-01 (in progress), T-02 (in progress), T-03 (its consumer contract is now tested), T-06 (note), F-04/F-05 (the door refusal now names them instead of T-01/T-02), F-11 (a defect in `--write`'s exit code, found by using the gate and fixed), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §10 "As built", `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`, `src/app/_flow/{refused_steps,useDocumentRun}.ts`** · **The two trust-path stubs became code, and one of our own guards turned out to be lying about it.** Nine decisions worth reading twice. **(1) `verified` is unreachable from an unmeasured shape, so nothing in this build may reach it that way.** `modelAttestationSchema` is deliberately loose — unknown keys stripped, every documented field optional — because every name inside `model_attestations[]` is `[Unverified by openapi]` and nothing is `[Measured]` (B-05). A loose schema plus **fail-closed checks** is the only honest combination: a strict schema would reject a real report we have not seen, and relaxing one later to make a test pass is exactly how a check stops meaning anything. So a report with no `request_nonce` is a **failed** binding, not a partial one — nothing then says it was made for this session rather than replayed from last week's. **(2) A verifier that says nothing is a failure, not an agreement.** `verifyWithServer` turns a 501, an unreachable `/api/verify` and a 200-with-unparseable-body into `server_verification ran:true passed:false` and `status:'failed'`; the alternative — treating silence as "no objection" — converts the security check into a comment. The consequence is recorded rather than hidden: T-03's route still answers 501, so **as of today this gate can only ever return `failed`**, and that is correct behaviour, not a defect to smooth over until a key arrives. **(3) Key material goes in a structured field, never in prose.** `GateOutcome.signingKey` is a field, not a check `detail`, because `detail` is rendered on the receipt and read aloud by a screen reader (ARCH §8: no secrets in logs; same reasoning, different sink). **(4) The `signing_address` a signature response carries about itself is data with no authority.** Verification is always against the key the gate bound this session; a test swaps a valid signature's claimed address to prove that trusting the served field would let a response name its own issuer. **(5) Whose signature it is is a separate named check.** `response_signature` (valid **and** the served `text` agrees with our own hashes — either half failing alone is a fail) and `signed_by_model_enclave` (`provider_tee` only), so a gateway TEE's signature can never be flattened into "the enclave serving the model signed this answer". **(6) The gate and the verifier are built by one function, `createTrustSession`,** because they share the two facts that decide everything — one session nonce (rule 4) and the key the report bound to it. Built separately, a caller can wire a verifier to a gate that never ran and verify against nothing; the composed version can only report `no attestation has run`. **(7) The ECDSA test vector had to be signed by hand, and that caught a real bug in the test.** `@noble/curves` 2.4 hashes its input inside `sign()` (probed twice: `recover(digest-as-is)` → nothing, `recover(sha256(digest))` → recovery 1), so it cannot produce a signature over an already-computed EIP-191 keccak digest — which is precisely what the service's ECDSA signatures are. `signEcdsaOverDigest` does the scalar arithmetic explicitly: same curve parameters, different code path from the recovery the verifier uses. The first draft's hardcoded group order was **mangled** (`…af48a03b52256729b2a724c1` instead of `…af48a03bbfd25e8cd0364141`) and would have silently signed nothing verifiable; a hand-written signer is only trustworthy because the assertion proves it, which is the reason the vector is written this way and the reason it is worth the 40 lines. **(8) Our own door refusal had become false, and the fix is now a gate.** `refused_steps.ts` still told live modes that T-01 and T-02 were unbuilt — true on Monday, false by Wednesday. A stale refusal is worse than none: it points the next reader at finished work and hides the work that would delete it (AGENTS.md §9: docs describe what the code does). It now names **F-04** (no allowlist, so nothing to attest) and **F-05** (the relay forwards nothing), and `tests/verification/attestation.test.ts` reads ROADMAP.md to fail if a refusal ever names an item the ledger marks `[x]` — checked in both directions by temporarily flipping F-04 to `[x]`, watching the test go red, and restoring the file. The user-facing sentences were also reworded: they must stay capability statements, so "a private model" left and a test now rejects `enclave|encrypt|private` in them (rule 1). **(9) Using the roadmap gate found a bug in it.** `--write` recomputed §1 and then exited **1** quoting "§1 dashboard does not match" — a complaint about bytes it had just replaced, so `roadmap:check -- --write && git add ROADMAP.md` could never chain and a successful repair looked like a failure. Ledger problems are now re-read after the write while repository problems are not, because writing a table cannot change what a commit did. Both directions were run: a wrong phase row → `--write` exits 0; an `[x]` item with its `Evidence:` line stripped → `--write` still exits 1 and names line 134. - **2026-11-03 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · P-06 (done), F-11 (done), F-12 (in progress), F-09 (one follow-up closed), T-01/T-02 (contracts written, still unbuilt), new B-09, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.3/§6/§7/§13, `docs/PROJECT_STRUCTURE.md`, `AGENTS.md` §2/§3, `README.md`** · **A question can now be asked in a browser with no key, and the ROADMAP rule stopped being prose.** Two changes that belong together because both are about enforcement: the feature that was in progress when the last session ended, and the gate that makes this file's own rule machine-checked. Six decisions worth reading twice. **(1) Fixture Q&A matches on content, exactly as F-09's document replay does.** A question is answered only when the wording is one written for *that* document; anything else raises the new `no_fixture_for_question` rather than returning the nearest demo sentence. The first draft of `findPreparedReply` matched on the request alone, which is the failure the whole corpus was built to avoid: a person asking their own rent agreement when the clinic appointment is would have been told a date from a fake GP letter. Proved in the browser by accident — a keyboard test typed `When is the payment deadline?`, a wording nobody prepared, and the app correctly refused; the test now asks a prepared one and a separate one asserts the refusal. **(2) Mode F performs no verification call for a question either.** `askAboutDocument` orders it transcript → gate → question → signature → receipt, and `createFixtureClient()` refuses every attestation and signature GET; three unit tests run the real pipeline with both collaborators set to *throw if called*, so "no enclave was consulted" is a test, not a comment. `buildTrustRecord` hard-codes `e2ee: false` and forces `status: 'not_run'` when the endpoint is `fixtures`, so a Q&A record cannot inherit a pass from anywhere else, and `claim` is deliberately **not** a parameter it accepts (rule 1 as a compile error). **(3) One `TrustRecord` per call, and the receipt became a list** — closing follow-up (c) of the F-09 entry. A new `purpose: 'analyse' | 'qa'` labels each block ("Reading the document" / "Answering a question"). `CallPurpose` in `client.ts` is local metadata only and never enters the request body, so ADR-19's byte identity between what was sent and what is signed still holds. **(4) `verification.ts` writes the T-01/T-02 contracts down before they exist** (`RunHalt`, `GateOutcome`, `SignatureOutcome`, `fixtureGate`, `haltFrom`), because P-03 and P-06 were already being tested against injected fakes; the real implementations now have a shape to fill rather than a rumour, and live modes refuse at the door naming the item that owes them. **(5) The gate found two bugs in itself, and one in the session reducer.** `checkLedger` read §2 "Current focus" up to a *named* next heading, so a document whose sections were renumbered would have it run to end-of-file and §2 would appear to mention every ID in §8 — found by a synthetic ledger, fixed by ending a section at the next `## ` line, which is the rule the fixture was already assuming. The reducer's `question_answered` kept the previous `error`, so a refused question stayed on screen above the next answer; it now clears the error and derives `status` from whether an analysis exists. **(6) The credential used to push this is a blocker, not a footnote (B-09).** It was passed on the command line only — never in `.git/config`, a remote URL, a file or a commit — but a token that has crossed into a chat log is a token to rotate, and the repo's configured identity is not the one this work is authored as, so commits carry `-c user.*` per invocation instead of editing git config Six decisions worth reading twice. **(1) Fixtures match on content, not on call order.** The scaffold's `FixtureSet{responses:string[]}` is replaced by `FixtureCase{name,title,text,analysis}` in `src/lib/fixtures/cases.ts`, and a request is answered only when the document inside it is one of the three, character for character; anything else returns the new `no_fixture_for_input`. An ordered queue would have handed a lovingly authored summary of a fake care bill to a person who pasted their own rent agreement — the single most damaging false claim this product could make, and it would have looked like a passing demo. **(2) Mode F answers no verification call.** `createFixtureClient()` refuses every attestation and signature GET ("there is no enclave in fixture mode, so there is nothing to verify") and `createNearClient` no longer routes Mode F through a throwing stub; the pipeline records `status: 'not_run'` with reasons, so the receipt reads "demo data" rather than implying checks were attempted and failed. Three unit tests run the **real** pipeline against the **real** replay client with the gate and the verifier set to throw if called. **(3) Live modes refuse at the door.** `useDocumentRun` reports `model_unavailable` naming T-01/T-02 and the missing allowlist instead of constructing placeholder collaborators, so no screen can show a "verified" state that nothing produced. That refusal is the same fail-closed shape as a failed check (rule 6), and it is what kept this honest: the first draft of the composition root passed `model: ''` to live modes and hand-wired fake gate/verifier objects. Self-reviewed out. **(4) `SessionProvider` is mounted in `layout.tsx` under `AnnouncerProvider`**, and it holds an `AbortController` registry — not for P-10's convenience but because a run the app cannot stop is a run whose bytes are still leaving the device after the user pressed the button. A test navigates away and back to prove the document survives client-side navigation, which is the corollary of ADR-8 (memory only): a full page load would silently lose it. **(5) The corpus labels itself inside the text.** Every demo document contains the sentence "synthetic demo data written for Sightline", enforced by a test, and one of the three carries a deliberately authored **misquote** so the flagged-fact state is *shown* in the demo rather than described in the README. **(6) Two findings from making this run, recorded instead of smoothed over.** (a) Playwright's `127.0.0.1` baseURL meant the dev server never hydrated — every interactive assertion failed and a "submit" fell through to a native `GET /?document-text=`. Proved by measuring both hosts (`#document-text` had no React props under one and did under the other); fixed in `playwright.config.mts` by pointing at `localhost` rather than widening `allowedDevOrigins`, because relaxing a dev-time security default to suit a test is the wrong trade. - **2026-11-02 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · F-03 (done), F-05 (follow-up), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §6/§8** · **Built the NEAR AI Cloud client** — the first real network module, and the last piece of plumbing P-03 needs before the flow can exist. Four decisions worth reading twice, because each one is a place a plausible-looking implementation would have been wrong: **(1) the client returns the exact wire bytes** (`sentBody` + un-trimmed `rawText`) alongside the parsed JSON, because ADR-19's `request_hash`/`response_hash` are hashes of what was *sent and received* — re-serialising, trimming, or calling `.json()` would produce a signature check that verifies the wrong bytes and still passes; there is therefore no second read of the body anywhere in the file, and the streaming "two trailing newlines" note from SPIKE §5 has a test that keeps them. **(2) Retry is exactly one, and status-shaped rather than error-shaped**: transport failure and 408/429/5xx retry; a GET 404 retries because the signature cache is per-node (the documented transient); a **POST** 404 does not, because that model is genuinely absent; and a 200 whose body will not parse is **never** retried — we were already charged for that completion and a second returns different bytes, so the signature fetched afterwards would match nothing. A retry that quietly turns a verification failure into a success is how rule 6 dies. **(3) Mode F refuses to build**: `createNearClient({mode:'fixtures'})` throws `NotBuilt('F-09')` instead of falling through to `fetch`, because a run under a "Demo data. No real privacy guarantee." banner that silently spends real credits and shows real answers is both a false claim and a bill. **(4) URL steering is closed at construction** — relay base must be same-origin under `/api/relay/v1` (absolute, `..`, and wrong-prefix forms each throw), direct mode is pinned to the one host CSP allows and must be handed a key provider (ADR-17). Model names contain a slash, so they are percent-encoded into **one** segment (`model/Qwen%2FQwen3-…`); that is F-05's allowlist problem now, and it is recorded as a follow-up rather than guessed at, because how Next hands `%2F` to a catch-all has not been observed (B-05). · files: `src/lib/near-ai/client.ts`, `src/app/api/relay/[...path]/route.ts` (comment only), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §6 + §8, `ROADMAP.md` · evidence: `tests/unit/near-client.test.ts` **31 tests** with a mocked fetch, incl. byte-identity of `sentBody` against what the double received, `rawText` newline preservation, "two transport failures stop instead of looping" (asserts exactly 2 calls), "a 200 with an unparseable body is never retried" (asserts exactly 1 call), pre-aborted-signal-sends-nothing (0 calls), relay-sends-no-authorization / direct-asks-the-provider-per-call, and two sentinel tests proving document text and service text appear in neither the error message, the `AppError`, nor a serialisation of it (rule 7). `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, `npx eslint .` 0 errors, `npx vitest run` **169 passed | 9 todo** in 10 files (was 138), `npx next build` unaffected (no route changed). · follow-ups: **nothing has crossed this client live** — the shapes are SPIKE reading, and S-08/S-11 under B-05 must confirm them; P-03 now drives it; F-05 inherits the encoded-segment constraint; the `%2F` question is a checkbox on S-08.

- **2026-11-02 · Qoder agent (for `abrcrmb`) · S-01, S-04, S-06, S-10, S-12, S-13, F-04, F-05, F-06, T-01, T-02, T-10, T-13, new T-15** · Ran the Day-1 spike by **primary-source reading** (no API key yet, so nothing here is measured) and wrote `docs/SPIKE_RESULTS.md` from it. **S-04 is done**; S-01/S-06/S-10/S-12/S-13 are narrowed, not closed. Findings that changed the design: **(1)** E2EE to the enclave exists and is documented as protocol v2 (X25519 ECDH → HKDF-SHA256 `info "ed25519_encryption"` → XChaCha20-Poly1305, key from `model_attestations[0].signing_public_key`, headers `X-Client-Pub-Key`/`X-Model-Pub-Key`/`X-Signing-Algo`/`X-Encryption-Version`), but only Python/Node paths are documented and XChaCha20-Poly1305 is not WebCrypto → the strong claim is **reachable**, and T-10 needs one new dep (`@noble/ciphers`); ADR-16 makes the relay a **ciphertext-only** carrier once a browser call works, which is the first time "our server sees the document" can leave the threat model. **(2)** `GET /v1/attestation/report` and `GET /v1/signature/{chat_id}` both require a key and there is **no keyless attestation path** → Mode A's key must live in the browser (ADR-17), and Mode A can never be the default for our users. **(3)** `attestationSupported` / `providerType` / `verifiable` are **not on `/v1/models`** — they are on `GET /v1/model/{model_name}` — so **F-04 as written could not tell a TEE model from a proxied one** (AGENTS §4.3); rewritten (ADR-18). **(4)** The completion body carries **no signature**: `GET /v1/signature/{chat_id}` signs `{model_id}:{request_hash}:{response_hash}`, streaming payloads end in two newlines that belong in the hash, lookups can transiently 404 → retry, and bytes are retained 60 minutes → T-02 rewritten (ADR-19). **(5)** CORS is open for every non-`/v1/admin` origin in `cloud-api` `crates/api/src/lib.rs:1075-1097`, so **T-13 stays in** instead of being cut by default (live preflight still pending). **(6)** `nearai/nearai-cloud-verifier` is **MIT with a native TypeScript `ts/`** using `dcap-qvl-node`, so the TDX quote check is a port **[Go, last]** rather than the project's biggest risk; ARCH §10 now records the decided minimum honest set with a go/no-go per check. **(7)** `report_data` is "not currently returned in model reports", so nonce binding for a model is `request_nonce` + the NRAS token's `eat_nonce`. **(8)** A per-key cap exists (`PATCH …/api-keys/{key_id}/spend-limit`, nano-USD), so F-06 keeps the shared counter as primary and the cap as backup. **(9)** `@nearai/inference-sdk` is deliberately **kept out of the trust path** — the product is the receipt naming the checks that ran, so the checks must be our code; the SDK becomes a cross-check script (new T-15, ADR-20). **(10)** The `$10 Builders credit` figure is HACKATHON.md Q-000 only, not in NEAR's docs — recorded as an event promise, not an API guarantee.
